# Changelog

Notable changes per release. Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/);
version numbers follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html) and, before
1.0, are decided by model compatibility as CONTRIBUTING.md § Versioning states. Cutting a
release is described in [docs/project/releasing.md](docs/project/releasing.md).

## Unreleased

## 0.11.0 — 2026-10-23

### Added

- `pilot-apijson-diff`, an advisory derived-property differential: every corpus model is exported as the SysML v2 API's element JSON by `sysml -convert api-json` and by the pinned pilot's `JsonElementProcessingFacade` (derived properties included, provisioned by `scripts/download-pilot-json-exporter.sh`), the elements are matched by a stable key and every property is bucketed as present-equal, present-different, ours-missing or pilot-missing per (metaclass, property), so the metamodel's `derive*` constraints read as a measured scorecard (`docs/project/api-json-differential.md`, baseline `docs/project/api-json-differential-baseline.json`, reproduced by the oracle-reproduction workflow).

- API JSON writes evaluator-derived `qualifiedName` values and preserves mapping names that cannot be reconstructed from owned graph facts in `sysx:mappingQualifiedName` in standard and compact output.

- The api-json differential writes its match inventory (`pilot-apijson-diff-matches.json`) and, with `-lost-against <earlier run's inventory>`, lists per root and metaclass every element match lost and gained since that run; `docs/project/api-json-differential.md` records why naming members by their naming feature cost `docs/manual/examples` 171 matches (the pilot resolves no feature of this repository's own libraries) and that no derivation disagrees.

- API JSON export writes `null` only for absent single-valued owned properties with a zero lower bound and writes empty arrays for absent multi-valued owned properties.

- `sysml -migrate sysml` keeps the compartments a modeler collapsed on a Cameo symbol: the symbol's `SUPPRESS_*` compartment properties, its own and the ones inherited from the diagram's symbol styles, are written as `showAttributes`, `showParts`, `showReferences`, `showPorts`, `showConstraints`, `showItems` and `showActions = false` on the symbol's `DiagramLayout::Layout`, and the migration report counts them (`# compartments suppressed: N on M symbols`). The positioned-tree renderer honours the switches per symbol: a hidden compartment is neither listed in the box nor counted among the rows clipped by its stated size, while a rendering's `showCompartments = false` still hides every compartment.

- `CheckBehavior` RPC and `check_behavior` MCP tool: search every schedule of an action or state machine for violated properties, deadlocks, failures and divergent values, as `sysml -check-property` does. The request carries the CLI's `-check-property`, `-check-diverge`, `-check-input`, `-check-assume`, `-check-witness`, `-check-depth`, `-check-states`, `-check-unroll` and `-check-timeout` as typed fields; the answer carries the verdict, the report lines, the search statistics, each violation, divergence and final outcome with the schedule that replays it, the witness files when asked for, and the engine's standing. The service advertises the `check` capability; the Go, Python, Node, Java, Rust, Julia and MATLAB clients expose it as `CheckAction`/`check_action` and `CheckStateMachine`/`check_state_machine`.

- The Go client's `RenderView` takes `WithShow(elements...)` and `WithDepth(n)` beside `WithFullPorts()`, and its `RenderedView.Shown` lists the optional notation elements drawn, as the other clients already read them.
- The Rust client's `AnalysisResult.tool_dry_run` is the typed `ToolDryRun` preview a run under `AnalysisOptions.tool_dry_run` answers with, instead of leaving it on the wire message.
- The Python client's `Model.refresh_diagnostics(disable_lint=(), enable_lint=())` and `Connection.diagnostics(model_hash, ...)` ask `GetDiagnostics` under a lint selection, as `sysml -disable-lint`/`-enable-lint` select them.
- The Julia client's `execute_action`/`execute_state` take `advance`, `seed`, `draws` and `clock_step`, `run_analysis` takes `tool_dry_run` and answers a typed `ToolDryRun`, `render_view` takes `depth`, `list_engines(conn; probe=true)` probes each external engine, and `refresh_diagnostics(model; disable_lint, enable_lint)` asks `GetDiagnostics` under a lint selection.
- The MATLAB client's `executeAction`/`executeState` take `'advance'`, `'seed'`, `'draws'` and `'clockStep'`, `runAnalysis` takes `'toolDryRun'` and decodes the preview into `AnalysisResult.toolDryRun`, `renderView` takes `'depth'`, `listEngines(conn, 'probe', true)` probes each external engine, and `refreshDiagnostics(model, 'disableLint', ..., 'enableLint', ...)` asks `GetDiagnostics` under a lint selection.

- Automatically laid-out tree diagrams fold a node's owned members into compartment rows of its box on request: `%viz --style=COMPMOST` folds every owned usage no `Layout`, nested definition or package keeps a node, `--style=COMPTREE` the port usages with the ports nested under them, so the pilot's two spellings now draw instead of being noticed as not represented. The folding is `-render-compartments positioned|most|ports` at the CLI (on `-render`, `-render-all` and the document diagrams), `compartments=<folding>` on `%render`, `compartments` on the LSP `opensysml/render` request (listed by the `openSysmlRenderCompartments` capability) and on gRPC `RenderView`, whose rows carry the `compartment` of their parent they are drawn in; the Python, Go, Rust and Node clients take it. An edge at a folded member moves to its owner, the owner's containment of it is dropped as the nesting the row stands for, and the notice counts the members drawn as rows. The default output is unchanged.

- `%configure`, `%solve` and `%check` (and the MCP `configure` tool) accept a part or definition — a variation family or one of its configurations — and solve over the conjunction of every assertion about its objects: owned and inherited asserted constraints, carried requirements and satisfactions, the collection `%validate` checks, with the values its own features declare fixed; a carried requirement applies only where its assumptions hold, a satisfaction's requirement reads the part `by` names, and the report names nested parts whose own assertions are not in the query. A refused selection names the assertion refusing it; a part asserting nothing about its objects is an error.

- `%configure`, `%solve` and `%check` on a part or definition, and the MCP `configure` tool, now state what its nested parts' own objects assert — through the feature, its type or a variant of either — read through the holding feature (`engine.autoPort`), a variant's assertion applying only where the holding variation selects it (`engine == '6cylEngine' implies …`), as deep as `%validate` reaches, with nested declared values pinned; a variation point a variant alone declares is enumerated and listed under that variant only, and choosing it against a variant that lacks it is an error naming the selection it needs; a nested part the query cannot read as one object is refused by name with the reason instead of every such part being left out.

- The solver translates `P->forAll { in ref w; w == P::V }` and `P->exists { … }` over a variation point `P` that holds at least one object as the point's selection, `P == P::V` (`P != P::V` for `w != P::V`): a configuration holds only the variant it selects (SysML v2.1 §8.3.6, §7.6.7), so `%configure`, `%solve`, `%check`, `%explain` and the MCP `configure` tool now put such a constraint in the query instead of refusing it. A point that may hold no object, a body comparing the iterate with anything but a variant of the point, and every other invocation stay refused, each with its reason.

- **Connection and flow definition ends must be usages.** SysML v2.1 Beta 2 types `ConnectionDefinition::connectionEnd` and `FlowDefinition::flowEnd` as `Usage`, redefining `associationEnd`, so every end feature of a connection, interface, allocation or flow definition — inherited ones included — is a usage. A definition that inherits a KerML `end feature` from a kernel association (`abstract connection def B :> Links::BinaryLink;`, `abstract flow def F :> Transfers::Transfer;`) is now reported once, at the definition (`connection-def-end-not-usage`, `flow-def-end-not-usage`: `A connection definition end must be a usage.`, `A flow definition end must be a usage.`); owned usage ends that redefine the inherited ones, and definitions specializing `Connections::Connection`, `BinaryConnection`, `Flows::Flow` or `Flows::Message`, stay clean. The rule is ahead of the pinned pilot (2026-08), which predates it.
- **`Flows::Message` and `Flows::messages` have usage ends again.** As published, the base flow definition owns no ends and inherits `Transfer`'s KerML ends, so the rule above would report the standard library itself. The declared errata overlay now carries the repair the pilot applies for OMG issue SYSML21-681 — `Message` owns `end occurrence source`/`target` redefining `Transfer::source`/`target`, `messages` is an action usage owning ends redefining `Message::source`/`target` and `transfers::source`/`target` — on read, with the published file untouched. An erratum's corrected text may now span several lines, keyed to the one published line it replaces.

- A machine-readable constraint manifest, `docs/project/constraint-manifest.json`, is now the single
  record behind the validation-constraint census: every pilot-named constraint with its metaclass,
  the clause of the pinned KerML 1.0 / SysML 2.0 specification, the implementing location, the
  diagnostic code its probes observe, its negative case, its census status and a rationale for
  every status but ✅, plus the constraint-tier passes beyond the pilot's names, the declared errata
  and the OMG issues the census bears on. The census table in `validation-constraints.md` and the
  census summary quoted in `spec-compliance.md` are generated from it by `make docs-counts`, whose
  `-check` gates refuse a page or a manifest row edited without regenerating; `go test -C tools
  ./census/validation` holds each probe to a code its row records and each constraint-tier pass to
  a row or an `extensions` entry. It supersedes `validation-constraints-baseline.json`; no status or
  count moved.

- Every release publishes the `openmbee/opensysml` container image to Docker Hub for `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64`, tagged with the version and, for the newest stable release, `latest`: `sysml-grpc` is the entrypoint, on port 50051 with `/health` on 8081, and `sysml` and `sysml-lsp` are in `/usr/local/bin` beside it, all three stamped with the release's version. `make docker` builds the same image locally as `openmbee/opensysml:dev`.

- `Convert` reports the semantic check's diagnostics alongside the converted output when
  `ConvertRequest.include_check_diagnostics` is set, each with its own severity and code as
  `ParseFile` and `GetDiagnostics` report them; the `convert_check_diagnostics` capability names it.
  Off, the default, only syntax errors are reported, as before.

- Guide section and conformance fixtures for a requirement a part definition `satisfy`s and a verification the definition owns, run on a usage's object: the inherited `satisfy` is checked on the usage, a verification body reads a requirement usage of another package through a qualified name (`RequirementPkg::costReq.maximumCost`), and the owned verification binds its subject to the owning object as `this`. `subject p = self;` in such a usage keeps its `bound-feature-types` warning: `self` there is the case itself, as the reference implementation also reports.

- Every diagnostic now carries a stable, kebab-case code, registered in `internal/check/codes` with what it means, how to resolve it and the specification clause or validation constraint behind it: the bucket codes `type` and `type.expr` are replaced by one code per rule (`relationship-target-kind`, `binding-connector-type-conformance`, `operator-operand-type`, `invocation-argument` …), the parser names each misplaced-member rule after its owning-type constraint (`subject-membership-owning-type` …), and name resolution codes its findings at the source. `sysml -explain <code>` prints a code's explanation and `sysml -explain` lists them all; the REPL offers `%explain-code <code>`, sysml-mcp an `explain_diagnostic` tool, and the language server publishes a `codeDescription` link beside each diagnostic's code. Text diagnostics read `file:line:col: error[code]: message`, so the code sits in a stable, parseable position; `-json` already carried it. The catalogue in docs/reference/diagnostics.md is generated from the registry and `make diagnostics-check` fails when it drifts, and a sweep test over every model in the tree and the standard library proves each emitted code is registered.

- A rendering with nothing to draw says why. `opensysml/render` (and the gRPC and engine renderings) carry a notice for every result with no node, edge or row — the view exposes nothing, every element it exposes is rejected by its filter (naming the filter as written), what it exposes does not resolve or is nothing, nothing it exposes is shown by a rendering of this kind, or the view shows pictures alone — and, when the document has an error, its first error in the CLI's words (`<file> did not analyse cleanly; nothing was rendered: <line>:<col>: <message>`). The text and Mermaid forms end with the same reason, so a view whose filter rejects everything now reads `every element the view exposes is rejected by its filter @RequirementUsage; the rendering is empty` rather than `the view exposes nothing`. The VS Code diagram panel puts that reason in the status line and where the drawing would be, never an empty canvas (a layout without extent included), a table with no row lists the reasons in place of `No elements to list.`, and the extension logs every render with nothing to draw to the SysML v2 Output channel — every render while `opensysml.trace.server` is on — as the view, kind, node, edge and row counts and the notices.

- `ImportData` and `CompareResults` RPCs with the `import_data` and `compare_results` MCP tools: the tabular and JSON data import that sets the feature values a CSV, TSV, JSON or JSON Lines file assigns, one row per element, answering every assignment made and the edited model as a new model the service holds (`sysml -import`, `-import-as`, `-import-map`, `-import-format`, `-import-dry-run`), and the comparison that runs the configurations a SysML v1 tool's results index names and tables the tool's distributions of their observables beside the runs' (`-compare-results`, with `-runs`, `-seed`, `-draws`, `-clock-step` and `-observe` as request fields). Both call the lowering and comparison the REPL calls, now in `internal/workspace/dataimport` and `internal/exec/simresults`. The service advertises the `import_data` and `compare_results` capabilities; the Go, Python, Node, Java, Rust, Julia and MATLAB clients expose them as `ImportData`/`import_data`/`importData` and `CompareResults`/`compare_results`/`compareResults`.

- The homepage hero has a second scene, the Saturn V mass roll-up from `examples/runtime-showcase/mass-rollup.sysml`: a visit opens on one of the two scenes at random, tabs switch between them over one connection to the engine, and `?scene=mass` or `#scene=stack` forces one. The scene draws the vehicle's stages and engines as nested boxes with the `totalMass` the engine rolls up on each, Run instantiates and evaluates the vehicle and animates the roll-up from the engines up, Edit changes a mass and the totals follow, and "Add the instrument unit" switches to `saturnVWithIU`, whose roll-up the engine reports undetermined with its reason. A scene now declares its controls, presenter, program and variants; the landing model draws one box per instantiated object, folding a bound collection into the objects it lists.

- `TestLibraryAndPrefixSweep` (`internal/exec/runtime`) reads every Systems and Domain library feature on an object, calls every Kernel Function Library function and instantiates every grammar prefix on every usage kind, and pins each probe's outcome in `docs/project/library-and-prefix-sweep-outcomes.txt`, so a feature the runtime stops (or starts) evaluating is a reviewed diff; `-update-library-prefix-sweep` re-records it.

- `llm-bench` (tools/cmd/llm-bench) benchmarks how well a language model writes SysML v2 with the compiler in the loop: 50 natural-language tasks with gold models that validate under `sysml -validate` and the pinned pilot, a generate–validate–repair loop that feeds `sysml -validate -json` diagnostics back for up to three rounds, a compiler-only arm and an arm offering the `sysml-mcp` tools in-process, OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic and replay providers, and metrics for compile rate, repair rounds, structural fidelity over the API JSON export, unresolved-name hallucinations and pilot agreement; the method, corpus and results are recorded in docs/project/llm-benchmark.md.

- **The constraint manifest records every constraint the formal documents state.** `docs/project/constraint-manifest.json` now carries, beside the pilot-named rows, a spec-sourced row (`"source": "spec"`) for each of the 480 `check*`, `derive*` and `validate*` constraints that the Constraints subsections of KerML 1.0 §8.3 and SysML 2.0 §8.3 state and no pilot validator names, with the document's clause, prose and OCL and the status ❔ unknown. `go run -C tools ./cmd/formal-constraints` reads them from the pinned PDFs (SHA-256 verified, `pdftotext -layout`) deterministically: `-list` prints the extraction, `-update` records it keeping every hand-edited field, `-check` is the gate. The census table and counts keep counting pilot-named rows only; a generated formal-document coverage block on `docs/project/validation-constraints.md` states, per document and kind, how many constraints the documents define, how many the pilot names and how many remain unknown, and that page says how a spec-sourced row is promoted. Two pilot rows now cite the document's own name for the constraint (`validateDefinitionVariationOwnedFeatureMembership`, `validateUsageVariationOwnedFeatureMembership`) and four cite the clause in which the document states them.

- The conformance runner (`tools/cmd/conformance`) runs the suite through `sysml-mcp` as protocol `mcp`: it builds (or takes `-mcp-binary`) the server, starts it with `-tools runtime -root <fixtures>` over stdio and calls each scenario's RPC as its runtime tool, the request as the tool's arguments and the structured result read back as the response, so the tools are judged by the scenarios the service is. `make conformance-mcp` runs it with the report and JUnit outputs, and CI runs it beside `make conformance`. `sysml-mcp` honours `OPENSYSML_TEST_WITHHOLD_CAPABILITIES` as `sysml-grpc` does, so `-withhold-capabilities` applies to it too.

- **`sysml-mcp -token-file FILE`**, `-token TOKEN` and `OPENSYSML_MCP_TOKEN` authenticate the Streamable HTTP transport as the Model Context Protocol's authorization specification asks of a server on a network — an OAuth 2.1 resource server in the minimal form, with one bearer token issued out of band: every request on `/mcp` must carry `Authorization: Bearer TOKEN` (RFC 6750, compared in constant time) or is refused with `401` and a `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer` challenge naming the protected resource metadata, which is served at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp` (RFC 9728). The token is never logged, and serving it in cleartext off loopback is warned about. `-public-url URL` names the endpoint as clients reach it behind a proxy that terminates TLS, for the challenge, the metadata and the configuration block. `-print-config HOST -http ADDR` with a token writes the host's URL block with the `Authorization` header (Codex `http_headers`).

- **`sysml-mcp -http ADDR`** serves the Model Context Protocol over the Streamable HTTP transport at `/mcp` of that address, as one long-lived server for remote agents or several agents over one workspace: every client shares the workspace, the runtime cache and the REPL sessions, so `session_*` names are shared. A port alone binds loopback; any other address is served with a warning that the server authenticates no one. `-tls-cert`/`-tls-key` serve `https`; a request's `Origin` header is answered when absent, loopback or listed in `-allow-origin` (only a listed origin is granted the CORS headers a browser needs) and refused with 403 otherwise; SIGINT and SIGTERM close the sessions and drain the connections. `-print-config HOST -http ADDR` writes every host's block in its URL form (OpenCode `"type": "remote"`, Claude Code/VS Code/Devin `"type": "http"`, Cursor `url`, Codex `url`, Gemini `httpUrl`).

- `npx -y @openmbee/sysml-mcp` runs the MCP server with nothing preinstalled: the
  dependency-free npm package `@openmbee/sysml-mcp` (`packaging/npm/sysml-mcp`) picks the
  platform package `@openmbee/sysml-mcp-<os>-<cpu>` for the machine, at its own version, and
  runs the release `sysml-mcp` binary it carries with the caller's arguments and stdio,
  forwarding signals and exiting with the binary's status. The five platform packages (with
  LICENSE and the man page) are built by `client/node/scripts/build-platform-packages.mjs`
  from the release binaries, verified against their `.sha256` sidecars, and published by the
  release's npm job and the nightly beside `@openmbee/opensysml`, platform packages first.
  `sysml-mcp -print-config <host> -print-config-npx` writes the host's block in that form.

- **`sysml-mcp -print-config <host>`** writes the configuration block that declares the server to an agent host — `opencode`, `claude-code`, `cursor`, `codex`, `vscode`, `gemini` or `devin` — naming the binary by its absolute path, or by the command `-print-config-command` gives; alone, the flag lists the hosts. The checkout's `opencode.json` declares the server beside the language server, and `sysml-mcp` ships as its own per-platform archive (`sysml-mcp-<os>-<arch>.tar.gz`, `.zip` on Windows) that `install.sh`/`install.ps1` (`OPENSYSML_TOOLS`), Homebrew, Scoop and the Windows MSI install; the winget manifest carries the `opensysml` bundle alone.

- Every release tag lists `sysml-mcp` in the official MCP Registry as `org.opensysml/sysml-mcp`: `packaging/mcp/server.json` describes the npm package `@openmbee/sysml-mcp` (stdio transport, the `-root` argument with its default), the `MCP Registry` workflow waits for the npm version, installs the pinned `mcp-publisher`, logs in through the `opensysml.org` DNS record and publishes, `scripts/mcp-registry-txt-record.sh` prints that record from the maintainer's key, and `make mcp-registry-check` validates the listing without credentials.

- **`sysml-mcp` runtime tools.** The `runtime` group serves the one-shot service as tools, one per RPC with the RPC's contract: `parse`, `symbol`, `evaluate`, `instantiate`, `execute_action`, `execute_state`, `evaluate_calc`, `verify_constraint`, `verify_requirement`, `verify_satisfaction`, `validate_instance`, `run_analysis`, `run_sweep`, `query`, `run_document_query`, `render_model_document`, `render_model_view`, `export_graphs`, `convert`, `migrate`, `apply_edits`, `list_engines` and `server_info`. Arguments are the request message under its proto JSON names; the structured result is the response as protojson writes it, beside a readable text. Every tool taking a `modelHash` also takes `paths` and inline `sources` and parses them first, so one call suffices; a refused call keeps the status code's name (`NOT_FOUND`, `UNIMPLEMENTED`) and message, and the long-running tools report progress to a client that asks for it.

- **`sysml-mcp`, a Model Context Protocol server for coding agents.** OpenCode, Claude Code, Cursor, Codex and every other MCP host start it in the project directory and get OpenSysML as tools: the language server's workspace over the documents under the root, the runtime's one-shot service, and persistent REPL sessions. `-tools lsp,runtime,session` chooses the groups served.

- **`sysml-mcp` session tools.** The session group drives a persistent REPL session as typed tools with structured results: `session_load`, `session_submit`, `session_eval`, `session_print`, `session_save` (writes only with `apply`; both read and write only under the workspace root), `session_clear`; `session_run` for an action, state machine, calc or analysis case; the action and state machine debugger (`debug_start`, `debug_step`, `debug_continue`, `debug_current`, `debug_breakpoints`, `debug_stop`, `send`, `advance`); `features` and `instances` over the instance graph; the solver (`check`, `explain`, `solve`, `configure`, `optimize`); `sweep`, `runs` and `render_run`; and `repl_command` for every other `%` command. Calls on one session are served one at a time, and a call the client abandons interrupts the run.

- **`sysml-mcp` tool catalog.** One catalog (`internal/frontend/mcp/catalog`) now names every tool the server serves, the group that serves it, the `sysml-grpc` RPC and `sysml` flag it stands for and a one-line summary. The new `tool_catalog` tool answers it to a client, narrowed to the groups served, and the Tools section of `sysml-mcp(1)` is generated from it, so neither can drift from the tools registered. Drift gates pin the catalog to the code: every RPC of `api/proto/sysml.proto` must have a tool or be listed, with a reason, among the RPCs that must not be tools; the tools a group registers must be exactly the catalog's; and every `sysml` flag must be classified once, as the tool that performs it, as an operation the service does not serve yet (with what it lacks), or as CLI-only (with why). The runtime group gains `diagnostics`, the `GetDiagnostics` RPC, which reports every diagnostic of a parsed model as `sysml -validate` does, strict mode included through `strictConformance`.

- `sysml -metaclass-graph` writes the metaclass hierarchy of the pinned pilot metamodel as an RDF vocabulary graph in Turtle, without reading a model: one `rdfs:Class` per metaclass in the `sysml:` namespace, `rdfs:subClassOf` per declared general metaclass and `sysx:abstract true` on the abstract ones. The graph is committed as `internal/translate/rdf/ontology/sysml-metaclasses.ttl` with a drift gate (`make metaclass-graph-check`), so a store that holds one `rdf:type` per element can load it and find every specialization of a metaclass through `rdf:type/rdfs:subClassOf*`.

- Prefix metadata written before `individual` (`#Tag individual part def Article :> Body;`) is reported as `nonstandard-notation` — a warning by default, an error under `-strict` — naming the standard order (`individual #Tag part def …`); the declaration stays parsed with its metadata.

- Add lazy evaluation of derived SysML metamodel properties using semantic ownership, typing, and relationship data.

- A view's rendering now draws only the notation elements the SysML v2 notation requires plus the port symbols, compartments and keywords, and names the optional ones to show, each on its own: `parameter-symbols` (a case's actor, subject and stakeholder parameters and an action's parameter pins on the node's boundary), `port-symbols`, `compartments`, `edge-labels`, `keywords`, `multiplicities`, `typings` and `qualifiers`, or `all`. A rendering specializing a standard `Views` rendering chooses them in the model with Boolean `showParameterSymbols`, `showEdgeLabels`, … attributes, the nearest declaration deciding each; `sysml -render-show <elements>` on `-render`, `-render-all`, `-render-document` and `-render-documents`, `show` on the LSP `opensysml/render` request (the elements listed by the `openSysmlRenderElements` capability), `show=<elements>` on the REPL's `%render` and `%render-document`, `show` on `opensysml/renderDocument`, on the sysml-engine `RenderView` request and on the gRPC `RenderView` RPC (their results' `shown` listing what was drawn; `model.render_view(view, show=[...])` in Python, `show` on the Node, Java, Julia and MATLAB `renderView`/`render_view`, `Model::render_view_showing` in Rust and `WithShow` in Go), the VS Code `opensysml.diagram.show` setting and a document diagram block's `show` attribute (`attribute redefines show = "edge-labels, typings";`) add to what the rendering shows. A connection that ended at a parameter symbol or port left out ends at the node that declares it, and a label left out leaves no empty box.

- A default run (`reverse`, `declared`) now reports its values only up to the partial order the Kernel Semantic Library fixes. Each choice point it records is classified by the lowered footprints the checker's reduction already uses: alternatives that write what another reads or writes, or meet on one channel, conflict; the rest commute and are marked `independent`. A value a conflicting alternative wrote prints with an order-dependence marker — `x = 1 (order-dependent; 2 choice points — explore shows the set)` — in the CLI `-action`/`-state` output, the REPL (`%action`, `%step`, `%continue`, `%advance`, feature display), the trace (`choice … ; order-dependent: x`) and the gRPC/Connect surfaces (`order_dependent` on `FeatureValue`, `ExecuteActionResponse` and `ExecuteStateResponse`; `order_dependent` and `independent` on a `choice-point` diagnostic, under the `order_dependence` capability), and in the Python (`ActionOutputs.order_dependent`, `Diagnostic.order_dependent`/`independent`) and Node (`orderDependent`, `ModelDiagnostic.orderDependent`/`independent`) clients. Values nothing order-dependent touched print as before. Conformance cases state the marker as `orderDependent`.

- **The release-gate performance record of 0.10.0 against 0.9.2.** `docs/project/archive/performance-release-0.10.0-vs-0.9.2.md` measures the `v0.10.0` tag against the `v0.9.2` tag: every benchmark on both revisions under `benchstat`, the rows in doubt re-run interleaved, the stress constellation, and whole-binary validation, start-up and size, with a column for the release merge before the state-machine and inherited-step work where that work moved a row. Nothing in it blocks the release. Loading is 15–18% more allocations per element on connector-dense models (`sysml -validate` +14–15%), executing a step +40–66%, lowering an action chain twice the bytes for the inherited steps it now carries (linear in the chain), checks with arguments 1 016 → 1 440 B each, workspace edits beside a large document 3.0 times the bytes, the import-ambiguity warning firing 134 times on the Annex A vehicle example, and the exact-numeric calcs, each with its cause named; the fleet's hydration is 19–33% fewer allocations and the interpreted `Fib(25)` 8% faster.

- **The release-gate performance record of 0.11.0 against 0.10.1.** `docs/project/performance-release-0.11.0-vs-0.10.1.md` measures the 0.11.0 candidate — the head of `develop` the release is cut from, with every fix the measurement motivated merged — against the `v0.10.1` tag: every benchmark on both revisions under `benchstat`, the rows in doubt re-run interleaved, the stress constellation, and whole-binary validation, start-up and size, with each confirmed regression attributed to the change responsible and its fix listed; the cut point of `release/0.11.0` is re-probed on every row the merges after the measured head could move.

- A guide chapter, [Modeling product lines and variability](docs/guide/modeling-product-lines.md), walks the OMG training vehicle family from its superset model (variations, variants, the assertion tying engine to transmission) through what the validator enforces, configurations checked with `%instantiate` and `%validate`, `%configure` over the family and its configurations, and the tree and interconnection renderings, every output from the current build; linked from the fleets chapter, the REPL chapter and the `%configure` reference row.

- **Project interchange archives (`.kpar`).** The zipped form of a project directory (KerML 1.0 §10.3) is read wherever a project directory is: named on the command line beside a model, under `-library-root` / `OPENSYSML_LIBRARY_ROOTS` / the language server's `libraryRoots` as an installed project a `.project.json` usage selects, and as `OPENSYSML_LIBRARY_PATH` in place of the bundled standard library (a `.kpar` or a directory of them, as the OMG publishes the library). Archives are read from memory, their `.project.json` and `.meta.json` checked (both at the top level, mandatory fields, usage resources, index and checksum members, checksums verified) before a member is read, and a malformed one is a diagnostic on the archive's path. `sysml -bundle <dir> [-o name.kpar]` writes a directory as an archive — sorted members and fixed timestamps, so two runs are byte-identical; a missing `.meta.json` is generated — and `sysml -unbundle <file.kpar> [-o dir]` writes one back into a new or empty directory.

- **Installed dependency projects are found.** A directory holding a `.project.json` is an OMG project interchange project (KerML 1.0 §10.3). When a loaded file belongs to one, the installed projects its `usage` entries name are looked for under the project's well-known library directories (`.sysand/lib` for a sysand project) and under any directory `sysml -library-root`, `sysml-lsp -library-root`, `OPENSYSML_LIBRARY_ROOTS` or the language server's `libraryRoots` setting names, matched by their own `.project.json` (publisher and name against the usage's resource IRI, the latest installed version unless the constraint names one exactly), and their usages followed in turn. `sysml -validate <project directory>` loads the project's files and those of the projects it uses; a file named alone has its imports looked for in its project and the projects that uses, and a file inside an installed project finds the projects installed beside it. The language server indexes the same projects for a workspace folder, so their names resolve in the editor. Unrelated hidden directories stay skipped, the standard library's `.kpar` usages are answered by the bundled library, and no lock file is read.

- `python -m opensysml.generate` (and `opensysml-generate`) accepts a model of several files the way `sysml` does — the `.sysml`/`.kerml` files in order, or a directory read in path order — and parses them as one model through `ParseSources`, so a part typed by a definition in another file is generated as that class instead of failing with an unresolved reference; `--package NAME` (repeatable) restricts the output to one package and its nested packages. One file still goes through `ParseFile` and generates the same module as before; a module of several files is stamped with the hash of the files' hashes in order, and `--check` names every source and `--package` in its regeneration command.

- **`RenderDocument` names its file.** `RenderDocumentResponse` gains `file_name`, the file a rendered set (`-render-documents`) writes the document to in the requested form and the name the other documents' links to it use; populated under the `render_document_file_name` capability; the Java client exposes it as `RenderedDocument.fileName()`, and the SysON plugin downloads a rendered document under that name instead of deriving one of its own.

- **The DOT and PlantUML forms route their edges as asked.** `-render-lines splines|ortho|polyline` on `-render` and `-render-all`, the same word on `%render`, `"lines"` on the LSP's `opensysml/render` (the routings advertised by the `openSysmlRenderLines` capability) and the Pilot visualizer's `ORTHOLINE` and `POLYLINE` styles on `%viz` write `splines=ortho|polyline` on the DOT graph and `skinparam linetype ortho|polyline` in PlantUML; `splines`, the default, leaves each engine its curved routing. The two Pilot styles were accepted and noted as not drawn; Mermaid and D2, which route no edge themselves, note the routing.

- `-render-run` writes a run's timeline or sequence as a self-contained SVG drawing with the `svg` form (`.svg` output, or `-render-form svg`) and as an SMIL-animated replay of the run with `svg-animated` (`-render-form svg-animated`): a timeline's lanes and state bars over a time axis with a sweeping cursor brightening the state held, a sequence's lifelines and message arrows appearing in run order with a marker travelling along each. No script, no external reference, deterministic output, one second per step of the run; a viewer without SMIL shows the complete final picture. `%render-run` and the `render_run` MCP tool take the same form names, and `-render-link` links lanes, single-state spans and participants as the PlantUML form does.

- `RunRuns`, `SelfCheck` and `RecordRun` RPCs with the `run_runs`, `self_check` and `record_run` MCP tools: repeated action runs and Monte Carlo samples with their observed features tabled and distributed (`sysml -runs`, `-observe`), the reflective self-check of a model against the `SysMLValidation` rule constraints with its findings and counts (`-self-check`, `-self-check-package`), and the recording of a case's run, sweep or sample into the model as a new model the service holds (`-record-run`, `-record-into`). The service advertises the `runs`, `self_check` and `record_run` capabilities; the Go, Python, Node, Java, Rust, Julia and MATLAB clients expose them as `RunAction`/`RunMonteCarlo`/`run_runs`, `SelfCheck`/`self_check` and `RecordRun`/`record_run`.

- The runtime performs an occurrence def or usage as the interaction its body states: `%occurrence <name>` (`Context.ExecuteOccurrence`) realizes each `message` between `event occurrence`s of its lifeline usages (`part`, `item`, `port`, `occurrence`, `individual`) as a transfer posted from the lifeline object holding the source event to the one holding the target event, in the order the `then` successions admit; at each event the actions its body states are performed once on the lifeline object and the constraints it asserts are checked there; a typed usage inherits its def's interaction, and an occurrence usage of a nested interaction is performed at its place with the outer lifelines of its names. The run trace records the sends and accepts, so `-run-render sequence` draws the run as the modelled sequence.
- The SysML v1 migrator spells an interaction parameter a call argument reads through the occurrence def (`in rpm[1] = 'Spin Up'::rpm`), which the bare name inside the perform's body would not reach.

- The service and `sysml-mcp` take the run options the CLI already had: `DiagnosticsRequest` selects lints (`disable_lint`, `enable_lint`, as `-disable-lint`/`-enable-lint`), `ExecuteActionRequest` and `ExecuteStateRequest` carry `advance`, `seed`, `draws` and `clock_step` (as `-advance`, `-seed`, `-draws`, `-clock-step`), `RunAnalysisRequest.tool_dry_run` previews the first external tool call as a typed `ToolDryRun` instead of running it (as `-tool-dry-run`), and `ListEnginesRequest.probe` starts each external engine and checks its handshake (as `-probe`); a malformed option is an `invalid_argument`. The Python, Node, Java, Rust, Julia and MATLAB clients expose the same options — the run options on their action and state executions, `tool_dry_run` on their analysis runs with the typed `ToolDryRun` preview on the result, `probe` on their engine listings and the lint selection on a diagnostics call (`Model.refresh_diagnostics` in Python, `Model.refreshDiagnostics` in Node and MATLAB, `Model.diagnostics(DiagnosticsOptions)` in Java, `Connection::diagnostics_with_lints` in Rust, `refresh_diagnostics` in Julia) — and read the clock a run stopped at back out of a failure; the Go client selects lints with `WithDisableLint`/`WithEnableLint`. The gRPC and client conformance cases cover them, and the catalog's unserved list shrinks by those flags and `-samples`, which `RunSweepRequest` already carried.

- A conformance oracle over the specifications' own textual-notation examples: `./scripts/download-spec-examples.sh` fetches the SysML v2 and KerML PDFs at the pinned release, renders them with `pdftotext -layout` and `tools/cmd/spec-examples` extracts every example of clause 7 (and SysML 8.4) into `examples/spec-examples/<document>/<clause>/<n>.sysml|.kerml`, each headed by its clause and page and completed mechanically (wrapped in a package or, for an action body fragment, an action definition; a private import for each undeclared name exactly one standard-library package declares). `TestSpecExamplesDiagnostics` ratchets our verdicts per file (`OPENSYSML_REQUIRE_SPEC_EXAMPLES=1` in CI), `pilot-diff` referees the root against the pilot, and `docs/project/spec-examples.md` adjudicates every disagreement against the text.

- `sysml -stdlib -convert ttl|api-json` converts the bundled standard library — every KerML and SysML file of it, as one model — to stdout, to a `-o` file, or to the Flexo MMS branch `-o` names, publishing the library as a project other projects type against by element id. Every element carries its normative id: the named ones the pilot implementation's library XMI assigns, the derived ones name-based uuids under them (`-id uuid` is the default here). A branch `-o` now takes `-convert api-json` as well as `ttl`, both replacing the branch's graph, and `FLEXO_TIMEOUT` (a Go duration, `60s` unless set) bounds each repository request, since a whole-library write takes minutes. `TestFlexoInterop` measures the published library against a live stack — elements and properties delivered through `/elements`, and a user model typing a part by `ISQ::mass` and `ScalarValues::Real` read back and re-converted to notation — and records it in `tools/referee/flexo/testdata/library_expected.txt`.

- SysON plugin: the exporter now writes `calc def` declarations, `calc` usages, calculation result expressions and named invocation arguments that SysON 2026.9.2's own serializer leaves out, so document queries declared in a SysON project reach the service; a feature reference SysON's importer could not resolve is reported as an error naming the calculation instead of being exported as a hole.

- **SysON plugin: document generation.** The `runWithOpenSysML` mutation and the "Run with OpenSysML" dialog gain `RENDER_DOCUMENT`, which renders the selected document definition as Markdown or HTML, previews it (HTML in a sandboxed frame) and offers it as a download named as a rendered set names it, so links between downloaded documents hold, and `RUN_DOCUMENT_QUERY`, which runs the selected document query with bindings given as the dialog's inputs (a qualified name binds the element, any other expression a literal) and shows its rows as a table whose project elements select on click. The exporter now leaves out a project's unextended copy of a `library package` the service bundles, such as `DocumentQueries`, so the copy SysON needs to resolve names does not shadow the service's own library; a copy adding members is exported with a warning.

- The hygiene suite caps test files at 2,000 lines and test fixtures (files under a
  `testdata/` directory or named `*.golden.*`) at 200 KiB. The files already over a cap are
  allowlisted at their current size with a reason, so none of them can grow further and an
  entry is retired as soon as its file shrinks back under the cap.

- **Use cases execute.** A use case definition or usage is an action definition or usage (SysML v2 §7.19), so it now runs wherever an action does: `sysml -action DroneUseCases::chargeDrone model.sysml` runs its body start to done, `%action`, `%step` and `%tokens` in the REPL drive it, and the LSP debug surface starts it. Its `in`/`out` parameters read as an action's, an `include use case` member performs the included use case as a nested action whose parameters bind from the including one, its `subject` and `actor` members bind as a requirement's do, before its defaults are initialized, so a step or a default reads the objects they are valued with, and a use case specializing another inherits its body as an action does. `-analysis` still refuses a use case, which is no analysis case.

- **`sysml -validate` reports for a CI service.** `-format sarif` writes the run's diagnostics as SARIF 2.1.0 — one run, the tool's name and version, one rule per diagnostic code, each finding's severity as its level, its file relative to the working directory with line and column, and the fixes analysis offers — for GitHub code scanning and the other SARIF readers; `-format junit` writes JUnit XML with one testsuite per file, one failing testcase per finding and a passing one for a file with none; `-format json` is `-json`. A document goes to stdout or to the file `-o` names. `-baseline <file>` reads back a JSON report `-validate -json -o` wrote as the findings already known and suppresses each finding with the same code, file and message, whatever line it moved to, from the report and the exit status, saying how many it left out. `-warnings-as-errors` ends the run on a warning as on an error while the report keeps the warning's severity. The same SARIF and JUnit writers now serve the pilot-differential referee, from `internal/frontend/report`.
- **A composite GitHub Action, `Open-MBEE/OpenSysML@<tag>`,** installs that release's `sysml` with the install scripts, runs `-validate` over a path in the chosen format and, for SARIF, uploads the report to code scanning; the repository's workflow runs it over `examples/`.

- The constraint tier reports a variant usage whose kind is inconsistent with its owning variation (`variant-kind`, SysML v2 §7.6.7): a variant specializes the variation it is a variant of (`checkUsageVariationDefinitionSpecialization`, `checkUsageVariationUsageSpecialization`), so the variation definition, or the types of the variation usage, type the variant and the kind's typing rule judges them — `variant action a;` in `variation part def PC` or in `variation part pv : P` is `An action must be typed by action definitions.`, naming the variation and the type it gives the variant. A bare `variant x;` restating a separately declared usage declares no kind and is not judged.

- `SymbolInfo.metadata` carries `variation` on a variation definition or usage and on an enumeration definition, `variant` on a variant usage and on an enumerated value, and `abstract` on every variation whether or not the keyword is written (SysML v2.1 §7.6.7, §8.3), so the Python, TypeScript, Java and MATLAB clients and the MCP `symbol` tool can tell a variation point from a part and a variant from a member; the MCP `symbol` text now lists the metadata, and the wire-contract reference documents every metadata key.

- gRPC `VerificationVerdict` carries `instance_id` and `instance_type_id`, the object the case ran on as its owner, so the verdicts a case a part definition owns answers once per object are told apart by a client. The Go, Python, TypeScript, Rust, Java, Julia and MATLAB clients expose both (`VerificationVerdict.instance_id`/`instanceId`), and the Python and Rust explanations name the object as a requirement verdict's does.

- A view's filter conditions — its `filter` members, the filter clauses of its `expose` lines and those inherited from its view definition — now prune what a tree or interconnection rendering draws beneath each exposed element: a member a condition rejects is not drawn, nor its subtree, so `filter @SysML::PartUsage;` draws the parts without their attributes and ports and `filter not @Hidden;` blacklists the elements marked `#Hidden`. A connector whose end is pruned is counted in the notices instead of drawn. A view with no filter conditions renders as before.
- An attribute usage `depth` on a view (`view v { expose vehicle; attribute depth = 2; render asTreeDiagram; }`), or on the nearest view definition it specializes, bounds how many levels of nested elements a tree or interconnection rendering draws below each exposed element; `0` or none draws every level. An element at the bound keeps its ports and compartment rows and carries the note `nested deeper than N levels; not shown`, and the elements directly below the bound are counted once in the notices. A view definition may declare the attribute typed (`attribute depth : ScalarValues::Natural[0..1];`) for its specializations to redefine; a `depth` bound to anything but a non-negative integer literal is the `view-depth-value` error.
- `sysml -render-depth <levels>` (with `-render` and `-render-all`), `%render … depth=<levels>`, the LSP `opensysml/render` request's `depth` (advertised as `openSysmlRenderDepth`), the gRPC `RenderViewRequest.depth` — taken by the Python, Node, Rust, Java, Go (`WithDepth`), Julia and MATLAB clients and the sysml-engine `RenderView` request — and the VS Code `opensysml.diagram.depth` setting override the view's depth for one render.
- A `DiagramLayout::Layout` with `collapsed = true` about an element now draws it with no children in every form (text, DOT, Mermaid, PlantUML, D2), keeping its ports and the `collapsed` tag; the text form omits the compartments a Layout's `showParts = false` and the other `show…` switches hide, as DOT already did.

- The VS Code diagram panel gains the navigation of a GUI diagram tool, all panel-only and writing nothing to the model: Ctrl+wheel or pinch zoom about the pointer, drag-to-pan on empty canvas, **Fit** and **1:1** buttons with `+`/`-`/`0` keys, a viewport each panel keeps across reloads; click-to-select, which emphasises a node's relationships and neighbours and dims the rest; a **Relations** list showing or hiding relationship kinds per panel, with `opensysml.diagram.hideRelations` as a workspace-wide default; and **Auto layout**, which lays the diagram out with ELK ignoring the model's `Layout`/`Route` annotations until the next rendering or **Reset to model**.

- The VS Code diagram panel's `opensysml.diagram.routing` setting sends every edge the automatic layout did not route through the panel's libavoid router when set to `router`, a stated `DiagramLayout::Route` included: the stated routes stay in the model, undrawn, and their waypoint handles are hidden. `model`, the default, draws a stated route verbatim as before. The setting is the panel's alone; `-render-lines` and the exports keep the stated routes.
- `SysML: Move Diagram to New Window` (in the diagram panel's title-bar menu and the command palette) moves the diagram into a floating window, keeping its drawing, chosen view and style; a diagram opened afterwards joins it there. `opensysml.diagram.openIn: newWindow` moves the first diagram out as soon as it opens.

- The VS Code extension writes the language server it reached to the **SysML v2** Output channel as the server reports itself — `Connected to sysml-lsp nightly-20261019-e8607989d.` — and, when that is not the build the extension was packaged with (a release's `v0.10.1`, a snapshot's own tag), says which was: `Connected to sysml-lsp v0.10.0; this extension (0.10.2-nightly-20261019-e8607989d) was packaged with nightly-20261019-e8607989d.` A server that reports no name or version is said so. A diagram or diagnostic that an updated extension does not change is thereby told apart from one the configured or `PATH` server, built elsewhere, still produces.

- The VS Code diagram panel shows a drawn node's related elements without editing the view: "Show related elements…" on a node's context menu lists what relates to it by relationship kind with counts, and the elements chosen are drawn and marked as included, until "Hide from diagram" leaves them out again; nothing is written to the `.sysml` file. Behind it, `opensysml/render` takes an `include` parameter (capability `openSysmlRenderInclude`) drawing elements as if the view exposed them and marking each node `included`, and the new `opensysml/relatedElements` request (capability `openSysmlRelatedElements`) groups an element's generalizations, specializations, types, typed usages, owner, owned members, connections, flows, bindings and successions.

- `sysml-wasm` (and the `@openmbee/opensysml-wasm` package) now serves `RenderView`, answering with the engine's drawing shape; the release and nightly wasm assets and the npm package also carry `sysml-prod.wasm.gz`, the gzip-compressed browser build of the `sysml` REPL, so a page can load both the landing-page engine and the REPL from the published package. The site's engine and REPL loaders accept a plain `.wasm` URL (compressed in transit by a CDN) as well as a local `.wasm.gz`.

### Changed

- **Every v1 association is a `connection def`.** An anonymous association was written only as its member-end properties; it is now a `connection def` named after its end types (`WheelToCar`, marked `MigrationMetadata::SynthesizedName`) whose one usage is a `connection` typed by it joining `part` usages of the two end types, as a named association already was. An end a class owns crosses the property it is written as (`end wheels : Wheel crosses car.wheels;`), the mapping's feature chain, where that is sound; where it is not (an untyped opposite end, an end type not written or written as a usage, a non-binary association) the def is still written, the end crosses nothing and the report says why. A `private` or `protected` property an end crosses is written visible, as one a connector reaches is, and the report says which def reaches it. An end the association owns carries its multiplicity as the cross multiplicity (`end [0..*] ref car : Car;`), so the "End feature must have multiplicity 1" warning no longer fires on migrated ends. A link of an anonymous association now specializes its def like a link of a named one.
- **A tree view draws a `connection def` as a line between its end types.** A `connection def` whose two ends are typed by drawn definitions, and that carries no other member, is drawn as a line between their boxes labelled with its ends (and its name, unless a migration made it up), as a block definition diagram draws an association, instead of as a box of its own; the line wears the def's `Style` and `Route` and carries its `Note`s. An end that crosses a `part` or `ref` the tree draws as an edge from its owner takes that edge over, so a composite association is one line with the composition's diamond and the part's name, wearing the def's `Style` and `Route`, not a composition and a line; the line is plain, not the heavy connector of an interconnection diagram. An association block, a def that inherits its ends or specializes another, or one whose end holds a qualifier stays a box. The migrator pins a v1 association's diagram route to a def the tree draws as a line (`Route about WheelToCar`) instead of reporting it `not drawn` (the route of one kept as a box still is), and a shown end the association owns exposes the def, not the end, so same-named ends of two defs on one diagram raise no duplicate-member warning.

- Preserve ownership references needed by API-JSON import when omitting derived properties from compact output.

- `sysml -convert api-json` writes the OMG API JSON element shape for booleans: every non-derived boolean attribute of an element's metaclass is stated explicitly (`true` or `false`) instead of only when true; the reader tells an external API-JSON document from this mapping's own by an explicit criterion rather than by the presence of `isImpliedIncluded`, and documents that omit false flags still read as before.

- API JSON export preserves graph property order for sidecar-backed values through the `sysx:mappingPropertyOrder` extension.

- API-JSON writing now caches namespace and library-name lookups, reducing repeated name-resolution work without changing standard or compact output.

- API JSON export allocates less and avoids copying the model graph.

- `pilot-apijson-diff` records this repository's side of the comparison too: the baseline's `provenance.tools` gains `api-json-writer`, a digest over the non-test Go sources of `internal/translate/export`, so a writer change that moves the buckets is named by `-check` and by `TestCommittedBaselineStatesThisRepositorysProvenance` (`provenance.tools[api-json-writer].sourceDigest`) instead of surfacing as bare count drift; `-update` records it.

- The API JSON differential's pilot bridge now reads this repository's own libraries (`internal/workspace/libs/stdlib/OpenSysML Libraries/`, the directory the pilot differential hands its validators) after the pinned standard library, as an explicit `--extension-library` directory the referee passes, so members whose naming feature, type or redefined feature lives in `DocumentQueries`, `AnalysisRecords` or `OpenSysMLRenderings` resolve on the pilot's side and compare by name; the baseline records the directory as the `opensysml-libraries` input.

- The `n` performances of an action usage declared `[n]` in a `while`, `loop`, `for` or `if` body, written or inherited, are sibling tokens of the body's flow whose interleavings `explore` enumerates and `check` proves or bounds, as for a step at the top level of an action body; the coverage note that reported them as run one move each is gone, and the conformance case of two performances racing on one attribute now states its admissible outcome set.

- **A calculation result with no stated multiplicity is held by its effective multiplicity, as an action's parameters are.** A bare `return : Real` (or a result inherited from the calculation definition) is a reference usage, so SysML §7.6.3 gives it no implicit `[1..1]`: its effective multiplicity is the `[0..*]` of `Performances::Evaluation::result` it redefines (SysML §7.19.2, KerML §7.3.4.2), and one computed value is held as a one-element sequence on every surface — `%calc`, `%eval`, `%analysis`, `%sweep`, `%runs`, `-json`, traces, gRPC `EvaluateCalc`/`RunAnalysis`/`RunSweep`, the MCP tools, export and the clients. `return : Real` now answers `[0.75]` where it answered `0.75`; `return : Real[1]` and `return : Real[0..1]` still answer `0.75`, and an explicit `[0..*]` or `[2]` is unchanged. A test or client reading one value writes `return : Real[1]`, or takes the sole element. A body-local attribute, item or part usage stating no multiplicity holds the one value such a result gives it, its implicit `[1..1]`; an input parameter is bound as its argument came.

- `analysis case def`, `verification case def`, `analysis case <name>` and `verification case <name>` are reported as `nonstandard-notation` (an error under `-strict`): the SysML v2 productions spell `analysis def`, `analysis`, `verification def` and `verification` alone. The declaration is read as the analysis or verification case it names — an `analysis case def` no longer types its usages as a plain case definition — and a quick fix drops `case`.
- A `;` after the closing bare condition of a constraint, predicate or invariant body is reported as `nonstandard-notation`: that condition is the body's result expression, which no production terminates. The terminator stays in the syntax tree and a quick fix deletes it.

- **The compliance map and the validation census agree on every constraint they both name.** `docs/project/spec-compliance.md` cites the pilot's `validate…` constraint names as the manifest spells them (a raw identifier or the formal document's name resolve to the same row), writes `spec:validate…` for a name of the specification the pilot has no constraint for, and follows a citation with `(census faithful)` where the row describes a different rule or a sub-case than the census row. `validation-census -check` now refuses a cited name that is neither a manifest row, a stated `spec:` name nor a Go function of the tree, and a rule row whose status differs from the census status of a constraint it cites bare. Reconciling the two pages against the pinned pilot validator moved five census rows (`validateCaseDefinitionOnlyOneObjective`, `validateCaseUsageOnlyOneObjective`, `validateExhibitStateUsageReference` and `validatePerformActionUsageReference` to approximate; `validateDefinitionVariationMembership` to faithful) and four compliance rows (`expose` in a `view def` body to approximate; invocation of a non-behavior, an abstract metaclass on an annotation and a variant outside a variation to faithful).

- **Preserve UML opaque body text during migration.** Each raw body and matching language now appear as a non-executed textual representation with whitespace preserved, while supported body translations continue to use the trimmed first body and remain executable.
- **Retain UML state-machine structure and termination semantics.** Composite states with one region retain that region as a parallel sub-state. A region left without an active child by default entry counts as complete, whether its stand-in was entered or it has no stand-in or start; a stand-in's running do action defers that completion until it ends. A region activated later by a transition or fork branch must reach its final state. This matches PSSM *Entering 004*. A first entry through history uses the same default, while an owner with no default entry remains an error. Terminate pseudostates now terminate the whole state machine.
- **Clarify the v1-to-v2 transformation census.** Timed triggers and preserved opaque/state behavior are counted as faithful, while trigger-scoped event acceptance is recorded as deliberate.

- **A release rehearsal now tests under the tag.** The rehearsal checked out a branch, so the Go toolchain recorded `(devel)` as the module version and every unstamped test binary reported `dev`; the real tag pipeline checks out the tag and the same binaries report the release version, which is how a `v0.10.0` test failure escaped a green rehearsal. The Go suite's jobs now tag the checkout locally as the release being rehearsed before building anything, and the stand-in `CIRCLE_TAG` is derived by `scripts/rehearsal-version.sh`.

- A Systems or Kernel Semantic Library collection read on a package-level usage answers what the model declares, exactly as on an instantiated object: `p1.voids` is `[]` and `isSolid` `true`, `p1.suboccurrences` the subsetting parts, `p3.performedActions` the performed action, `p1.predecessors` `[]`, `p1.middleTimeSlice` `null`, where the Systems tier read `<undetermined>` and the Kernel tier answered; an abstract library collection of any kind (`performedActions`, a `ref`) holds its subsetters' values on both levels. `<undetermined>` remains for a usage whose own multiplicity the model leaves open, an unbound input, and a library collection whose lower bound nothing declared satisfies.

- `internal/translate/codegen`, the native compilation backend behind `sysml -compile`, is reached only from `cmd/sysml` and the black-box suite `tests/codegen`; `tests/hygiene` pins that boundary over every package's imports, tests included. The differential contract against the interpreter and its fixture moved from `internal/frontend/repl` to `tests/codegen`, and the compiler's record and `<unset>` refusal tests from `internal/exec/runtime` to the codegen package, so a build or test run of the runtime no longer compiles the backend.

- **Constraint census: the unknown `validate*` rows are triaged.** `docs/project/constraint-census-triage.md` records, for each of the 88 `validate*` rows the census listed as unknown, the smallest violating and conforming models, the verdict of `sysml -validate` and of the pinned pilot validators on each, and the class the row falls in. 29 pilot rows now cite the formal document's name for the same rule (`validateElementFilterMembershipIsBoolean` is the document's `validateElementFilterMembershipConditionIsBoolean`, and so on), so those document names count as pilot-named instead of as separate unknown rows; `validateOccurrenceUsagePortionKind` is recorded faithful against `W10BPortionOwnerPass`. The remaining rows are rules the textual notation cannot violate or rules the Beta 2 documents no longer state; none is a gap. The 208 `check*` rows are probed through the model-level `istype` query over two committed models: 43 implied specializations are observed and recorded against `Model.computeImplicitBases` — 29 faithful, and 14 approximate because a usage is typed by its base library feature's type rather than subsetting that feature, the one launch-ready item the record lists; the rest cannot be observed through `-convert api-json` (which writes no implied relationships) or `istype`, and the record says why for each. The 235 `derive*` rows are tabulated by whether the API JSON form emits the property (6 yes, 20 partial, 209 no), and the 128 constraint names only the Beta 2 PDFs state are each mapped to the recorded name they truncate, misspell or rename; the usage-subsets-base-feature item is the only launch-ready gap left in the backlog.

- **A SysML v1 owned rule migrates as the OMG mapping shapes it: a `constraint def` whose result is the specification, asserted by a usage typed by it.** `sysml -migrate` writes `constraint def 'Positive mass' { in ref context : Vehicle[1]; context.mass > 0 }` beside `assert constraint 'positive mass' : 'Positive mass' { in ref :>> context = this; }` in the constrained block, instead of one `assert constraint 'positive mass' { mass > 0 }`; the context parameter is written only when the specification reads the block's features. A rule of a constraint def, a behavior or an operation keeps the inline form, since no context parameter reaches their parameters, and a non-Boolean specification stays unmapped. The runtime's `this` inside an asserted constraint or requirement usage now names the object owning it, so `-validate=<object>` evaluates the migrated form; checked about no object (`-constraint` without a subject, as the MDK plugin verifies a v1 block's constraint), `this` denotes an exemplar of the type made from its declared defaults for the check and discarded with it.

- The VS Code diagram panel no longer creates model elements: the toolbar's **Add…** list and the node menu's **Add …** and **… from here…** entries are gone, since new members and connections are written in the text. The panel keeps **Go to declaration**, **Rename…**, **Move to…**, **Delete…**, dragging a node or an edge's waypoint, and <kbd>Shift</kbd>+drop to reparent. The server's `addMember` and `addConnection` operations are unchanged for the language server's `opensysml/applyModelEdit`, gRPC and MCP clients.

- **Superseded measurement records are archived.** The September 2026 performance census, execution study and profile and the release comparisons up to 0.10.0 against 0.9.2 move from `docs/project/` to `docs/project/archive/`, indexed by [`docs/project/archive/README.md`](docs/project/archive/README.md); `docs/project/` keeps the current comparison, [0.10.1 against 0.10.0](docs/project/performance-release-0.10.1-vs-0.10.0.md), and `docs/internals/performance.md` the method. The staged plans that built the Python gRPC bindings are removed from `docs/internals/design/`; the design they implemented is [`python-grpc-bindings.md`](docs/internals/design/python-grpc-bindings.md).

- **Sweeps and Monte Carlo plans are a package of their own.** `internal/exec/sweep` holds the sweep plans, ranges, rows, tables and work queue and the Monte Carlo plan that `internal/exec/runtime` held; the runtime offers them the parameters a calc or case declares (`Context.Parameters`), the classification of a value against a type (`Context.ClassifyValue`) and the scalar-type reads they type ranges by (`Context.PositiveScalar`, `Context.QuantityNumber`), and imports none of the packages layered on it, which `tests/hygiene` pins. What the CLI, REPL and gRPC service print and return is unchanged.

- **The compliance map distinguishes SysML v2 / KerML rules from UML-derived and OpenSysML ones.** A new 🧩 *Extension* status marks rows whose normative source is UML 2.5.1, PSSM or fUML (pseudostates, call-event triggers, state-body `fork`/`join`, run-to-completion and completion events, deferred events) or an OpenSysML library or notation (the math, stochastic and state-activity libraries, document queries and rendering, analysis engines, solving); ✅ *Faithful* now claims SysML v2 / KerML semantics only. No row's implementation or test column changed. The build-time census counts the new status, and the Tier 5 wording in the architecture notes, the status tables and the README state the supported subset and its evidence — every conformance case passes, self-assessed against the Kernel Semantic Library with no external behavioral referee — and that a default run is one tool-chosen linearization of the model's partial order, with `explore`/`check` showing the whole set.

- `explore` and `check` no longer report `complete` over a construct whose unordered alternatives the runtime performs in one order without a choice point. Such a construct is a typed coverage gap (`Exploration.Unenumerated`, `CheckReport.Unenumerated`; none is recorded today, the repeated-step interleavings that were one being choice points now): the exploration is incomplete while one stands, its status reads `incomplete after N runs; not enumerated: …`, the result's standing is observed rather than proved, and the gap is carried by identifier in the REPL verdict, the gRPC `ExplorationStatus.unenumerated` field and the Python and Node clients' `unenumerated`. Conformance cases state the gaps exploring them records under `exploreGaps`, replacing the prose `exploreNotes`.
- The fixed scheduling policies (`reverse`, `declared`, `seed:<n>`) record the step order their round of due do behaviors takes ahead of a dispatch that would act as the `step order` choice point the unit-at-a-time policies draw, so a default run reports the same choice points `explore` enumerates; nothing about what runs changes, and the default trace of a state machine with a do behavior beside such a dispatch gains one `choice at t=…` line per such move.

- The export suite's largest fixtures are under the 200 KiB fixture cap: a Turtle golden the writer makes larger than that is committed as its sha256 digest and first 40 lines (`*.golden.ttl.sha256`, written by `-update` like the whole goldens and compared byte-exact; a mismatch writes the whole output beside it as `*.actual`), the four largest toolkit interchange exports are committed gzip-compressed, and the superseded `then done;` membership fixture is cut down to the action that exhibits the shape. The `oversizeFixtures` allowlist in `tests/hygiene/size_test.go` is empty.

- **Declare the bundled OpenSysML extension libraries as non-standard.** `DiagramLayout`, `IdentityMetadata`, `MigrationMetadata` and `SysMLValidation` were written `standard library package`, which both asserted membership of the normative Kernel/SysML libraries and drew a pilot warning (`User library packages should not be marked as standard`); they are project extensions and now read `library package`. RDF export no longer marks them `sysml:isStandard true`.

- The RDF and API JSON writers now own the `Membership` through which a `FeatureReferenceExpression` names its referent and a `FeatureChainExpression` its target feature where the notation places it — before the result parameter, and between the operand and the result for a chain — and list it under `ownedMembership` as well as `ownedRelationship` (KerML 1.1 § 8.2.5.8.3 `FeatureReferenceMember`/`FeatureChainMember`; `referent` and `targetFeature` are derived from it, § 8.3.4.8.5 and § 8.3.4.8.4). The `Membership` a `MetadataAccessExpression` or an invocation owns to the element it names is an `ownedMembership` too. Previously the writer restated the derived edge as a `Membership` appended after the result and absent from `ownedMembership`.

- The Flexo MMS interoperability expectation (`tools/referee/flexo/testdata`) records what the current RDF and API element forms survive in a running stack: a connector end's feature chain reaches the service as one `FeatureChaining` per link owned through the end's `ReferenceSubsetting` (`chainingFeature`, `featureChained` and `ownedFeatureChaining` all delivered), the element form's root `Namespace` and its `OwningMembership` are served back, and the source-position annotations (`sysx:sourceLine`, `sysx:sourceColumn`, `sysx:sourceEndLine`, `sysx:sourceEndColumn`) join the `sysx:` properties the service drops on both its graph-load and commit paths; the measured totals are in `docs/project/roadmap.md` § D3.

- The Flexo MMS interoperability measurement — `TestFlexoInterop`, `TestFlexoInteropApply`, their fixtures and committed expectations — moved from `internal/translate/interop/flexo` to the `tools` module as `tools/referee/flexo`, beside the other referees; the package under `internal/` now holds only what `sysml -convert`, `-sync-diff` and `-sync-apply` use (configuration, branch URLs, the conditional graph push and the SPARQL read of a commit). Run the gate with `FLEXO_INTEROP=1 go test -C tools ./referee/flexo -run TestFlexoInterop`.

- A `DiagramLayout::Layout` binding only `collapsed` or `show…` switches no longer needs `x` and `y`: `metadata Layout about vehicle::engine { collapsed = true; }` folds the element in every form and leaves its place to the form, so a fold-only annotation no longer turns the drawing into a positioned one that leaves unplaced nodes undrawn. The LSP rendering reports such a node `collapsed` with no `x`, `y`; the gRPC and engine renderings report its geometry `collapsed` with the new `unplaced` flag set (`RenderGeometry.unplaced`), its coordinates meaning nothing. `opensysml/applyModelEdit`'s `setLayout` writes such a `Layout` for a `layout` given `collapsed` and no `x`, `y`. A `Layout` binding a size, one coordinate, or nothing without `x` and `y` is still the `diagram-layout-value` error.

- **Two imports bringing distinct elements of one name into a namespace now hide both from it.** KerML 7.2.5.4 excludes such memberships from the importing namespace, so an unqualified reference to the name (or short name) is looked up in the enclosing namespaces instead and is an ordinary `unresolved reference` where none has it, in default and strict mode alike, with a hint naming the imports that hide it and the qualified names that reach each; the `Duplicate of imported member name` warning stays on the import and marks the hidden memberships. A namespace re-exporting both imports has no such member, a qualified name into the importing namespace does not reach a hidden one, and a specialization finds none through its general's protected imports. One element reached twice, an owned member of the name, and an inherited member beside an imported one keep resolving as before (a specialization inherits both and is warned, as the type is). A called name is read the same way, so two `calc def pick` reached through two imports leave `pick(2)` unresolved with the hint, while an overload set one namespace owns, a type inherits or one import brings still selects by signature. Previously the first import's member was bound, as the OMG pilot implementation does.

- **`install.sh` and `install.ps1` fetch the `opensysml-<platform>` bundle only when a tool it carries is asked for.** `--tools sysml-grpc` or `--tools sysml-jupyter-kernel` alone now downloads and verifies only that tool's own asset, the `from:` line of the summary lists just the assets that will be fetched, and `tar` (or `unzip`) is required only when the bundle is.

- The SysML v1 migrator writes an InstanceSpecification as one usage typed by its classifiers — `part myCar : Vehicle { attribute :>> mass = 1350.5; }` — with the usage kind following the classifier (`item`, `occurrence`, `constraint`, `port`, `attribute`), an instance of an actor, use case or requirement subsetting the classifier's usage, and an instance link as a `connection` usage between the instance usages typed by the association's `connection def`, as the OMG v1-to-v2 transformation does, in place of an `individual part def` specializing the classifiers. Slots holding instances are bound to the instance usages (`part :>> mcs = 'mcs 1';`), a property default naming an instance is written as its `default`, a simulation configuration's `target` subsets the target instance, and instance tables list the package-owned usages of the classifier.
- Instance tables and generic tables over `InstanceSpecification` list the usages the instance specifications became: a classifier written as a usage (an actor, a use case, a requirement) is no longer listed among its own instances, a classifier-scoped instance table lists none of the classifier's features, the `InstanceSpecification` metaclass filter selects the instance usages rather than occurrence definitions, and neither lists the usages the migration itself writes for the end types of an association or for an actor, which stand for no instance specification. A property whose default names a result snapshot that infers its classifier from its slots keeps the default.

- **The Java client's protobuf message classes are generated at build time rather than committed.** `client/java/opensysml-client` generates `org.openmbee.opensysml.proto` from `api/proto/sysml.proto` during the Maven build, with `protoc` pinned to the `protobuf-java` version; the 279 generated files leave the repository, `make proto-buf` regenerates the Go stubs only, and `api/proto/buf.gen.yaml` no longer needs the Buf Schema Registry's remote Java plugin. A `.gitattributes` marks the remaining generated stubs, test fixtures, baselines and binary artifacts `linguist-generated`, so GitHub collapses them in diffs and leaves them out of the language statistics.

- A call of a Kernel Function Library declaration the runtime does not evaluate as a function (`BaseFunctions::'istype'`, `'hastype'`, `'@'`, `'as'`, `'meta'`, `'all'`, `'['`) is refused with the library's reason — the function is abstract and its type parameter is a type reference the operator notation evaluates — before its arguments are read, where it failed as `cannot evaluate definition <Type>` before; the conformance record pins every such declaration beside the operator notation that evaluates, `ComplexFunctions::ToString`/`ToComplex`, `NaturalFunctions::'/'` and `OccurrenceFunctions::create` on real receivers.

- The homepage's Saturn V mass roll-up scene is drawn in the same colours as the stack scene instead of the plain white figure: a scene now tones its boxes (`primary`, `secondary`, `accent`), the stack by the kind its model declares as before, and the roll-up filling the top-level parts like the stack's projects, leaving nested engines plain like its components, and accenting any part whose roll-up the engine reports undetermined.
- The scene's instrument unit says what it is for: its status line explains that the model leaves the mass out on purpose and why that is valid SysML, and a second step, "Give it its mass", draws `MassRollup::saturnVComplete`, the same vehicle with the mass supplied on the usage, whose roll-up completes at 2,943,728 kg. A scene variant can build on another (`requires`): its toggle is offered while that one is on, and switching it off returns there.
- A rendering heads a redefining usage that declares no typing of its own with the types it inherits from the features it redefines (KerML 1.0 §7.3.4.5), each once and through a chain of untyped redefinitions, as it already did for a usage inherited unredefined: `part :>> instrumentUnit { :>> mass = 2000 [kg]; }` is drawn `instrumentUnit : InstrumentUnit`, a redefined `subcomponents` collection carries its `Component`, and `part both :>> left, right` over `left : A` and `right : B` reads `both : A, B`.
