Every rendering form that heads a node with a type follows — interconnection, tree, general, case, mixed, sequence, behavior and the element table — and so do the `typings` of RenderView.

- **The documentation homepage runs on the combined WebAssembly module through the typed browser client.** The landing diagram is drawn, instantiated and run through `@openmbee/opensysml/browser`'s `connectWasm` over `sysml-wasm.wasm.gz` built from the tree (`make docs-engine-assets`), in a Worker, instead of a hand-rolled synchronous JSON-RPC adapter over `sysml-engine.wasm.gz`; the module's URL is the single `data-osml-wasm` attribute of the hero, so pointing the site at a published `@openmbee/opensysml-wasm` release is a one-line change. A scene (`editors/vscode/src/landing/scene.ts`) names the model, the drawn view, the shown notation and the state machine the controls run, so a second scene can be added without touching the page's plumbing. The engine and the combined module answer `RenderView` as the protojson `RenderViewResponse` the gRPC service returns (nested `geometry`/`style`, `origin` spans, ports, routes, notes, canvas and rows), the module advertises `render_view`, and `renderedViewFromJson` reads such a saved rendering; the snapshot the page paints before the module loads is that response, regenerated by the same test that proves the module parses, renders, instantiates and runs the model. A traced `ExecuteState` answers the service's `DocumentEvent` records (`time` as a `DocumentValue`, `object` and `target` as `DocumentObject`s) instead of the engine's own trace shape, and the module advertises `state_trace`, so the page's debugger steps the run through the typed `executeState`. The showcase state machine's first state is `begin`, so it no longer shadows the standard library's `StateAction::start`.

- `make lint` runs staticcheck under `GOMEMLIMIT=2GiB`: unbounded, its heap on this module peaked near 4 GiB and the 4 GiB CI executor killed the job on some runs, while the soft limit holds the peak near 2 GiB for about a tenth more GC time.

- **`sysml-mcp -http` on an address other machines reach** (`0.0.0.0:8765`, a host name) is refused at startup with exit status 2 and a message naming `-token-file`, `-token` and `OPENSYSML_MCP_TOKEN` unless a bearer token is configured; it was served with a warning that the server authenticates no one. A port alone or a loopback address is served as before, with no token, and enforces a token when one is given.

- `sysml-mcp` confines every file a tool touches to `-root` through one shared check, `toolkit.Root`, for the workspace, runtime and session tool groups alike. The groups now agree: a dangling symlink is judged by where it points, so one leading out of the root is refused by the workspace and runtime tools too; every group reports a refused path with the same wording; and a `-root` that does not exist is refused at startup.

- **The project is named OpenSysML MDK.** The Runtime Environment and Development Kit is the second generation of OpenMBEE's Model Development Kit, and MDK now names it in the README, the documentation site, the contributor guide and the landing page where it used to be called the OpenSysML REDK. The description, the repository, the Go module path, the `opensysml` package and registry names, the binaries, the release assets, the `OPENSYSML_*` environment variables and the RE/DK build names are unchanged. The Cameo Systems Modeler plugin under `editors/mdk/`, until now itself named OpenSysML MDK, is Magic MDK; its Maven coordinates, plugin id, Java packages and the `Magic MDK DocGen` profile it writes into models keep their names.

- **A v1 connector that only carries item flows is migrated as its flows alone.** The SysML v1 migrator wrote a connector realizing item flows as a `connection` plus, beside it, an anonymous `flow of T from a.p.x to b.q.x` per item flow, two unrelated members a renderer draws as two lines where the source tool drew one connector with an item-flow label. A flow is itself the connection between the two ports, so a connector carrying nothing of its own — no name, type, comment or stereotype — whose every item flow is writable is now written as its flow(s) alone, each named after its item flow (an anonymous one a diagram shows is named after the connector's ends, as a shown connection is, and marked `MigrationMetadata::SynthesizedName`; a strict migration lists it in the stand-in comment instead), and the diagram's `DiagramLayout::Route` and the view's `expose` go on the flows, every flow of the connector exposed and routed. The report says the connector was written as its flows. A connector with a name or type of its own, a comment, a stereotype, or an item flow that cannot be written keeps its `connection` as before, and its writable flows are still named after their item flows (unexposed, since the view already exposes the connection). An interconnection view now reads a `Route` about a `flow` as it does one about a `connection`. OMG's `FlowUsage_Factory` spelling (`flow f :> c of T from … to …`) is not used: both this validator and the pilot's reject it, which the transformation census records.

- The SysML v1 migrator writes a UML Interaction as a SysML v2 sequence — an `occurrence def` (a `verification def` for a test case) whose lifelines are usages typed by the represented property's type, holding `event occurrence`s ordered by `then`, each message a `message` between the events it leaves and reaches (a signal as the payload, a call performed at the receiving event, a reply carrying the call's result), interaction uses as ordered occurrence usages with their arguments bound, state invariants as asserting events — instead of an executable `action def` scenario of sends and accepts; lifelines standing for the Interaction's own properties (a use case's black-box scenario) now resolve, and a SysML Sequence Diagram becomes a `SequenceView` view carrying the tool's lifeline `Layout`s and message `Route`s, which the sequence renderer and the layout lint now read. Combined fragments, duration and time constraints, execution specifications and gates are reported where v2 sequence notation has no form; reply assignments and arguments reading the owning block's features are reported as not written.

- The SysML v1 migrator no longer writes a stereotype or relationship kind as a comment beside the element (`/* «Trace» */`, `/* applied stereotype «Critical» */`, `/* «Block» tags with no v2 form: … */`), which modeling tools render as a note on every diagram. A «Trace», «Copy» or other dependency kept as a plain `dependency` is named by its kind (`dependency 'A traces B' from A to B`), as an include or refine connection is, and every stereotype the v2 form does not carry is a `@MigrationMetadata::AppliedStereotype { stereotype = …; profile = …; tags = …; }` marker in the element's body; a UML Extend kept as a dependency is named `'A extends B'` with its extension points in its `doc`. Under `-strict` the marker is a line comment and the dependency's name alone carries the kind. Documentation comments are unchanged.

- **A Cameo Requirement Table or Refine Requirement Matrix is reconstructed from its owning scope.** Cameo stores no content for these two diagram kinds — the `DiagramRepresentationObject` has a `binaryObject` with no `streamContentID` and no `usedObjects`, and the scope is serialized nowhere in the model file — so their views came out empty (`the diagram shows nothing; the view exposes nothing`). The SysML v1 migrator now writes the view of a Requirement Table owned by `P` exposing every `requirement` written under `P` (nested and contained), and the view of a Refine Requirement Matrix owned by `P` exposing every «Refine» written under `P`, the `connection 'A refines B'` and the two usages it joins; both are still rendered `Views::asElementTable`. The report row says plainly that the tool stored no rows (cells) and that the view is reconstructed from the owning scope, with the count exposed, and the view is approximated. A diagram of either kind that lists `usedObjects` or has a symbol stream, or that carries a «DiagramTable»/«DependencyMatrix» application with the tool's scope, is migrated as before.
- **A view rendered `asElementTable` lists each exposed element once.** The table rendering wrote one row per exposed element and one per element declared in it, so an element the view exposes in its own right beside its owner (`expose P::**`, a requirement table exposing a requirement and the one nested in it) was listed twice: under its owner and as a row of its own. A member the view exposes is now left out of its owner's rows and keeps only its own.

- The runtime showcase's `LunarMission` holds the landing on an `accept` of a `GoForDescent` signal before the descent burn, so `apollo11`'s performed mission waits in lunar orbit for mission control's GO instead of completing as the object is created; the browser REPL walkthrough "Step through Apollo 11" attaches to that waiting performance, shows the parked token, sends the GO with `%send` and steps it home. `apollo8` still fails the landing budget and never waits.

- **A SysML v1 Operation migrates to its block's composite `action <op> { … }` usage, and every call chains to it.** The migrator no longer synthesizes an `action def <Op>` plus an `action <op> : <Op>;` feature: the usage itself carries the parameters, the pre/postconditions as `assert constraint` and the method as body, keeping a «DirectedFeature» direction. A CallOperationAction is written `perform action <call> ::> <target>.<op>` for `this`, features of `this` and parts a port connects to; a call on an object handed in or created is an action usage whose `in <pin> : <Block>` parameter a nested perform chains through, its pins bound to the operation's parameters by position (a pin no parameter pairs with is reported, not declared; a loop such a pin decides or feeds a variable of is written body-once, saying so). An operation whose method a state runs, or acts on another block through its ports, keeps its `action def` with a context parameter, which the block's `action <op> : <Op>` usage binds to the object. The OMG mapping's `perform action <op>` on the block is not written because it is one performance the object enacts, which a call would join rather than invoke; the migration reference says so.

- The order-dependence marker now follows the data: a value computed from an order-dependent one stands on the same choice points and prints with the same marker — `y = 2 (order-dependent; 2 choice points — explore shows the set)` after `assign y := x + 1` where two unordered writers wrote `x` — through assignments, the guards of decisions, `if` and loop conditions and transitions (what the selected branch writes stands on what the guard read), a performed action's inputs and outputs, the payload a `send` carries to the `accept` that binds it, and a state machine's effects. A value nothing order-dependent reached stays bare, a merge or join adds no dependence, and a write that reads nothing order-dependent clears the mark of what it rewrites. The marker is the same typed field on every surface (the CLI and REPL, the trace, `order_dependent` over gRPC/Connect, the Python and Node clients, the MCP `execute_action` and `execute_state` tools); the `choice-point` diagnostic's `order_dependent` list still names what the choice itself touched. A performed node's pins report as `node.pin` (`doIt.x`), each performance holding its own, and an invocation's written arguments (`Add(x = 5)`) are what its input pins stand on. Conformance cases state derived marks as `orderDependent`.

- **Pull-request CI runs in two tiers.** A draft pull request runs the quick tier: the binaries, the Go suite without `-race` in three shards (`make test-quick`), the download-free static checks, the WebAssembly, Xpect and conformance gates, and only the clients and docs whose own paths changed (`CI_TIER=quick scripts/ci-changed-areas.sh`). Every push to a non-draft pull request, marking one ready for review, or the label `ci:full` runs the full tier, the whole suite as before. Both tiers report through the one `Build and test` check.

- **The two `sysml` builds are named after the two halves of the Runtime Environment and Development Kit.** The default build with every feature group is the DK (Development Kit) build; the build without the optional and developer-only groups, formerly the "production" build, is the RE (Runtime Environment) build: `-tags sysml_re` replaces `-tags sysml_prod`, `make build-re` and `make build-wasm-re` replace `make build-prod` and `make build-wasm-prod`, and they write `bin/re/sysml` and `bin/wasm/{js,wasip1}/re/sysml.wasm`. The binary is named `sysml` in both builds; RE and DK are names of the packages, not of the executables. What each build links is unchanged, as are the per-group `sysml_no<group>` tags.

- A rendering now draws the `«keyword»` line over each node's name by default (`«part def» GUI`, not `GUI`), the way the SysML v2 notation's examples, the pilot visualizer and SysON draw their nodes; `view.DefaultShown` is port symbols, compartments and keywords. `-render-show`, `show` and a document diagram's `show` attribute still add to that default, `all` still names every element, and a rendering specializing a standard `Views` rendering with `attribute showKeywords : Boolean = false;` still leaves the keywords out. The VS Code diagram panel stacks the line the way the notation and the other forms do, the `«keyword»` over the name rather than under it, and styles each label line by what it is rather than by its position, so a detail line under a node drawn without keywords is no longer styled as one.

- A SysML v1 migration writes a root `uml:Model` as `package <ModelName> { … }`, as the OMG
  transformation maps it, instead of writing its members at the file's top level. Qualified
  names are written from that package, `$::` staying only where a member still shadows the
  global name; an unnamed Model is named after the file; a member of the Model named like a
  standard library root package (`Requirements`, `Views`, …) keeps its name, only a root
  declaration being renamed; the report's Model row reads `written as package <name>`.

- `internal/exec/runtime` no longer exports the identifiers only its own files use: 325
  functions, methods, types, constants and variables that nothing outside the package
  referenced are unexported, and nine methods nothing called at all are removed. The API
  the CLI, REPL, gRPC service, LSP, analysis, document and code-generation packages consume,
  the identifiers `docs/reference/api.md` documents, and those other packages' tests exercise
  the runtime through keep their names.

- The `internal/exec/runtime` tests build their hand-made action graphs, evaluated attributes, constraint packages and classifier-behavior contexts through shared helpers (`actionSymbol`, `succession`, `evalAttribute`, `constraintPackage`, `behaviorContext`, `mustInstantiate`) instead of repeating the setup in each test; the cases and their assertions are unchanged.

- **The SonarCloud coverage denominator no longer counts code no test can run.** `sonar.coverage.exclusions` now also names the pilot API JSON exporter (`scripts/pilot-json-exporter`, a Java program run inside the pinned pilot's JVM), the Flexo interop gate's live-stack wrappers (`tools/referee/flexo/stack.go`, each one request to a running Flexo MMS stack) and the Homebrew formula template (`packaging/homebrew/Formula/opensysml.rb`, for which `brew test` writes no coverage), each with its reason beside it; every rule still applies to them, and files that mix pure logic with live calls stay measured.

- CI now reports to SonarCloud the coverage of the SysON backend and Magic MDK plugin tests (JaCoCo), of the VS Code, JupyterLab and sysml-mcp npm package tests (lcov; new `make vscode-coverage`, `jupyterlab-coverage` and `mcp-npm-coverage` targets), and of the LLM bench's tests, which the coverage job ran without `bin/sysml` and so skipped; the job now builds it and requires it.

- **The SonarCloud coverage gate no longer counts the PowerShell installer.** `install.ps1` and its fixture `scripts/install-test.ps1` run under pwsh in CI but produce no coverage report, so every line of them counted as uncovered new code and failed the `develop` quality gate after a change to either.

- The runtime robustness suite is one `robustness_<feature>_test.go` file per feature: the
  cases of the former single `TestRuntimeRobustness` registry in `robustness_test.go` now run
  under `TestRuntimeRobustness<Feature>` functions beside the newer per-feature files, and
  `robustness_test.go` keeps only the shared helpers and fixtures. The documentation pointers
  follow the cases to their files.

- Strict conformance (`-strict`, the REPL's `%strict`, the editor's `strictConformance`, the service's `strict_conformance`) now reports a namespace whose memberships are indistinguishable as an error: `Duplicate of other owned member name`, `Duplicate of owned member name`, `Duplicate of other alias name`, the short-name variant and `Duplicate of inherited member name` (KerML `validateNamespaceDistinguishibility`), so `sysml -validate -strict` exits non-zero on `part def A; part def A;`. The default mode keeps them warnings, and `Duplicate of imported member name` stays a warning in every mode, since KerML 7.2.5.4 hides the colliding names rather than leaving the namespace ill-formed.

- The `sysml-syntax` js/wasm build no longer links `regexp`: the build-info version matchers are hand-written, which saves about 124 KB gzipped (8%). Its gzip budget moves from 1,540,000 to 1,500,000 bytes, about 10% over the measured size, while adding the resolver or the standard library still trips it; `regexp` joins the dependencies the syntax command is checked not to link.

- Every SysML usage and KerML feature now directly specializes the base library usage its kind implies (`Parts::parts`, `Items::items`, `Ports::ports`, …, `Links::links`, `Base::things` for a usage of no kind), as the `check*UsageSpecialization` clauses and KerML `checkConnectorSpecialization` state: `istype`, `Conforms` and `DirectSupertypes` report the base usage as a general, while the usage's types stay those of the kind's base definition, so `that`, parameter and role redefinitions, multiplicities and runtime classification are unchanged. A feature typed by a metaclass subsets `Objects::objects` rather than `Metaobjects::metaobjects`, a feature typed by a datatype subsets `Base::dataValues`, a KerML `datatype` or `function` declaration subsets no base feature, and an individual occurrence usage subsets `Occurrences::occurrences` beside being typed by `Occurrences::Life`, which is now its feature type rather than `Occurrences::Occurrence`. A library record names the base usages a symbol's kind implied among its supertypes, so a model loaded from records tells them from the declared ones.

- **A migrated use case declares its actors.** The SysML v1 migration writes each actor association of a use case as an `actor` parameter nested in the `use case` usage, after its `subject` (written as `subject;` when the use case has actors but no subject, since v2 requires the subject to come first), subsetting the actor's one package-level `part` usage with the end's multiplicity — `actor operator :> Operator;`, `actor attendant [0..1] :> Attendant;` — so the use case reaches its actor in v2 semantics. The actor stays one element: no `part def` and no second usage, so a tool draws it once. The connection written for the association joins the parameter, `connection 'Operator to Control Instrument' connect Operator to 'Control Instrument'.operator;`; an actor end the use case owns is that parameter rather than a second one (named after the actor when it is anonymous); a parameter named after the actor rather than an association end is marked `MigrationMetadata::SynthesizedName` (a comment under `-strict`), and two associations from one actor get distinct parameters. A named association's connection, typed by its `connection def`, still joins the use case usage, as the def's end subsets it. Use-case diagrams draw a use case's subject and actor parameters as pins on its node, as an analysis case's parameters are drawn, so an actor is one node and its association one line ending at the use case. In an interconnection rendering the actor parameter is a pin on the use case's node, drawn with `-render-show parameter-symbols` as a case's other parameters are, and the connection from the actor's part to the use case is drawn whether or not the pin is; a rendering exposing the use case alone draws neither, since the actor is the part outside it.

- **A requirement with no `require constraint` is satisfied, not unevaluable.** A requirement check's result is `allTrue(assumptions()) implies allTrue(constraints())` (SysML v2 §9.2.11 `Requirements::RequirementConstraintCheck`), and `allTrue` over an empty sequence is true (KerML §9.2.3 `ControlFunctions::allTrue`); a viewpoint or concern is such a check (§9.2.17 `Views::ViewpointCheck :> RequirementCheck`). `-satisfy`, `%satisfy`, `-requirement`, `%requirement`, `%view` and the gRPC checks now report a `satisfy` of a requirement, concern or viewpoint that states no required constraint as `✓ … holds`, with the standing naming why (`vacuous: viewpoint reading states no required constraint, so it holds vacuously`), where they reported `? … could not be evaluated … no condition to evaluate` and exited 2; `assert not satisfy` of such a requirement is violated, and `%check` of it is satisfiable by every assignment, while `%check` of a part asserting `not satisfy` of it is unsatisfiable. A constraint stating no condition still has no verdict, a denial or violation query of a condition-less requirement still has nothing to deny, and a `require r` naming the requirement being checked is now reported as the cycle it is instead of being read as no condition.

- A variation is always abstract (SysML v2 §7.6.7, `validateDefinitionVariationIsAbstract`, `validateUsageVariationIsAbstract`), so api-json and Turtle now state `isAbstract: true` for every variation definition, variation usage and enumeration definition, as they already stated `isVariation`; Turtle read back as notation writes `variation` alone, never `abstract variation`.
- The reflective `featuringType` of a variant of a variation usage is that usage's featuringTypes rather than the usage itself (`checkUsageVariationUsageTypeFeaturing`): a variant is an owned member, not an owned feature, of its variation.

- `variation-member-not-variant` now reports an `objective` owned by a variation usage, as it already did in a variation definition: SysML v2.1 §8.3.6.4 `validateUsageVariationOwnedFeatureMembership` (`isVariation implies ownedFeatureMembership->isEmpty()`) admits no owned feature membership, and an `ObjectiveMembership` is one. The pilot exempts it (its own `TODO`), so this is stricter than the pilot; the validation census records `validateUsageVariationMembership` as faithful and the pilot rejection corpus gains the objective case as an OpenSysML-only rejection.

- The gzipped size budget of the validation-only `sysml-core` WebAssembly build is 6,500,000 bytes, up from 6,300,000: the check and view passes it links had grown past the original figure, so the gate now records the size it was set against and leaves headroom for that growth.

### Fixed

- An abstract definition or usage is now named with its written `abstract` keyword in every rendering — `«abstract part def»`, `«abstract part»`, `«abstract datatype»` on tree, interconnection and mixed diagrams in text, Mermaid, DOT, D2, PlantUML and the editor's diagram payload, in the kind column of element tables, in hover, completion, REPL kinds and solver verdicts (`Abstract part fam permits …`) — where it was drawn as a plain `«part def»`/`«part»`, indistinguishable from a concrete element (SysML v2.1 §7.6.2, §7.6.3, Table 4). A variation keeps `«variation …»` alone, since the specification says a variation is always abstract and the keyword is not written on it (§7.6.7). The kind keyword behind a modifier still decides what a kind-keyed rule reads: an abstract KerML classifier such as `Base::Anything` still types a part, an abstract definition is still drawn in the definition shape and fill, and a moved `abstract part` is still admitted where a part is.

- `accept after d` / `accept at t` where `d` or `t` is an `in` parameter, a parameter bound to a literal or an attribute initialised from one no longer fails with "must be constant, got sequence": a feature's value is a sequence, so one holding a single duration is read as that duration when the accept is armed, while one holding none or several is a typed error naming the count; a continuous dynamics' `timeStep` bound to a parameter steps the clock and scales the state's rate the same way.

- An `accept` in a `while`, `loop`, `for` or `if` body parks the body's performance until a matching message arrives, as a top-level accept does, and is a deadlock only once nothing can send to it; the compliance row, conformance cases (an accept two blocks deep, one in a loop body of an action a part performs woken by a sibling object's sends) and a robustness suite for the parked body's failure modes now record that.

- An action flow view whose `Layout` positions a body's `start` that no succession leaves now draws that start as an initial node at the `Layout`'s position instead of dropping the `Layout` with a `diagram-layout-unplaced` warning; renderings without such a `Layout` are unchanged.

- A positional argument to a case or requirement that declares an `actor` before an `in` parameter binds that input, not the slot after it: actors bind by name only and take no positional slot, so positional arguments fall on the other parameters in declaration order, the positional arity leaves actors out (a surplus argument is still refused) — in the call, in a function value's arity and in the statement-order memo key alike — and the compiled calc tier declines a shape with actors; a conformance case, a robustness suite and a compliance row record the rule.

- `ExecuteAction`, `ExecuteState` and `CheckBehavior` with a `performerSymbolId`, and the REPL's `%action <action> <object>` (so `sysml -action "<action> <object>"` with or without `-advance`), run the performance the object already performs of the action, or the machine it exhibits, rather than starting a second one beside it when the run is bounded by `advance`: the outputs, performer attributes and occurrence are the object's own in both modes, inputs for a performed action are refused as a run to completion refuses them, and an object performing the action under several usages is refused as ambiguous.

- **An analysis case states one objective, and a declared `perform`/`exhibit` is checked for what it references.** `analysis def A { objective o1; objective o2; }` and `analysis a : A { objective p1; objective p2; }` report `Only one objective is allowed.` on the second objective, as every other case kind did and as the pilot does (SysML 8.3.22.2–3); `%optimize` improves a further objective lexicographically when it is the objective of an analysis nested in the case (`analysis thenMargin { objective widest : MaximizeObjective { ... } }`), the case's own first and then each nested analysis's in declaration order, and the solver fixtures and the REPL reference say so. `exhibit state e references p;` and `perform action f references p;` report `Must reference a state.` / `Must reference an action.` at `p` when `p` is no state or action, as `exhibit p;` / `perform p;` already did (8.3.18.2, 8.3.17.14); an unresolved `p` stays name resolution's. The census moves the four rows to faithful (✅ 152 → 156, ⚠️ 12 → 8), and the rejection oracle gains four cases (319, 310 rejected by both).

- The API JSON element form now states what the OMG API JSON shape carries for three authored facts an external reader otherwise misreads: `isComposite` is written `true` or `false` on every feature and usage (`part wheel` reads back composite, `ref item cargo` keeps its `ref`), a `MetadataAccessExpression` owns the `Membership` of the element it reads and derives `referencedElement` from it, and the standard library elements a model references are bare `@id` references rather than ownerless `isLibraryElement` stubs a reader would take for new roots. Turtle still names the library elements it references; older documents with stubs, with only `referencedElement`, or with no false flags read as before.

- API JSON feature-chain links now use one scoped resolver for aggregate chains and their `FeatureChaining` targets; unresolved links omit the derived aggregate while preserving owned relationships and their graph values in `sysx:mappingValues`.
- API JSON omits unresolved collection-valued references rather than writing empty collections, and preserves expression-namespace targets across feature-chain links.

- API JSON preserves ordered, repeated `Feature::chainingFeature` links and omits a chain as a whole when any link is unresolved.

- API-JSON import mapping-name reconstruction decodes escaped final qualified-name segments and combines owned relationships, redefinitions, and memberships in graph order.

- API JSON feature chains now resolve typed members of library features that the graph represents only by reference.

- API JSON imports preserve unnamed member names when unrelated relationships are reordered.

- API JSON preserves unnamed member names across metadata annotations and structural relationships.

- API JSON name resolution now considers declared and membership short names, and later feature-chain links resolve only within the preceding feature's type and supertypes.

- Omit unresolved API-JSON object references instead of writing `@ref` values, and preserve source graph values through a consumed `sysx:mappingValues` sidecar.

- **An association that only backs a value property is not a `connection def`.** MagicDraw backs each «ConstraintParameter» port, and a value property, with an anonymous association between the property and an end the association owns, typed by the property's owner. Such an association was written as a `connection def` named after the end types (`GainToReal`), approximated, one per parameter; it links nothing, since a value is not a referent, so it is now skipped as notation only with the reason "the association only backs `Block::prop`, which is written as a feature", its owned end with it. The same shape backing a `part` property is a composition and is still written as a `connection def`. A block definition diagram line from the block to the value type is drawn by the tree rendering as the attribute's edge, and the line's `DiagramLayout::Route` is written about the attribute.

- Analysing a set of documents (`sysml -validate`, the gRPC `ParseSources` call, a workspace's
  batch) now settles each namespace's import collisions (KerML 7.2.5.4, the hidden colliding
  imports) once for the batch rather than once per document, and skips the route check for an
  entry re-exported through namespaces none of which hides anything. A chain of documents each
  re-exporting the previous one (`public import P::*`) was analysed in time quadratic in the
  chain's depth: 200 such documents took about 270 s and now take about 15–20 s. Each
  document's interface record still depends on everything the shared decision read.

- A `member binding` declared in an end feature's body ahead of its cross feature is no longer taken as the owned cross feature (KerML 1.1 `Feature::ownedCrossFeature` rejects binding connectors and metadata features), so a mistyped cross feature after it is now reported with `cross-feature-type` as it is without the binding.

- A bound member that reads a feature chain through a package-level usage whose member's count the model leaves open (`attribute boundLimit : Real = req.some.limit;` over `part some : Sub[1..*];`) is `<undetermined>` on an instantiated holder, as the chain read directly is, instead of the value of a lower-bound element nothing in the model picked; so is a derived feature of such a usage read at model level (`req.own` over `own = some.limit`). A count a binding (`= (a, b)`), a redefinition (`:>> some[2]`) or a fixed multiplicity closes still evaluates, and an instantiated object keeps the lower bound it materializes.

- Interconnection diagrams no longer draw the boundary symbols of a positioned node (the pins of
  its `actor`, `subject` and `stakeholder` parameters and of its ports, and the ports it declares)
  on top of one another when their routes end at one point, or a route ends where an unconnected
  port's pin is drawn: the symbols of a side are spread along it, in the order their routes come
  from, each route ending on its own symbol. Symbols on one side keep clear of a corner a
  neighbouring side's symbol holds; a run too wide for the stretch left runs past the far corner,
  or is centred between two held corners. A lone symbol stays where its route places it.
- The SysML v1 migrator marks the connection names it makes up for an actor's anonymous
  association to a use case (`'Preview Image to Operator'`) as `MigrationMetadata::SynthesizedName`,
  so diagrams no longer label those lines; a named v1 association keeps its label.

- A calculation's result parameter is held in the shape its declared multiplicity gives it, as an action parameter is: `return : Real[0..*] = x` and `return : Real[2]` hold a sequence on every surface (REPL, `-json`, trace, gRPC and the clients, analysis and verification cases), an explicit `[1]` or `[0..1]` the bare value, and a result stating no multiplicity the one-element sequence its effective `[0..*]` gives it, as a bare action parameter already did (SysML v2 §7.6.3 implies no `[1..1]` for a reference usage; §7.19.2 has a `return` redefine the `[0..*]` result it inherits; KerML §7.3.4.2 gives it only the default subsetting of `things`). A verdict, selected alternative or vector a case reads from such a result is read from the sequence's one element, so a `VerdictKind[0..*]` result no longer leaves the case inconclusive. A parameter declared `[0..*]` holds one bound value as a one-element sequence, which a condition (`if`, `while`), `==` and `===` read as the value it holds, as the other operators already did. The compiled tiers follow: the scalar fast path declines a parameter or result declared to hold more than one value, and a generated Go or C program holds its collection parameters and result in the same shape and reads a one-element collection the same way, so every tier answers alike.

- Recognize the hostless `http:///SysML/….ecore` namespaces Eclipse UML2 gives the
  SysML profile's packages, as Cameo's "Eclipse UML2 (v5.x) XMI" export writes them,
  when migrating SysML v1 XMI: a standard Block or Requirement applied through them
  is classified like one from the OMG or Papyrus namespaces. Other roots, hosts, or
  a namespace with a query or fragment remain distinct from standard SysML.

- Reading a member of a calc, analysis or verification case usage through the usage answers every feature the usage has, not only its outputs: the subject, an actor or an `in` parameter as the usage binds it (`p1.v.s`, `VerPkg::vp.s`, `holder.check.who.id`, `c1.x`), a local or part the body declares (`check.depth`, `check.gear.id`), and the objective and the other members read through the case's occurrence (`scaled.fits.result`) — on an instantiated owner and at package level alike — instead of `unknown output`. A subject, actor or input nothing binds reads `<undetermined>` whatever its lower bound (`in vals : Integer[0..*]` included; an unbound actor or input leaves the case running); return-parameter reads are unchanged. Actors are parameters of analysis and verification cases, bound by name as the subject is, and one bound to several values keeps the multiplicity of the actor it redefines. An actor a usage renames at its position (`actor crew = op` over the definition's `actor pilot`) binds the actor it redefines, so the body's reads of `pilot` see the value. A case usage declared on a part has one occurrence per owner object: two parts' `check.gear` are two objects, each with its owner's values, instead of one shared object.

- **The compliance census reads a row's status from its `Status` column.** `scripts/mkdocs_census.py` and `tools/census/doccounts` counted a table row only when the whole row carried exactly one status marker, so a rule row whose notes mentioned another status was silently left out of the published census, and a marker mentioned before the status cell could be attributed in its place. Both counters now take the status from the table's `Status` column, split cells as the site's renderer does (a pipe inside a code span or escaped does not split), and refuse — naming the line — a row whose status cell states no status or two, a row a blank line separates from its table, and a status marker in a table without a `Status` column. Ten rows of `docs/project/spec-compliance.md` that the census had dropped are counted again, and the rows that named two statuses in one cell or wrapped over several lines now state one.

- **A CI step that installs packages with apt fails within minutes when the Ubuntu mirror stalls, instead of hanging until the job's limit.** `apt-get update` against `archive.ubuntu.com` sat without a byte for an hour on the `Go race tests (model)` shard and for close to four hours on `MATLAB/Octave client tests`, so no test ran and the pull request showed red. Every workflow step that runs apt now goes through `scripts/ci-apt-install.sh`, which bounds each fetch (`Acquire::http::Timeout=30`, `Acquire::Retries=3`), makes a failed index fetch fail `apt-get update` (`APT::Update::Error-Mode=any`) rather than fall back to stale lists, bounds `apt-get update` to three minutes and the whole fetch to eight, retrying three times, downloads the archives before `dpkg` runs in five-minute attempts that resume one another and, on GitHub's runners, drop the mirror that failed an attempt from the runner's mirror list so the next attempt asks the next mirror; each such step also carries a ten-minute `timeout-minutes`, so the script reports its own failure before the step is cancelled. The Octave job, whose 195 MB of archives a trickling mirror held for twelve minutes even so, additionally keeps the archives it installed from in the Actions cache and seeds apt from them on the next run, so the mirror is only asked for what changed. What the jobs install and test is unchanged.

- **The command reference spells `sysml`'s flags as `-help` does.** The flag table in `docs/reference/cli.md` wrote every flag with two dashes (`--migration-results`, `--render-show`, …) where the usage, the manual page and the rest of the reference write one (`-migration-results`); the table now matches. Both spellings are accepted on the command line, as before.

- `col#(i)` over an object of an `OrderedCollection` (`attribute os : OrderedSet { :>> elements : Integer = (3, 5, 7); }`) now answers the element at `i` of `col.elements`, as `CollectionFunctions::'#'` declares and the qualified call `CollectionFunctions::'#'(os, 1)` already did: `os#(1)` is `3` where it was the `os` object itself (the one-element sequence the object is under `BaseFunctions::'#'`). A position past the elements is an index-out-of-range error; an `Array` keeps its `'array#'` positions and an unordered collection, which the function does not take, is still positioned as the one value it is.

- A compartment row for a member with no name or type to show (an unnamed attribute with typings hidden) reads as the member's keyword, as a head with nothing else to show does, instead of an empty line that took a row of the stated box and left the compartment's name written over nothing.

- `%configure`, `%solve`, `%check` and `%explain` reports (and the MCP `configure` tool) now write every name as the notation does: the value a variation point is fixed or chosen at, the values named in a conflict, the condition an `Against` line quotes and the subject of the verdict (a part, a requirement, or a satisfaction assertion with the names it writes) are quoted where the notation quotes them (`'Cfg Space'::'the family'::engine = 'Cfg Space'::'Engine Choices'::'4cyl'`), as the selection lines already were; a name that needs no quotes is written as before.

- A conformance check against a named library type — the ones every redefinition's derived
  `isVariable`/`isConstant` ask through `Usage::mayTimeVary` (`Occurrences::Occurrence`,
  `Links::SelfLink`, `Occurrences::HappensLink`, `Actions::Action`) — compares the candidate and
  its memoized supertypes to the one element registered under the name instead of building the
  qualified name of each supertype on every check, which the 0.11.0 performance record found
  costing the connector-dense `ImportValues` benchmarks 29% more allocations (135.5 k → 175.2 k
  per load) and a synthetic document's analysis 10% more. A name no or several elements carry
  is still compared by name.

- A connection usage whose ends are declared in its body and attached by reference subsetting (`#multicausation connection gate { end #cause :> causes ::> a; … }`, SysML v2 §7.13.2) is a connector object like one with a `connect` clause: `-instantiate` no longer reports `gate.causes: multiplicity violation: 0 value(s)` on `CauseAndEffect::Multicausation`, since each attached end's value is a value of every feature the end subsets (KerML 1.0 §7.3.4.4), named or not, and a `#cause`/`#effect` end subsets `causes`/`effects` through its semantic metadata — whose `baseType` the library binds with an `as` cast, now read like a `meta` cast; such a connection's ends also appear in relationship and interconnection views.

- A constructor argument that binds a feature whose value is written with `=` — `new A(x = 2)` or `new A(2)` under `item def A { attribute x : Integer = 1; }` — is now reported as overriding that binding value (KerML 8.3.4.10.2), with the same message and `default =` hint the redefinition form `attribute :>> x = 2` already draws; a `default =` value and an unvalued feature still take the argument.

- The RDF and API JSON writers own a declaration head's relationships in the order the notation spells them: a usage's typings, specializations and multiplicity come first in `ownedRelationship` as written (`:>> x : T = v` owns its Redefinition, then its FeatureTyping, then its FeatureValue), ahead of the value and the body members, and a definition's Subclassification ahead of its members; `ownedSpecialization` and the other owned-relationship lists follow the same order. `Element::ownedRelationship` is ordered (KerML 8.3.2.1.2) and the textual notation adds each owned relationship as it parses the clause (KerML 8.2.1, SysML 8.2). The reader spells a head back in the order the graph owns its relationships, the multiplicity part included, instead of a fixed clause order. Where a head relates one target under two clauses that materialize as the same class (`subsets x specializes x`), the writer states each element's keyword as `sysx:headClause`, so the two come back apart and in order under either id form; a multiplicity no membership places is written once, with the first typing clause.

- **Resolving names through an import cycle no longer slows with every lookup.** Each document a resolution came to depend on was appended to every read capture open at the time, once per dependency note, so a shared collision set computed inside an import cycle gathered a list that grew with the number of lookups and was sorted afterwards. A capture now notes a document once per run of notes; the dependencies it carries are the same.

- Instantiating a `ShapeItems::RightCircularCone` or `RightCircularCylinder` no longer reports `xoffset.dimensions: no value for feature mRef` or `edges.length: no value for feature Circle::radius`. A body valuing one feature of a redefined attribute (`:>> xoffset { :>> num = 0; }`) governs over the inherited `default` only for that feature, so the default supplies the rest (`mRef`) — also when the redefined declaration is a package-level attribute, as the Geometry library writes its offsets — and a `ScalarQuantityValue` object stating its `num` and `mRef` reads as that quantity (`0 [m]`). The cone's and cylinder's base circle radius is a feature the library gives no value — it binds the semi-axes from the radius, never the radius from them (`docs/project/omg-issues.md`) — so the check passes over the edge length derived from it as it passes over any unset feature, and reading it is the typed no-value error naming `Circle::radius`.

- A verification case declared inside a `part def` now runs as a feature of an object of that definition, so its body reads the owner's features: `-validate=<object>`, `-requirement`, `-satisfy` and the gRPC `validate_instance`/`verify_requirement`/`verify_satisfaction` report its verdict once per object of the definition the check is about, naming the object, and `-analysis "<Def>::<case> <object>"` (and `run_analysis` with a subject) runs it on the object given as the case's owner rather than refusing it for declaring no subject; with no object it runs on the single object of the definition the run holds and refuses to choose between several. A `satisfy` without `by` that a definition states is likewise checked on the object of that definition.

- The VS Code diagram panel marks a focused node's neighbourhood from the rendering it has just drawn: after an edit moved a relationship, the new canvas was marked with the old rendering's neighbours until the layout settled again.
- The panel's Relations list counts the edges the canvas has — those at drawn nodes, a kind the filter hides included — and no longer lists or counts an edge at a node folded under a collapsed owner, which the canvas never draws.

- The VS Code diagram panel sorts its relationship-kind list with an explicit code-unit comparator, so the order is the same in every locale.

- The VS Code diagram panel applies a change of `opensysml.diagram.routing` made while a drag is in progress once the drag settles — a drag's preview and a pan's scroll position stay as they were until then — instead of keeping the routes it started on until a later render.
- A drag's preview in the VS Code diagram panel is drawn on the canvas the drag began on, so a preview that draws less no longer clamps the scroll offsets, and a cancelled drag comes back where it was.

- Mark wide and dense tables by header-cell count and select them by class in the PDF stylesheets, preventing large documents from timing out during WeasyPrint layout.

- **The documentation site builds again.** `docs-requirements.txt` pins `pymdown-extensions` to 12.1: the unpinned 12.2 release changed its `Highlight` constructor, and `mkdocstrings` 1.0.6 fails on every page with a Python API block (`Highlight.__init__() missing 1 required positional argument: 'md'`), which broke `make docs` and the Pages workflow.

- A document query can project what a row feature's own value binds: the built-in property `valuation` (KerML `Feature::valuation`) reads the declared or redefined constant — a quantity with its unit, an enumeration literal, a string — or the expression's value evaluated, in `Project` `properties`, `Column(path = …)` and `OrderBy`; a usage bound to nothing or a row that is not a feature — an object row included — is an empty cell. Before, the projectable properties were metaclass properties and member features *of* the row only, so a table over an interface definition's attributes could list names and types but never the bound numbers (`unknown property value`).
- A metadata def in a `<metadata def>::<feature>` property string or `Column(path = …)` is named as `WhereMetadata` names one — by qualified name or by a name unique over the model — so a query beside `metadata def Verification` reads `Verification::method` without spelling the def's full path; an ambiguous short name stays an unknown property. The planner accepts the same spellings as the executor.
- A parameter that writes no value and redefines nothing explicitly reads the binding of the parameter it redefines by position: `action focus : Focus { in level; }` over `action def Focus { in level : Integer = 3; }` gives `level` the value `3` in a document query, as a `properties` member of `focus` and as the parameter row's `valuation`; before, only an explicit `:>>` carried the binding and the cell was empty.

- Enumerating what a wildcard import brings — the references index, rename, completion and
  every unqualified lookup that imports feed — no longer pays, per member, for the hidden-import
  machinery when nothing is hidden: a member re-exported through another namespace is checked
  against the memoized `hidesBelow` before any route is walked, its qualified name is compared
  segment by segment instead of being built, and the routes gating it are visited in place
  instead of copied into a list the lookup then reads once. The language server's cold
  references query and warm rename of the 0.11.0 performance record go from 6.28 M and 12.4 M
  allocations to 1.94 M and 3.74 M; a member re-exported through a second wildcard import costs
  2 allocations to enumerate instead of 6.

- `alias a of b`, `featuring f featured by T` and a parenthesised parameter list on a declaration (`behavior setX(c : C) { ... }`) are each reported once and the member read as meant, so the rest of the body still parses; the alias in a type body, the relationship body on `featuring ... by`, and a `language` representation without `rep` have golden fixtures, as the KerML grammar admits them.

- `%eval` of a usage declared in another document than the prompt's scope reads the object
  already materialized for it (the one a behavior was performed on) instead of materializing a
  second one holding the declaration's defaults.

- An attribute of an exhibited state machine assigned through the exhibit usage by another performance of the exhibiting part (`assign g.missDistance := …` from a `perform action` beside `exhibit state g`) now reaches the running machine: the machine's data mirrors the one performance the usage names, so a change trigger on that attribute (`accept when missDistance < …`) fires on the write instead of the machine reading its initial value for the rest of the run; an attribute the exhibit usage binds to the part's (`:>> missDistance = Flight::missDistance`) behaves the same.

- `ExecuteState` (and every runtime caller performing a machine on an object) given a path to the exhibited usage itself (`scenario.tug.fdir`, the performer the CLI's `-state` names) now runs the machine the owning part exhibits, as the CLI does, instead of starting a second performance on the usage's occurrence beside it — the second copy received no port traffic, so a timer-driven safe-mode request appeared alongside the real run. A `performers` object of a runtime conformance case may name such a path too.

- The scheduled "Extension libraries upstream" workflow runs the suite the way the PR workflow does: it provisions the pinned corpora, the Reference-FMUs and Node 24 (through the shared `.github/actions/corpora` step the PR workflow now uses too), sets the matching `OPENSYSML_REQUIRE_*` variables, and gives each package the quick tier's 45-minute timeout, so its verdict on an upstream library change is about the libraries rather than about an absent corpus, the runner's Node or a timeout. `TestKerMLFeatureMetaclassClassification` skips when the pilot corpora are absent and fails only when they are required, like the other gates over those corpora.

- A reference into one of OpenSysML's own libraries (`DocumentQueries`, `MOSA`, `OOSEM`, …) — a redefined or typing feature, a metadata or view usage's type, a feature reference's referent, an imported namespace — is written by element id (`{"@id": …}` in the API element form, an IRI with a library stub in Turtle) instead of a `{"@ref": "name"}` name reference, since every element has an `elementId` (KerML 8.3.2.1.2). The extension libraries' elements carry fixed, deterministic ids derived as the standard library's normative ids are, under the libraries' upstream URL rather than an OMG specification URL; hover reports one as `(extension, OpenSysML)`.

- An element filter (`filter @T;`, `import P::*[@T]`, `expose X::**[@T]`) and a classification (`@T`, `@@T`) now test conformance against the Type element the name resolves to, through its specialization closure, rather than against the qualified name: a type named through an `alias` or a re-exporting import selects what the aliased Type annotates, and a distinct Type that shares the qualified name (the same package opened in another document) selects nothing it does not annotate. The name alone decides only when it resolves to no element, or between two library copies of one declaration (a workspace namesake of a library type is not that type); likewise `Base::Anything` is the top type every type conforms to only as the library declares it, not as a workspace type of that name.

- A view filtered by a metaclass (`filter @PartUsage;`) rendered nothing, with no diagnostic, when the workspace held the standard library twice — two copies of `sysml.library` under the folder, or an `OPENSYSML_LIBRARY_PATH` tree containing two — because a metaclass declared by more than one document counted as not declared at all and the filter then rejected every exposed element; the VS Code diagram panel showed a blank. Such a filter now classifies by the metaclass's name across the copies, as it already did when no library was loaded.

- A migrated block definition diagram keeps Cameo's routes for its generalization lines: the migrator writes each as a `DiagramLayout::Route` about the specific definition naming the general it is drawn to (`ref :>> general : Sup;`), the report counts them as written rather than "no v2 member", and the positioned tree steers the specialization edge to that general along the waypoints — one Route per general of a subtype with several, and a view's own Route when the subtype is drawn in more than one. The layout check reports a Route naming a general the view draws no edge to.

- `sysml -graphs` and the `ExportGraphs` RPC no longer panic with a nil pointer dereference on a `parallel` state definition (or a state usage exhibiting one): the vertices standing for its orthogonal regions sit at the top of the machine under no parent state, and the export now names and numbers them there instead of dereferencing the missing parent.

- Starting or joining a performed action on its performer with an input for a reference parameter the declaration binds (`perform action a : A { in ref :>> context = other; }`) is accepted when the input is that bound object, whether the performance has already started or is still held back by a succession order. A held performance used to accept only the performer itself, since it had no stored binding to compare against; the comparison now evaluates the declared feature value in the performer's scope, without releasing the held performance, and compares by sole element as the started case does. The performer itself is refused where the declaration binds another object, held or started alike. The gRPC conformance cases take a `performer_symbol_id` and `instance_id` inputs for `ExecuteAction`.

- **A hidden import is not inherited.** An imported membership that KerML 7.2.5.4 hides — one colliding with another import's name or with an owned name of the importing namespace — is no membership of that namespace, so a type specializing it no longer inherits the hidden member: `view v :> Introduction;`, where `Introduction` exposes two parts' `payload`, no longer draws `Duplicate of inherited member name 'payload'` at `v`. The `Duplicate of imported member name` warning on the import itself is unchanged.

- **Resolving names through an import cycle no longer re-walks the cycle for every lookup.** Whether anything is hidden below a namespace is settled by walking the namespaces its imports re-export from; inside a cycle of imports that walk meets a collision computation still in progress, cannot be kept, and was repeated by the next lookup through the same namespace. A walk cut that way is now remembered for as long as the computation that cut it runs, as the provisional collision sets already are, and answers the same, undecided, until then.

- `istype`, `@`, `hastype` and `as` judge an object by every type it inherits through the feature holding it (KerML 1.0 §7.3.4.3–§7.3.4.4), not its stated type alone: with `item front : Panel :> faces` in an `item def Rig :> Objects::StructuredSpaceObject`, `rig.front istype Objects::StructuredSpaceObject::StructuredSurface`, `rig.front istype Objects::Surface` and `rig.front @ Objects::StructuredSpaceObject::StructuredSurface` are `true` and `rig.front as Objects::Surface` yields the object, where each answered `false` or `()` before although the object already carried `StructuredSurface`'s `edges`. `hastype` names the direct types only (KerML 1.0 §7.4.9), so `rig.front hastype Panel` and `hastype StructuredSurface` are `true` and `hastype Objects::Surface` stays `false`, and a `Panel` held by nothing that subsets `faces` is still no `StructuredSurface`.

- The generals a declaration's kind implies (the base usage it subsets, the definition it is typed by) are derived once per symbol and memoized, as its other supertype queries are; every type query of a feature re-derived them, which made model execution and the CLI about 40 % slower.

- The features a feature redefines, directly or through them, are computed once per model, and a parameter's implicit redefinitions take its position from its owner's memoized parameter list instead of rebuilding it on every query; same redefinitions, fewer allocations per query — and per execution root frame, which asks for every feature it holds.

- The base feature a declaration's kind implies — the library usage it subsets, the `Base::things` fallback, and whether a declared specialization chain already reaches it — is looked up once per symbol and memoized with its other implied generals; every implied-generals and direct-supertype query re-walked the declared chain and re-resolved the library name, which made semantics-heavy workloads (editing a large model, validating a stress model) about 25 % slower than before usages subset their kind's base usage.

- **Resolving a name through an import no longer computes the import collisions of every namespace down the chain of imports.** Enumerating what a namespace import brings consulted the imported namespace's own collisions (the memberships KerML 7.2.5.4 hides) even when every member it reached was the namespace's own, which no collision can hide; over a workspace analyzed one document at a time that made the work quadratic in the length of the import chain. The collisions are now computed only where an import contributed a member.

- `ImportData` with `import_dry_run` now validates the model the last file would produce too, so a final file whose values make the model invalid is refused rather than previewed as a success; `CompareResults` answers `undecided`, with a note, for a configuration whose tool stored no observable and whose runs produced no number, instead of `holds` over nothing; the Julia client's `applied(::ImportResult)` is false for an import that left the model as it was — no value set, or only values it already had — which the service answers under the original hash.

- Invalidate cached import enumerations when the resolver's semantic model changes.

- An `include` whose target is a use case definition, or any other non-feature, is now rejected in default mode with `Must reference a use case.` and `includes target must be a usage, found useCaseDef` at the target's span, as the pilot validator reports; `perform` and `exhibit` of a definition draw the kind message the same way, and an alias is followed to its target. Includes of use case usages — direct, typed, with a body, by qualified name, via alias or inside a use case usage — stay clean.

- Prefix metadata between `individual` and `def` or the usage keyword (`individual #Tag def Flight :> Campaign;`, `individual #Tag part article : Article;`) now keeps the declaration an individual definition or usage with the metadata attached; it was parsed as a plain KerML class, so an occurrence definition could not be specialized and the individual usages typed by it were reported as not typed by an occurrence definition.

- An inherited guard, effect or assignment reads and writes the features its declaring scope resolves (KerML 1.0 §8.2.3.5): a specialization's feature that duplicates an inherited name without redefining it is held beside the general's under its owner-qualified key (`Base::go`), so the issue's `Derived` performance gives `c = 1`, each namespace's guard reads its own feature, and a redefining feature still takes the redefined one's place. The frame probes take the frame by pointer again, as the held-frame scan was tuned to.

- A usage that inherits a KerML type through subsetting or redefinition is judged by the same rule as one typed with it directly: a type its kind's standard-library base already specializes (such as `Base::Anything`) is redundant and no longer reported, while a KerML type of another metaclass (`attribute a : Objects::Object`) is now reported on the declaring usage as well as on the usages subsetting it. Flow usages now carry their implicit base `Flows::flows` (`Flows::messages` for a message, `Flows::successionFlows` for a succession flow).

- **`install.sh --version nightly` no longer refuses the bundle it just downloaded.** The nightly workflow listed the four `opensysml-<platform>.tar.gz` bundles twice in `SHA256SUMS.txt` (the glob that appended the Python sdist matched them too), so the installer's digest lookup printed the digest twice and the comparison failed with `does not match SHA256SUMS.txt` on a correct download; every nightly since the clients' snapshots were first published was affected, on Linux and macOS, for any `--tools` selection that includes `sysml` or `sysml-lsp` (tagged releases, `--os windows`, `install.ps1` and the tools published on their own were not). The workflow now names the Python distributions exactly and checks, before signing, that the manifest lists every asset once and that each is present with the digest it names; `install.sh` and `install.ps1` count a line repeated verbatim once and refuse a manifest that lists an asset with more than one digest.

- Interconnection renderings draw constraint usages and constraint defs as nodes, with their
  parameters — the `in`/`out`/`inout` attributes the constraint def declares and any the usage
  declares itself — as squares on the border labelled outside, the way a part's ports are drawn, so
  a SysML v1 parametric diagram migrated to a `Views::asInterconnectionDiagram` view shows its
  constraint properties with the bindings ending at their parameters instead of dropping each
  constraint with a "has no place in an interconnection rendering" notice and leaving the lines
  dangling. A `DiagramLayout::Layout` about a parameter (`about first::input` for one usage's,
  `about Law::input` for every usage's) places its square on the nearest side of the constraint's
  border, and the `port-symbols` notation element hides the squares with the ports' and attaches
  the bindings to the constraint itself. Mixed renderings draw constraints the same way.
- The SysML v1 migrator writes a connector's `DiagramLayout::Route` with its points run from the
  feature the connector's first written end names to its second's, reversing a line the diagram
  drew from the second end, so a rendering following the Route from `bind a = b`'s `a` follows the
  drawn line instead of collapsing it onto one end.

- A document whose annotation names an undeclared metadata type, or whose `about` metadata usage names undeclared elements, now has an interface record: the unresolved reference is the document's diagnostic, not a provisional supertype or an absent `about` usage, so the record is written and holds among its neighbours instead of being refused or written off as stale. The spec-examples root joins the incremental and interface-record differentials in `tests/model`.

- A Kernel Semantic Library collection the runtime tracks for every object (`timeEnclosedOccurrences`, `spaceTimeEnclosedOccurrences`, `sameLifeOccurrences`, `timeSlices`, `portions`, …) now unions the object with what the shape features subsetting it hold, in shape order, each object once: a part owning port `q1` reads `timeEnclosedOccurrences` as `[p, q1]` (Parts.sysml `ownedPorts :> timeEnclosedOccurrences`, KerML 1.0 §7.3.4.4), where it read `[p]` alone, and a model-level read through an open subsetter stays open rather than counted at its lower bound.

- Reading a Kernel Semantic Library collection of an object — `self.suboccurrences`, `self.subobjects`, `self.subperformances` and the other `[0..*]` members the Kernel tier declares — now answers the values of the shape features that subset it, directly or through what they redefine (a nested `part` is one of `subparts`, which subsets `subitems`, `subobjects` and `suboccurrences`), so `self.suboccurrences->size()` on a part def with two nested parts is `2` where it failed with `object has no such feature`; a Kernel collection nothing subsets reads as empty, a Kernel member that is no collection and the frame roots the runtime tracks itself keep their error.

- **Every Kernel Semantic Library member of an object reads.** `p1.self`, `p1.timeSlices`, `p1.superoccurrence`, `p1.localClock`, `p1.predecessors` and the other members an object inherits from the Kernel Semantic Library failed as `object has no such feature: member <m> not found in instance`, the same text a name the type lacks produced, while the same reads on an action usage answered `<undetermined>`. The Kernel tier stays out of the object's shape; a read of one of its members is now answered by one rule, on every surface (expression chains, `self.<m>` in a definition body, action and state objects, `%eval`, the gRPC `FeatureValue` RPC): `self` is the object and every collection `self` subsets (`timeSlices`, `portions`, `spaceSlices`, …) holds it; a member the library gives a value (`localClock default universalClock`, `this default self`) evaluates it; a member declared `inverse of` a composite collection is answered through composition (`p1.pa.superoccurrence` is `p1`); a lower bound of 0 with nothing recorded is empty or null (`predecessors`, `incomingTransfers`, `middleTimeSlice`); a lower bound of 1 or more with nothing recorded is undetermined (`snapshots`). A name the type lacks is refused naming the type (`p1 has no member ownedParts`), and a name a redefinition masks names the redefinition (`p1 has no member startShot: Parts::Part::start redefines it`). Writes are unchanged.

- A stated box keeps its keyword line beside a name that wraps: the DOT writer shrinks the name and the keyword together, from the node's size down to the floor, and drops the keyword only when even the floor holds no line for it, so a Cameo-sized box whose two-word name wraps still reads `«part def»` above it. Detail lines and compartments are fitted after, as before.

- A `.kpar` archive named as a library root (`-library-root`, `OPENSYSML_LIBRARY_ROOTS`, sysml-lsp `libraryRoots`) is read as an installed project instead of being dropped; a model file named from inside an archive finds its imports in the archive's other members and in the installed projects the archive's own `.project.json` uses, even when the archive lies inside another project's directory; and a library path (`OPENSYSML_LIBRARY_PATH`) naming an archive, or a directory holding one, that is not a project interchange archive now fails the library load with the archive's name and reason instead of loading an empty library.

- A coordinate frame the standard library declares with its axes in its own body (`ISQSpaceTime::universalCartesianSpatial3dCoordinateFrame`, `:>> mRefs default (SI::m, SI::m, SI::m)`) is now read as the frame value it describes, as a model's own `spatialCF` is: `universalCartesianSpatial3dCoordinateFrame` evaluates to `universalCartesianSpatial3dCoordinateFrame [m, m, m]` where it was `<undetermined>`, `(1.0, 2.0, 3.0) [universalCartesianSpatial3dCoordinateFrame]` is a position vector where it was refused as not a quantity expression, and `coordinateFrame` on a `SpatialItems::SpatialItem` answers its library default where the read failed with `no value for feature universalCartesianSpatial3dCoordinateFrame`. A frame-typed usage with no body of its own, and the library's frame types themselves, are value-held as before.

- A library symbol's implied generals — the base definition its kind implies and the base usage it subsets — are recorded with its facts and read from the record in every model, instead of being derived again over the library by each model that queries them; this returns the runtime's type and feature walks (`statedTypes`, `effectiveTypes`) to their speed before usages subset the base usage of their kind.

- **`library-root-name` is about the standard library alone.** The warning that a root namespace is named like a standard library package also fired on a root named like one of the bundled OpenSysML extension libraries (`DiagramLayout`, `MigrationMetadata`, `OpenSysMLRenderings`, …) — on every file the migrator writes with `-portable`, which inlines those `library package`s so the output loads in a tool that ships only the standard library. Those libraries are not standard library packages (KerML 1.0 §7.2.5.3 reserves the standard library's names alone), so a root named like one is no longer reported; a root named like `Base`, `ScalarValues` or `Requirements` still is.

- A `*` written as a feature value is typed as the Positive KerML makes it (`checkLiteralInfinitySpecialization`), so `attribute b : Boolean = *;` and `attribute s : String = *;` are reported as `binding-connector-type-conformance` errors, as a string bound to an Integer is, while `Natural`, `Positive`, `Integer`, `Rational` and `Real` targets still take it; it counts as one value under `feature-value-count`, so `(1, *)` fills an `Integer[2]`. Before, the value's type was unknown and such a binding went unchecked.

- `llm-bench` recordings keep the MCP tool specs the model was offered, and a replay offers those rather than the running server's, so `TestOllamaRecordingReplays` and `-provider replay` still reproduce a recorded run after `sysml-mcp` rewords a tool description; the request digests still cover the specs.

- **Instantiating a `ShapeItems::RightTriangularPrism` reports what is wrong once, where it lies, and nothing for what is merely unset.** The check `-instantiate` makes over a new object reported every feature value that failed to materialize, so one failing value was reported again for each feature reading through it — a prism given only its `height` printed five errors for two causes — and a value derived from an attribute nothing gives a value to (`xoffset = length / 2` over an unset `length`) was a finding while the unset attribute itself was not. A failure is now reported once, as the innermost feature value that failed, located by the usages walked to it (`in shape: feature value rf.width: cyclic feature value dependency: rf.width`); a value open for an unset feature is passed over as the unset feature is, and reading it is still the typed no-value error naming that feature. The two findings a fully dimensioned `RightTriangularPrism` still reports are the vendored library's own — `rf`'s `width = rf.width` is bound to itself, and `hypotenuse`'s `length = (length^2 + width^2)` writes an area to a `LengthValue` — recorded in `docs/project/omg-issues.md`.

- The Java release job pins `central-publishing-maven-plugin` 0.12.0 (was 0.7.0): the Central Portal API now returns a `warnings` field on deployment status, which 0.7.0 rejected as an unknown property, so the `v0.10.1` publish was reported failed after Central had published it.

- **The `sysml-mcp` catalog's CLI-only notes name what the server really has.** A caller asking after the CLI's `-debug` or `-quiet` is pointed at `sysml-mcp -log-level`, the flag the usage defines, rather than a `-log` flag that does not exist, and the note that the server reads `OPENSYSML_LIBRARY_ROOTS` in place of `-library-root` holds for every tool group: the `lsp_*` tools' language server now looks for installed projects under those roots as the `session_*` tools' sessions already did. A test under `cmd/sysml-mcp` pins each flag and variable the catalog names to one the usage documents.

- The `apply_edits` and `run_sweep` MCP tools no longer require `operations` and `ranges`: an empty batch and a sweep without ranges reach the service, which refuses them in band (`EDIT_FAILURE_NO_OPERATIONS`, the sweep's `error`) as the RPCs do, rather than being refused by the tool's schema.
- The `parse` MCP tool parses one document through `ParseFile`, which it stands for as well, when the service lacks the `parse_sources` capability; several documents, a `baseModelHash` or a named inline document still need `ParseSources` and are refused as the service refuses it.

- **`sysml-mcp` session tools stay inside the workspace root on every path.** A `repl.Session` now takes a file guard (`SetFileGuard`) that every file it reads or writes must pass — the files `%load`, `%save` and `%import` name, those a directory or glob expands to, and the dependencies found beside them, which the dependency search now asks about before reading a candidate (files really under a library root are exempt) — and `sysml-mcp` installs the workspace-root check on each session it opens. So a `%load`, `%save` or `%import` sent through `repl_command`, or a directory load that walks to a symlink pointing out of the workspace, is refused like a typed `session_load`/`session_save` naming the file.

- **`session_run` binds an action's inputs.** The `sysml-mcp` tool's `arguments` were read for a calc and an analysis case only; for `kind: action` they were dropped, so the action ran with its input parameters unbound and the verdict reported whatever that produced (a type mismatch in the body, typically) rather than the arguments' effect. The arguments now bind the action's `in` parameters as the runtime binds an invocation's: positionally in parameter order, or written `parameter=expression`, with an unknown parameter, a parameter bound twice and a wrong count each refused by name. The REPL session gains `RunActionWithInputs` beside `RunAction`; under `%engine check` or explore an action with arguments is unresolved, as those search every schedule from the action's own start. Run on an object that already performs the action, the arguments must be the bindings its performance holds (`in ref :>> context = this`, given as `context=<the object>`), as the ExecuteAction RPC requires; the comparison now reads a stored reference binding as the one-element sequence a bare pin holds, so the object itself passes where before only a not-yet-stored binding did.

- A `sysml-mcp` session save (`session_save`, `%save` through `repl_command`) writes through an `os.Root` opened at the resolved workspace root, as the `lsp` tools already did, so a symlink repointed out of the root between the path check and the write can no longer carry the file outside the workspace; the write stays atomic (a temporary file renamed over the target) and keeps an existing file's mode. The bare `sysml` prompt, which has no root, writes as before.

- The Magic MDK plugin names a migrated project's elements below the package the migration writes its root Model as, so Verify and the other operations find them again and their outcomes still annotate the Cameo elements.

- The runtime reads the member a declaration registered from its scope's declaration index
  instead of collecting every member of the scope and scanning for it, which the lookup of a
  nested requirement's redefining default did for each member of each instantiated type:
  instantiation and satisfaction allocate as they did before a nested requirement's subject
  was bound to its enclosing part.

- The runtime computes a feature's effective types (the types it states conjoined with
  those it inherits through subsetting, redefinition or reference) once per model instead
  of on every object it makes: instantiation and satisfaction of models whose features
  subset typed features allocate as they did before objects were typed by every inherited type.

- The execution runtime computes what an expression reads — the footprint a `while` or `until`
  condition, an `if` guard, a transition guard, a `for` collection or a `send` message stands
  on for order dependence — once per model instead of on every pass: a loop's every iteration
  rebuilt its condition's footprint, two allocations per pass that the 0.11.0 performance
  record found on every loop row (`IntegerArithmeticLoop` 9 072 → 11 073 allocations per run,
  280 → 483 KiB). A pass of the loop is back to the cost of its body.

- **The resolver keeps the members an import surfaces for the query that asks again.** Enumerating what a wildcard import brings into a namespace — the members of the imported namespace, those its own imports re-export, less what a colliding membership hides — was computed anew for every document a references query indexed and every occurrence a rename rewrote, and dropped when the enumeration returned; on a workspace of re-exporting packages one references query enumerated the same imports thousands of times, a gigabyte of member lists per query. The enumeration is now kept per import and namespace, journaled with the document that wrote it so an edit drops it, and kept only when nothing it read was still being computed.

- The semantic model decides once per member whether it restates a frame root (`Anything::self`,
  an occurrence's `timeSlices`, `incomingTransfers`, …) or a coordinate frame's featuring
  reference, instead of walking the member's redefinition and subsetting chain and building the
  qualified name of every feature on it each time a type's shape is computed: instantiating
  many types that share their library members allocates as it did before an item's nested
  actions were counted among its `ownedPerformances`.

- `x @@ T` now classifies the metadata of `x` as `x meta T` reads it, an annotation stated about the element as well as its own metaclass (KerML 1.0 §7.4.9.2: the operand of a metaclassification is `x.metadata`), so `Belt @@ Safety` over `#Safety part def Belt;` is `true` in an expression and `[@@Safety]` selects `Belt` in an element filter, where both previously held for the metaclass alone; `Belt @@ SysML::PartDefinition` and `belt @@ SysML::PartUsage` answer as before.
