- A metadata annotation written among the members of a calculation, constraint, action, analysis or verification body (`@VerificationMethod { kind = VerificationMethodKind::test; }` inside a `verification def`, `@CredibilityAssessment { … }` inside an `analysis def`) annotates the body as a comment or `doc` does and is no step of it, so the body's result expression or `return` runs; it was lowered as an executable statement and refused with ``statement not executable: … `then` statement in a body is not executable``, while the same annotation on the usage or written from outside with `metadata … about` already ran. Conformance cases for each body kind, a robustness suite and a compliance row record the rule.

- **A SysML v1 class whose stereotype specializes «Block» outside the document is a `part def`.** MagicDraw and Cameo archives apply their SysML profile's «Subsystem», «System», «Domain» and «External» without defining them; the migrator now follows the generalizations the archive's module snapshots declare — of the tool's modules and a user's own, and from a stereotype the document defines into a module's — and knows the tool's documented block stereotypes when no snapshot does, so such classes migrate as blocks — nested parts and ports, connection defs and table filters included — instead of falling through to `occurrence def`. The applied kind is kept as an `AppliedStereotype` marker; the report lists the class as mapped, or approximated with the reason when the generalization came from the tool's documentation rather than the archive.

- Connection definitions nested in view or requirement usages now refer to usage-typed ends through a feature chain from the package-level usage, so migrated models validate.

- **A migrated viewpoint's framed concerns are distinguishable.** A concern a viewpoint frames by reference, `frame <concern>;`, takes the referenced concern's name as its own, so two framed concerns of one name, or a framed concern named like a stakeholder of the viewpoint, left the viewpoint with indistinguishable memberships (KerML `validateNamespaceDistinguishibility`). Such a frame is now written in the grammar's named form, `frame concern <name> ::> <concern>;`, under a name fresh in the viewpoint and listed in its `SynthesizedName` marker; a frame whose name is its own alone stays `frame <concern>;`.
- **A call of a calc declares its result pin once.** The action written for a `CallBehaviorAction` of a `FunctionBehavior` declared its result pin bare and then again with the call's value, two members named `result`; it now declares the pin once, taking the value.

- SysML v1 migration: a node that is a UML `ExceptionHandler`'s `handlerBody` no longer starts with the activity and runs unconditionally beside the main flow. SysML v2 has no exception handler, so each handler is reported as not migrated (its protected node, body and exception type, or that it names none and so catches nothing in UML) with a `/* not migrated: ExceptionHandler … */` note, and the body is written as a `ref action` nothing starts and the migrated action does not perform, its own steps and layout kept.

- **Dependencies and comments on nested requirements parse again.** A dependency end or comment subject that is a nested requirement usage is now written as a qualified name (`A::B::C`), not a feature chain, which the grammar does not allow there.

- A nested `action` or `state` usage declaring no type (`action a1;`, `state s1;`) is a performance object its owner holds before anything performs it, as a typed one (`action a1 : Act;`) already was: one object for a usage declaring no multiplicity, as many as a fixed bound states (`action a[2];`), and none made up for an open bound (`[0..1]`, `[*]`), which keeps the open-multiplicity rule. `p3.a1` reads the object instead of `<undetermined>`.
- The usages count, as listable sequences (`#(i)`, iteration, `includes`) and at model level as on an instantiated object, in the collections the library says they belong to: `ownedActions`, `ownedStates`, `ownedPerformances`, `involvingPerformances`, `suboccurrences` and `timeEnclosedOccurrences` of a part, `ownedPerformances` of an item, `subperformances` and `enclosedPerformances` of an action. The collections of a package-level action usage nothing performs read `<undetermined>`, as the usage itself does.
- The context feature `this` of a performance held under `ownedPerformances` is its owner (a part's or an item's action or state), and of one held under `subperformances` its owner's `this` (Objects.kerml, Performances.kerml), instead of the performance itself. An action nested in an `item` subsets `Objects::Object::ownedPerformances` (KerML `checkStepOwnedPerformanceSpecialization`) rather than only `suboccurrences`.

- A requirement usage a definition owns now holds an object of the usage itself when its body only restates the subject, so a subject bound from the owning object's features — `subject s :>> s = sub;`, `subject s = sub;`, a bare `subject = sub;`, a sibling part, or a satisfy usage's `by pa` — or a `default` applying when nothing else values it (`subject s :>> s default sub;`, the library objective's `default Case::result`) — reads that value through the owning object (`p1.rq.s`, `ca2.obj.subj`), and `p1.rq.result` is the requirement's verdict on it, as `-validate` on the object already computed; a subject nothing binds stays `<undetermined>`. `satisfy requirement r : R by x;` is checked against the conditions `R` states instead of being refused as satisfying no requirement, on `x` even when `R`'s subject declares a default, and a negated one reads `R`'s verdict as its `result` while its standing is the negation. The usage's conditions read its own features — `attribute v :>> v = 5;` — not the owning object's of the same name, in the read and in the check alike; a subject written `=` to an empty feature is refused for its multiplicity rather than keeping the inherited value.

- A nested action usage typed by state-space dynamics or by a tool-performed action, binding the action's features on the usage (`action fall : FreeFall { :>> stateSpace = …; :>> timeStep = …; }` in an analysis or action body), is now performed as the same usage is standalone — stepped by the integrator, or run by the tool — instead of its merged body running as an empty flow that left the bound initial values in place and reported them as the result; a shape the run cannot perform (no initial state, an integrator the runtime does not provide, a tool nobody registers) is now refused where it was silently completed.

- **The nightly VS Code extension is versioned as a development build of the release it leads to.** The snapshot's `opensysml-sysml.vsix` carried the extension manifest's version — the release already made — with the night as its pre-release part (`0.10.1-nightly-20261019-e8607989d`), which SemVer ranks *below* the released `0.10.1`, so VS Code offered the release as an upgrade from every night after it and a night never read as newer than the release it built on. The vsix now carries the same development version the Python and npm snapshots do, `0.10.2-nightly-20261019-e8607989d`: every night ranks above the release it builds on and below the one it leads to, and the workflow still refuses a vsix whose stamped version is not the one it publishes.

- **A generic table over UML `Operation` lists the block operations again.** Since a block-owned operation migrates as its block's `action` usage, a `sysml -migrate` table whose `rowElementType` is `Operation` — or `Namespace`, which holds operations in UML — selected only the `action def` an operation a state runs keeps; it now also lists the operations written as usages, by name, as an `Actor` or `UseCase` table lists its usages.
- **A table over UML `Operation`, `Interaction`, `Activity` or `OpaqueBehavior` no longer lists state machines.** A `state def` conforms to `ActionDefinition` (as a `calc def` does), so these tables listed every state machine, and `Operation` and `Interaction` tables every calculation; the query now excludes the definitions those metaclasses never migrate to.

- A feature owned by the root namespace of a document is package-level, so redefining it from another root feature is now the error `A package-level feature cannot be redefined`, as it already was inside a `package`; and a package-level or root feature that redefines a feature of a type (`part e2 : Engine :>> Car::engine;`, or a KerML feature `featured by` a type) now reports `Must be an accessible feature`, since it has no featuring type to reach the target through (KerML `validateSubsettingFeaturingTypes`). Both match the pinned pilot validator; redefining a package-level feature from inside a type stays legal.

- A binding a part writes through an action it performs to a pin of one of its nodes (`bind run.measure.report = theReport;`, the form the feature-accessibility rule admits) now holds as the performance runs: the pin takes the part's value before the node starts, so a later node's `in report = measure.report` and an output default over it (`out go : Boolean = report.size <= 10.0;`) evaluate against the bound value instead of failing at the action's initialization with `operator '<=' is not defined for a sequence`; an extent query over objects performing such an action (`(all Body)->size()`) counts them without that failure.

- A performed action's parameter is held in the shape its effective multiplicity gives it from the moment it is bound, on every surface alike: a `[1]` pin holds its one value bare and a pin written without a multiplicity, or `[0..*]`, holds a one-element sequence — in the action's results, `-json`, the trace, the performing object's slots and the gRPC `FeatureValue` — where before the frame side printed the value as computed (`5`) while the slots held `[5]`, and the trace's shape changed with the order the body read the pin in. Every consumer that takes one value takes the element of a one-element sequence, as KerML's sequence semantics give it: performer and start targets, called functions, vector and measurement-reference arguments, `ConvertQuantity`, binding-connector equality, a send's target, tool and solver inputs; a repeated step's pin reads are flat. Calculation results and case verdicts are still returned as computed.

- Diagrams draw a port whose `DiagramLayout::Layout` is the source tool's rectangle — a SysML v1
  diagram sizes a port's shape to carry its name — as the notation's port square on its owner's
  border, named beside it: the Layout gives the port its side and place along it, from the
  rectangle's centre, never its size. The rule is one for a part's declared ports, the ports it
  has from its definition, and a definition's ports in a tree. An interconnection view that
  exposes a definition's port beside a part typed by the definition no longer draws the port
  twice, as the part's pin and as a box of its own over the part's title.

- A `snapshot` or `timeslice` usage nested in an occurrence reads the whole's value of every feature its own body does not value (`part panel : Panel { attribute :>> width = 3; snapshot atReview : Panel { attribute :>> height = 5; } }` — `panel.atReview.width` is `3` and the type's `product = width * height` evaluates over the portion to `15`), so a constraint over a snapshot's derived attribute no longer fails with `width has no value in the model`: a portion is the occurrence it is a portion of (SysML v2 §7.9.3, KerML 1.0 §7.4.5), its bound value beating the type's `default`, and a write to the whole is seen by its portions.

- In a positioned tree rendering (a migrated block definition diagram), a member of a drawn definition or usage that has no `DiagramLayout::Layout` of its own — the attributes, nested parts, constraints, ports and enum literals a source tool listed in compartments and gave no symbol — is drawn as a row of a compartment inside its owner's box instead of being left undrawn: rows are grouped by kind in the graphical notation's compartment order (`attributes`, `parts`, `ports`, `actions`, `constraints`, …), written as the member's textual notation (`gain : dB = 15.0`), kept inside the box's `Layout` size in DOT with the rows there is no room for counted in a `// not represented:` notice, and no edge is written to them. Members with a `Layout` of their own, unpositioned trees and renderings with `showCompartments = false` are unchanged. All of DOT, Mermaid, PlantUML and D2 draw the rows; the text and data forms are unchanged.

- The `Style` and `Note` annotations of an inherited `start` or `done` node (`about Run::start`)
  now colour and annotate that node in the rendering, as its `Layout` positions it; the SysML v1
  migrator writes them for an initial node, flow final node, initial pseudostate or final state
  symbol the export does not position, instead of dropping the colour and freeing the note.

- Migrated diagrams place the symbols of initial nodes, flow final nodes, initial pseudostates
  and final states (written as `start` and `done`, with a `Layout about Run::start`), and route a
  control flow, object flow or transition from its source to its target however the tool drew the
  line; a start marker is no longer drawn on top of the action its flow leads to.

- Report unresolved naming redefinitions with a typed unresolved-reference reason during qualified-name evaluation.

- A declaration whose value names itself by qualified name (`attribute a : Real = test::self::a;`), two declarations naming each other, or a redefinition bound to the feature it redefines (`:>> a = test::self::a`) now evaluates to the `cyclic feature value dependency` error naming the declaration the cycle closes on — in `-e` expressions, on an instantiated object, in the REPL and over the gRPC and LSP surfaces alike — where before the read recursed until the process died of a Go stack overflow; the same cycles spelled with dot notation already reported it, and the two spellings now report through one mechanism.

- A typed action body nested in its own definition, directly, through inheritance or through mutual typing, now executes: lowering defers the body and lowers it once when a performance of the node first starts, so a recursion the model bounds (`action def Walk { in depth : Integer; if depth > 0 { action step : Walk { in depth = depth - 1; … } } }`) runs to its depth and one it does not bound ends with the action-step budget's typed error instead of the former refusal at the top level of a body or an exhausted stack inside a loop or branch body.

- A redefinition that declares no direction no longer passes for a redefined `in`, `out` or `inout` feature: `:>> durationS = 3.0` in a usage body under `in durationS` is reported with `Redefining feature must have a compatible direction` (KerML 8.3.3.3.8), as `in :>> durationS = 3.0` is the conforming form; and a step's parameter is held to that rule for the parameter it redefines by position as well as the one its clause names, so `action stepA : Step { out :>> output; }` under `action def Step { in input; out output; }` is reported on `out :>> output`.

- `redefinition-not-end` now follows KerML 1.1 Beta 2 `validateRedefinitionEndConformance`: a non-end feature redefining an end feature is reported only when its owning type is an Association or a Connector. A usage typed by a connection definition (or a KerML feature typed by an association) may redefine an inherited end without `end`; redefinitions inside connection, interface, allocation and flow definitions and usages, KerML associations and connectors are still reported.

- A collection operation's body may be a function the argument names rather than a body expression, as KerML's function reference expression allows and the library's own `NumericalFunctions::sum0` (`collection->reduce '+' ?? zero`) relies on: `(1, 2, 3)->reduce '+'` is `6`, `->reduce max` the largest, `->collect Neg` applies a model's `calc def` to each element, where every such form was refused with `requires a body expression, got function`. A function declaring a different number of parameters than the operation passes is a body-arity error, and a value that is no function is still refused.

- A reference usage that reference-subsets a feature of the same object (`ref part y ::> x;`) holds that feature's values, so `rig.y` and `rig.y.id` read through to `x` instead of refusing the read as uninitialized, and a required reference nothing values on an object the model stands behind (`ref part peer : Node;` read as `rig.peer`) is `<undetermined>` like an unset attribute rather than an error; an object no model stands behind (`new Rig().peer`) and a binding or connector end over a valueless feature keep their typed refusal.

- A release rehearsal no longer fails its Go suite jobs with "the changelog records vX.Y.Z as released, but client/release-digests.json does not pin it": the suite jobs tagged their checkout as the release but did not export the stand-in `CIRCLE_TAG` the build and publish jobs run with, so the hygiene test read the local tag as a release that was already out. The rehearsal command that tags the checkout now exports the stand-in tag first, and the suite's tag-only checks (the protobuf breaking-change baseline, the binaries' version) run as they do on the tag.

- REPL, Jupyter kernel and MCP sessions: a submission that does not parse and declares no name (such as a bare expression typed with a trailing `;`) is now reported once and then dropped from the session buffer, instead of staying there and making every later expression evaluation fail with its stale `parse failed` diagnostic until `%clear`. Valid nameless submissions (imports, comments) and broken declarations are kept as before.

- A release build no longer fails at the landing-page diagram and the Node client jobs between the release's version bump and its tag. The bump pins the client's five platform packages at a version the tag has not published yet, so the lockfile cannot lock them, and npm 11.6 and later make `npm ci` refuse such a lockfile. The Makefile and every CI job now install `client/node` from its lockfile with `npm install --ignore-scripts`, which skips the optional packages it cannot resolve and installs everything else exactly as locked; each job asserts the lockfile is unchanged by the install, and a hygiene test keeps every consumer of the client and of the `sysml-mcp` package off `npm ci`.

- **An association end's name is drawn at the end it names, clear of the other labels.** A composite part's name and multiplicity on the composition to its type, a `ref`'s on its reference edge, and each end of a connection def drawn as a line between its end types, are the name of the edge's end (`Edge.FromLabel`, `Edge.ToLabel`), not the edge's own text: the DOT form sets it as `headlabel`, placed by `head_lp` beside the last segment of a routed edge before that end, PlantUML in quotes before the end's alias and D2 as the arrowhead's label; Mermaid's flowchart and the text form, which have no place at an end, keep it in the edge's text. A routed view's labels are placed in order, each clear of the drawn boxes, the notes, every routed line, the canvas's edge and the labels before it — the other side of the line, then a step along it — so compositions whose Cameo routes share a trunk no longer stack their names at one midpoint.

- A tree or interconnection rendering expands a typed usage through its definition: a bodiless `part saturnV : LaunchVehicle;` shows the stages LaunchVehicle declares and each `stage1 : Stage` the engines, attributes and ports its type declares, inherited features included and a redefining feature standing in for the one it redefines; a connector a definition declares is drawn in each usage of it, a binding to a redefined feature attaches to the feature redefining it there, and an element reached again through its own typing is noted rather than unfolded without end. `RenderView` over the engine, gRPC and combined surfaces, `%viz`, `-render` and the VS Code diagram webview all draw the same walk; the homepage's landing snapshot is regenerated.

- The REPL and the `sysml` command index each loaded document over the scope tree the document already built, instead of a second tree built from its AST, so a declaration reached from another file's scope — a qualified enumeration literal in a `-calc` argument, a package-level default a part in another file reads — is the one symbol the runtime holds. Before, with more than one file loaded, the two trees gave one enumeration literal two identities, so `==` and `includes` compared them unequal: a calc's enum-typed parameter never matched the literal in its body unless the defining file was loaded last, a rollup over units whose modes came from a default in another file summed to zero, and a `+` of two such rollups kept only the term that happened to resolve in the right tree.

- `%help` lists every option `%render` takes — the line routing, `verdicts`, `show=<elements>` and `depth=<levels>` — where it named only the form, palette, style, ports and link template; the `usage:` line already did.

- Validating a requirement that `require`s many sibling requirements, or a verification objective that `verify`s them, took time exponential in the member count: each `require`/`verify` member takes its short name from the requirement it references, and resolving that reference asked the owning scope for every sibling's derived short name again, so a group of ten took seconds and twelve never finished. The resolver now memoizes a feature's derived short name once its naming feature is settled, holding one that was asked while a sibling's naming was under way until that sibling proves to have no short name, so the group validates in time linear in its members; a check-level test over a fourteen-member group and objective and a benchmark pin the complexity.

- A result parameter an object inherits from the library behavior it enacts now answers the object's own evaluation instead of failing as a missing feature: `RequirementCheck::result` on a requirement, viewpoint or concern object is its required conditions conjoined, `Case::result` is the case's own `return` parameter, and `VerificationCase::verdict` is the verdict the case's body produced; an evaluation reading an unbound input (a subject nothing supplies) is undetermined. A verification usage read as a calc answers the same inherited `verdict`/`result` names. A constraint usage of a part (`constraint c { … }`, `assert constraint c2 { … }`) read on the object — `rack.c`, `rack.c2`, `rack.c.result`, `new Rack().c2` — is the constraint evaluated on that object, `<undetermined>` with the reason where an input is unbound, where it failed as an uninitialized feature value.

- `-self-check-package` house rules (and `meta` casts) can read the relationship-valued features of the metamodel: `Element::ownedRelationship`, `ownedAnnotation`, `owningRelationship`, `owningMembership`, `textualRepresentation`; `Namespace::ownedMembership`, `ownedImport`; `Type::ownedFeatureMembership`, `featureMembership`, `inheritedMembership`; `AnnotatingElement::annotation`, `annotatedElement`, `ownedAnnotatingRelationship`, `owningAnnotatingRelationship`, `Documentation::documentedElement`; and the requirement properties their memberships derive — `text`, `requiredConstraint`, `assumedConstraint`, `framedConcern`, `actorParameter`, `stakeholderParameter`, `VerificationCase*::verifiedRequirement`, `satisfiedRequirement`, `assertedConstraint`, `satisfyingFeature`. Memberships, Annotations and Imports reflect as metaobjects of their own (`OwningMembership`, `FeatureMembership`, `RequirementConstraintMembership` with `kind`, `RequirementVerificationMembership`, `SubjectMembership`, `Annotation`, `NamespaceImport`, …) with the ends and attributes their metaclass declares, and `disjoint from`, `unions`, `intersects`, `differences`, `featured by` and `inverse of` clauses reflect as `Disjoining`, `Unioning`, `Intersecting`, `Differencing`, `TypeFeaturing` and `FeatureInverting`. These rules were reported as "reflective feature is not derived" (unevaluated) before.

- `send … via p` from a state machine whose definition owns `port p`, exhibited by a part, now leaves through the performance's port as the model binds or connects it: a `bind m.p = p` or `:>> p = Part::p` on the exhibit usage sends through the part's port, and a connector written against the performance's port (`connect m.p to q`) carries the message; before, the send failed with "joined to no port that can receive it" however the state's port was bound. A state port nothing binds or connects is still unroutable, as its namesake on the part is another feature. A transition's `accept … via q` on a port the state definition owns, when the exhibiting part has no port named `q`, now receives what reaches the performance's port through its binding or a connector written against it, instead of never firing.

- A sequence rendering (`SequenceView`) now orders its messages by the successions between their events, each message's send and receive events sharing one row: an order stated through an event no message attaches to is carried through it, and declaration order decides only between messages nothing orders. A relay whose `then` successions on one lifeline order a send before another's, while the receiving events are left unordered, renders `a, b, c` where it rendered the receives' declaration order `a, c, b`; a cycle among the stated orders is still reported and declaration order stands. The flow and self-message of the handshake fixture, which nothing orders, now keep their declaration order.

- A `case`, `use case`, `view`, `viewpoint`, `concern`, `rendering`, `enum`, `satisfy requirement` or `include use case` usage a definition owns is a feature of its objects: reading it on an instance answers the nested object (or the enumeration literal) instead of refusing the member, and the standard library's collections of those kinds (`Case::subcases`, `UseCase::includedUseCases`, `View::subviews`, `Viewpoint::concerns`, `Rendering::subrenderings`, …) read on any object. An untyped one with no body (`view sketch;`) materializes an object like an untyped `part`, validating the owner visits the assertions those objects state, and `all` over their definition enumerates them.

- An invocation's argument list is evaluated in one run: `%calc`, `%analysis`, `%sweep`, `%runs`, `%invoke`, `%check` and the dry run of a tool call, positional and `parameter=expression` arguments alike, bind every argument from one evaluation of the list, so a seeded `random` or `uniform` in a later argument continues the stream the earlier one drew from instead of restarting it at the seed's first value, and `%seed` reproduces the whole invocation. The MCP `session_run`, the inputs it binds to an action, and the REPL's `RunCalc`, `RunAnalysis`, `RunMonteCarlo` and `RunActionWithInputs` entry points bind through the same evaluation.

- `%configure` and `%solve` read a variant or enumeration literal back whole when the solver writes its qualified name as a `|quoted|` symbol — a package or feature name with a space, a hyphen or a quoted name, as in every OMG variability teaching model — instead of failing with `<first word> is not a value of <variation>`; a reply the driver keeps as the solver's own text now writes back exactly as the solver wrote it.

- Five productions the specification's own examples use now parse with the shape the grammar gives them: a cross feature before the `ref` of an end usage (`end a_cross[1] ref a[1] : A;`), a terminate action usage naming the occurrence it terminates (`action terminate1 terminate process;`, lowered as the bare statement is, and ending that occurrence rather than the machine when a state transition reaches it), an accept payload with a value part (`accept : ChangeSignal = TriggerWhen({ level > threshold });`, waited on as `accept when` is when the invocation resolves to the library's `Triggers` function), a keyword-less usage stating `ordered`/`nonunique` without a bracketed multiplicity (`orderedContent ordered :>> content;`), and a directed calculation parameter whose body ends in a result expression (`in calc redefines whileTest { test1 }`). A multiplicity after the feature a connector end references, which no production admits, is reported once at the multiplicity instead of cascading.

- **The execution runtime computes a statement's order and stamped footprint once per model, not per executor or pass.** An assignment in an action body records what it writes through the statement's footprint with the performance's own places stamped as its; that stamping cloned the statement's read and write places on every execution, two allocations per executed statement, so a `for` loop of a thousand passes allocated two thousand more objects than before. The statement order the footprint comes from was also memoized per executor, so a chain of a thousand actions rebuilt every body's order on every run, and a state's entry or do behavior rebuilt its order on every entry. Both are now computed once per model, and a footprint with nothing to stamp is used as it is.

- `Must be an accessible feature (use dot notation for nesting)` is now reported on a `specialization subset f :> T::g;` statement as it is on the inline `feature f :> T::g;` form: both notations write the one Subsetting, and KerML 8.3.3.3.10 holds of it however it is spelled.

- A state transition into a terminate node that names the occurrence it ends — directly or through a choice or junction branch — now projects the node's body and the move to its body's completion vertex into the transition's footprint, so scheduling sees the body's reads and writes instead of only an unresolved target.

- A state-space dynamics action performed again in one `sysml` process — a second `-action`, or
  an `-action` after an `-analysis` or another action — now integrates from its bound initial
  state for its whole `stopTime` and ends at the same values as the first performance. `stopTime`
  and `time` (`StateSpaceIntegration::FixedStepDynamics`) are durations from the performance's
  own start, not instants of the shared clock, so a performance begun after the clock had
  advanced no longer ends at its initial state, drops a step or reports a `time` offset by the
  earlier runs. A negative `stopTime` is refused as a typed error.

- The state-machine interchange fixture `tests/export/testdata/interchange/states.sysml` is valid SysML v2 again: its referencing `do x;` subaction moved to a second `state def` so each state owns at most one subaction per kind, and its toolkit JSON exports were regenerated with sysml-toolkit v0.10.10. Reading toolkit full JSON, a succession end the toolkit reaches only through an implied `ReferenceSubsetting` is the derived position a `then` left unwritten, not a written end: `then done;` after a named action no longer comes back as `succession first a then done;`, and `then timeslice secondEvent;` / `then action step2 { }` come back attached to their declarations instead of as a `succession first … then …;` ahead of them.

- A parameter a `perform` usage's body restates (`perform action run : Sequence { in sysLimit = 6.8; }`) implicitly redefines the parameter at its position of the performed action, so a model-level read through the performance of a nested node's pin valued from it (`action decision : Decide { in limit = sysLimit; }`, read as `run.decision.limit`) now holds the supplied value instead of an empty sequence, and an output default over that pin (`out go : Boolean = limit <= 10.0;`) evaluates instead of failing the materialization of the performer with a `type mismatch` over the empty operand; the performance itself already ran the node with the value.

- A `concern def`, `viewpoint def`, requirement or case whose first owned member is a `stakeholder` or `actor` and that declares no `subject` is now reported with `Subject must be first parameter.` (SysML `validateRequirementDefinitionSubjectParameterPosition`): an actor or stakeholder is an `in` parameter, so it displaces the subject the declaration inherits from first position; a `frame concern` of such a definition is reported too, an owned subject declared after one carries the error itself, and a declaration whose subject (named or anonymous `subject;`) comes first stays silent.

- Instantiating a part whose `shape` is a `ShapeItems::Cylinder` or `Cone` no longer reports `binding end cannot be resolved "cf.edges": feature edges not found` and `Surface has no member vertices`: an object held by a feature typed by one definition that subsets a feature typed by another has both types (KerML 1.0 §7.3.4.3–§7.3.4.4), so `cf : Surface :> faces` carries `StructuredSurface`'s `edges` and `vertices`, a member both types declare is one feature, a redefinition inherited through either type names one feature under both names (chains of the two types that share a name being one chain), a feature stating several types (`part both : A, B`) holds values of every one, and a Kernel member the model binds through any of the object's types is a slot of the object. A subsetter typed by an unrelated definition now contributes an object of both types to the feature it subsets rather than failing as a type mismatch.

- Instantiating a `ShapeItems::Pyramid`, `RectangularPyramid` or `Tetrahedron` no longer fails with `feature value shape.apex: multiplicity violation: 2 value(s) bound to a feature with multiplicity upper bound 1`: a feature declaring no multiplicity that binds a value while subsetting a type-owned feature (`item apex :> vertices = wall.apex`) takes no default of one (SysML v2 §7.6.3) and is bound by the upper bound of what it subsets, so the two apex vertices of a wall are admitted under `vertices[0..*]`; a feature redefining it and restating none is bound the same way, a subsetted bound naming a value of the owner (`vertices [limit]`) is read in the owner's scope, and a subsetting feature restating `[1]` or subsetting nothing still refuses two values.

- A usage that subsets or redefines a definition (`part eng : Engine :> Mass;` with `attribute def Mass`) is now an error in default mode for `.sysml` sources, reported at the target as `subsets target must be a feature, found attributeDef`, as it already was for `.kerml` sources: KerML's `Subsetting::subsettedFeature` and `Redefinition::redefinedFeature` are typed Feature, a rule SysML usages inherit and the pinned pilot validator enforces (`Couldn't resolve reference to Feature 'Mass'`). Redefinition targets are judged by the same rule; the reference form of a satisfy or verify usage (`verify RD;`) stays with the referent-kind rule, so a definition there is reported once.

- `subsetting-constancy-conformance` now fires for SysML usages: a usage that may vary in time (owned by an occurrence definition or usage, not a portion, not a composite action — `Usage::mayTimeVary`) and subsets or redefines a `constant` usage or an end without being constant itself is an error, as `validateSubsettingConstantConformance` requires; before, only a KerML `var` feature was ever reported. The rule reads the subsetted feature's own constancy, so a variable feature subsetting a non-constant feature that itself subsets a constant one is no longer reported. A parameter that implicitly redefines the general behavior's parameter at its position (no `:>>` written) is held to the same rule, as its direction already was.

- **SysON plugin: document-query bindings recognise qualified names with a linear scan.** This avoids stack overflow on very long names, accepts escaped characters in quoted names such as `'Frank\'s Braindump'`, and evaluates unquoted non-ASCII letters as expressions because KerML basic names are ASCII-only.

- **The SysON plugin drops a project's copy of a bundled library when SysON files it under its root namespace.** SysON puts every uploaded document, a user library included, under an unnamed root `Namespace` that its serializer never prints, so the exporter saw no `library package` at the resource root and sent the project's `DocumentQueries` copy to the service, where it shadowed the bundled library: the service warned that the root namespace has a standard library's name, and `part def MassReport :> Document` was refused as "not a document" because its `Document` was the copy's. The root namespace's members are now exported one by one, so an unextended copy is left out with the run message saying so, a copy that adds members is still exported with the warning, and the run's element ranges point at the members rather than at the namespace.

- **The execution runtime takes a state machine's transition without allocating.** Taking a transition put the executor under the choice points the guard stood on through two closures, one to release the control and one to put the executor back where it was, made again on every transition taken; a state machine looping on a transition ten thousand times made twenty thousand of them. The control is now released by its position and the executor restored from a value, so taking a transition allocates nothing of its own.

- The metamodel evaluator answers `TypeFeaturing::owningFeatureOfType` (KerML 1.1 §8.3.3.2.14): the property was mapped to the relationship's owning end in a `case` that duplicated an earlier one for the same metaclass and so was never reached, leaving the property unresolved in the API JSON and `-self-check-package` surfaces; the one `TypeFeaturing` case now maps `source`, `featureOfType` and `owningFeatureOfType` to the owning Feature and `target`/`featuringType` to the owned Type.

- The unbounded value `*` is answered wherever the ordering KerML gives it decides the result, in the function notation as in the operator notation: `'>'(*, 1)`, `'=='(*, 1)` and the other comparison and equality functions, `max`, `min` and `abs` in every numeric library package (`max(*, 1)` is `*`, `min(*, 1)` is `1`, `abs(*)` is `*`), `ToString(*)` (`"*"`) and unary `+`, which the evaluator refused before while answering `* > 1`. Arithmetic over it (`+`, `-`, `*`, `/`, `%`, `**`, `^`, unary `-`, `..`, `sum`, `product`) is still an evaluation-time error naming the operation — the Kernel Function Library defines no result for it, and the pinned pilot leaves such expressions unevaluated — while the checker accepts the well-typed expression, as it accepts a division by zero. Beside a feature the model leaves open, the ordering stays open in both notations too (`u < *` was refused while `'<'(u, *)` was answered), and `max(*, u)` is `*` whatever `u` holds.

- The runtime judges a one-value sequence written to a unique feature as repeating nothing
  before building the set it judges repeats by, so a calculation's result held in its
  one-element shape is read into a unique feature without allocating: a loop stepping a
  calculation usage allocates five fewer times per result read, recovering most of what
  holding results in their effective multiplicity added.

- **An unresolved name among namespaces whose imports are themselves unresolved no longer stalls analysis.** The hint explaining an unresolved name reads the imports around it, resolving each target; a target that failed to resolve on that account asked for a hint of its own, and with several unresolved imports in namespaces that import each other the hints multiplied without bound. A lookup made on behalf of another now reports nothing and so computes no hint, and a library namespace, which hides nothing, is not read for one.

- A feature chain through a package-level usage no object stands for (`costReq.maximumCost`, `pkgPart.someAttr`) now reads the value the member binds — or inherits from the feature it redefines — as the qualified name `costReq::maximumCost` reads it, through valueless members of exactly one on the way (`req.details.limit`) honouring the nested redefinitions the usage and its types declare (`attribute :>> details.limit = 9.0;`) and the names a redefinition aliases, exactly as an instantiated object reads them, in `-e` expressions, verification and analysis case bodies and `require constraint` bodies alike; before, the chain was undetermined and a verification case reading it ended in an `error` verdict. A member nothing binds, a usage whose instances are not exactly one, or a member whose count the model leaves open stays undetermined; a bound value that does not evaluate reports its error; and a usage an object stands for still reads that object.

- `sysml` diagnostics count a diagnostic's UTF-16 column in place instead of encoding the line prefix, and convert a snippet's source to bytes once per snippet rather than once per diagnostic; same columns, fewer allocations per diagnostic.

- The solver judges a requirement that assumes but requires nothing as the evaluator does: its assumptions are the antecedent of its claim (`allTrue(assumptions()) implies allTrue(constraints())`), never asserted on their own, so `%check` and `%solve` of such a requirement — or of a satisfaction of one — are satisfiable instead of asserting the assumption, and the `holds` question of the gRPC `VerifyRequirement` and `VerifySatisfy` calls proves it from a violation query that is false instead of refusing with `ErrNoConditions`. A constraint assuming but requiring nothing still yields no verdict, as a `ConstraintCheck` states no result.

- A feature chain through a variant named with no selecting object — `Case::big.m` through the variation definition, `cases.big.m` or `engine.electric.power` through a variation usage, a variant with a declared value such as `TargetCases::largeCase.bodyMass`, or a part bound to a variant and read through — now evaluates to that variant's value, as the pilot does, instead of failing with `cannot chain through non-instance member (variant)`; a variant bound as an analysis or verification case's subject, directly or through a configuration part, is evaluated the same way. Selection semantics are unchanged: a bound variation still reads its one selected variant, and an unselected one still reports so.

- A variation definition or usage is named `variation part def`, `variation part`, `variation attribute` and so on wherever an element's notation is shown — the «keyword» of tree, interconnection and mixed diagrams in every form (text, Mermaid, DOT, D2, PlantUML, SVG and the editor's diagram payload), the kind column of element tables, compartment rows, hover and completion — the way SysML v2.1 §7.6.7 declares it and Table 4 draws it, where before only its variants were marked and the variation point read as an ordinary `part`; a typed variant (`variant part x`) is likewise named `variant part`, not `part`. The `variation` keyword is the notation's marker that the element is abstract (a variation never writes `abstract`).

- The solver compares a value-typed variation point — one whose every variant declares a value, `variant attribute low : Integer = 3;` — by the value its selection stands for, as KerML `BaseFunctions::'=='` is value equality for data values and the evaluator already answers: `P == P::V`, `P == 3`, `P != P::V` and the quantifier forms `P->forAll { in ref w; w == P::V }` hold for every variant declaring that value, so `%configure`, `%solve`, `%check` and the MCP `configure` tool now permit the same selections `%validate` accepts instead of selecting one variant by name. Two variants named outright still compare as the choice itself, an object-valued variation still compares its selection, and a point whose variants declare values and objects both is refused with that reason.

- A verification case a part definition owns, swept with no object of the definition instantiated (`-satisfy`, `-requirement`, `-analysis` without an object, gRPC `VerifySatisfaction`/`VerifyRequirement`/`RunAnalysis` and their JSON), is `inconclusive`, saying it `needs an object of <Definition>: none is instantiated` as the definition's `satisfy` line does, rather than an `error` about the owner's feature having no value; an analysis case a definition owns is refused the same way. A case run on an object of the definition is unchanged.

- An objective's `verify r;` is no longer reported as a satisfaction assertion by `-satisfy`, `%satisfy` and gRPC `VerifySatisfaction`, which answered it `could not be evaluated` with no subject to read; the verification surfaces report it.
- A `satisfy r;` a definition states is checked model-wide (`-satisfy`, `%satisfy`, gRPC `VerifySatisfaction`) against every object of that definition the session or the service holds, one verdict per object as `-validate=` decides it, and asks for an object of the definition when none exists instead of failing on the requirement's subject (`no value for feature product`); one a usage states instantiates that usage, as `by` does.
- A verification objective stating only `verify r;` is satisfied — a requirement check over no constraints is true (`Requirements::RequirementConstraintCheck`) and `verify` records what the case verifies, not a condition — so the case is decided by its body's verdict and `-analysis` exits 0 for a passing case and 1 for a failing one; an objective whose own condition cannot be evaluated stays `undecided`.

- **Reject `frame`, `filter` and `accept` members written outside the bodies that own them.** A `frame` is a RequirementBodyItem — a requirement, concern or viewpoint body, never a view's; a `filter` is a PackageBodyElement and a view/view-def body item — a package or the file root admits it, a viewpoint, rendering or part-def body does not; an `accept` is an ActionBodyItem — an action, calculation or case body (and the state body that already read it), not a view or part def. Each is now reported at the member instead of parsing silently, matching the reference grammar. A `frame` in a view definition or usage body, which 0.10.0 parsed with a `nonstandard-notation` warning (an error only under `-strict`), is now a parser error in every mode, since `FramedConcernMember` appears only in `RequirementBodyItem`.
- **Report `then <target>;` written outside an action or state body.** A target succession sequences members of an action, calculation or case body, and a state body reads it as a target transition; anywhere else it is OpenSysML extension notation, warned by default and rejected under `-strict`.

- **A migrated tree view exposes only what a symbol of its own stands for.** MagicDraw lists a class box's compartment rows — attributes, constraint properties, enumeration literals, ports listed in a compartment — among a diagram's used elements, and keeps an association end's role name as a symbol on the line. The SysML v1 migrator exposed each of them, so a tool opening the view drew every attribute, and every part usage whose only appearance was a role name, as a node of its own: an internal block diagram on a block definition diagram. A view rendered `asTreeDiagram` now exposes the definitions, usages, ports and lines that have a symbol, and leaves a listed element no symbol stands for, or whose symbols all lie on a line, to the exposed element that draws it; the diagram's ledger entry counts them as "compartment rows or end labels of exposed elements, not exposed". Hidden symbols and listings (`visible` false) are off the diagram as before. The `DiagramLayout::Layout` and `Route` annotations are unchanged, with shorter qualified names where a same-named end usage is no longer exposed. Interconnection, use case, state and activity views are not affected.
- **The line backing an attribute's type on a block definition diagram keeps its route.** An anonymous association that only backs a value or enumeration attribute is skipped as notation, and the tree rendering draws the attribute's typing edge from its owner's box to its type's box; the Cameo line's `DiagramLayout::Route` is now written about that attribute, run from the owner's box whichever end the tool drew first, so the rendering follows it instead of reporting the route `not written`. A private attribute, which the view cannot name, takes no route (reported `not exposed`).

- State renderings position, style, and annotate parallel-state regions with the Layout, Style, and notes stated about the substates that declare them, so a Layout about a region is no longer reported as unplaced.

- A view's `render tree;` is exported to Turtle and API JSON under the name of the rendering it references — `qualifiedName` `V::tree`, `name` `tree`, no `declaredName` — as SysML v2 `RenderingUsage::namingFeature` and the SysML v2 API name it, rather than by its position (`V::@0`). The same holds for every other member named by its naming feature instead of a declaration: `perform a;`, `exhibit s;`, `include uc;`, `frame c;`, `require r;`, `assume r;`, a variant reference and `part :>> engine;` now carry `sysml:name` and the effective qualified name; graphs that still name such a member by position read back as before.

- **The WebAssembly gate no longer times out on a slow CI runner.** `tests/wasm` compiles and vets the whole tree for both wasm targets, 7-11 minutes on a hosted runner, and ran under `go test`'s default ten-minute budget, so a slow run died with `test timed out after 10m0s` in whichever test was running rather than on an assertion. `make wasm-check` now gives the gate an explicit budget (`WASM_GATE_TIMEOUT`, 25 minutes), each command is linked once per run and shared between the build, run and browser-REPL tests, every link is its own timed subtest, and the GitHub Actions gate restores a build cache of its own that a push to `develop` saves, so a pull request starts from develop's compiled objects instead of compiling both targets cold.

## 0.10.1 — 2026-10-14

### Added

- **The release-gate performance record of 0.10.1 against 0.10.0.** `docs/project/performance-release-0.10.1-vs-0.10.0.md` measures the `hotfix/0.10.1` candidate against the `v0.10.0` tag: every benchmark on both revisions under `benchstat`, the rows in doubt re-run interleaved, the stress constellation, and whole-binary validation, start-up and size. The patch is at parity everywhere but the executor, where the frame feature-identity rule costs +7–17% per loop step with allocations unchanged once five avoidable regressions it exposed — the resolver's generalization re-walk, a resolver lookup per name read, frames copied per probe, a per-member slice in the distinguishability check and a root-frame index grown entry by entry — were fixed in their own changes; analysis allocates 1–6% less than 0.10.0.

### Changed

- **A changelog fragment is skipped as already folded only when `## Unreleased` or the latest release carries its entry.** `scripts/changelog.py` compared each fragment against every version in `CHANGELOG.md`, so a new entry whose text repeated a line of an old release was dropped without a word. Only those two sections can hold an entry an interrupted or repeated fold already wrote, so only they are searched now, and `render`, `release`, `summary` and `check` name each skipped fragment and the section that carries it on stderr; `check` still passes.

- **`install.sh --tools all` and `install.ps1 -Tools all` install every tool the chosen release published.** `all` stood for the four tools of the current release, so on a release that predates one of them (`sysml-jupyter-kernel` before v0.10.0, `sysml-grpc` before v0.9.0) it failed to download the missing asset instead of installing the rest. Both scripts now resolve `all` against the release's `SHA256SUMS.txt`, install the tools it lists for the platform, and say which it lacks; a tool named explicitly on a release without it still fails.

- The SysML v1 migrator translates a call of a mathematical function by its bare name in an
  English constraint or guard body (`sine_wave = sin(t)` is `sine_wave == TrigFunctions::sin(t)`):
  `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `cot`, `arcsin`/`asin`, `arccos`/`acos`, `arctan`/`atan` through
  `TrigFunctions`; `sqrt`, `abs`, `min`, `max`, `floor`, `ceil`/`ceiling`, `round` and `pow`
  as the script dialects write them; `exp`, `ln`, `log` and `atan2` through
  `OpenSysMLMathFunctions`, refused in a strict migration. Each takes the arguments the library
  function takes and yields its result type. In an English or a script body alike, a `Complex`
  argument is refused where the parameter is a `Real` and from `min` and `max`, which order
  their arguments; `abs` of a `Complex` is `ComplexFunctions::abs`, and `abs`, `min` and `max`
  of a `Number` go through `NumericalFunctions` rather than `RealFunctions`. A dotted name, a name outside the table, a name of
  several words before the `(` or the wrong number of arguments stays refused, and the reason
  now lists the names an English body does read.

- **The `export` race shard of CI has the same 55-minute package timeout as `migrate`.** `tests/model`, which that shard runs, takes 25-30 minutes under the race detector on the CI executor, so it reached the 30-minute default timeout on roughly one run in four and failed the `develop` build that the nightly snapshot selects from.

### Fixed

- **The SysON plugin exports the members of SysON's root namespace as the document's roots.** SysON files every uploaded document under an unnamed root `Namespace` its serializer never prints, so `runWithOpenSysML` exported an empty document and every name in the project was unresolved; the exporter now walks that namespace's owned memberships and exports each member as a root of its own, with its line range and index entry.

- **The client test suites accept a release that pins the Jupyter kernel binaries beside the service.** From v0.10.0 a release pins both the `sysml-grpc-*` and the `sysml-jupyter-kernel-*` assets, and the Python, Java, Node and Rust suites held every pinned release to exactly the five service assets, failing the v0.10.0 pin; they now require the service family whole in every release and the kernel family whole wherever it is present.

- **A direction prefixes every usage kind a behavior body admits.** `out concern c : C;`, `out requirement r : R;`, `out use case u : U;`, `out verification v[*] : V;` and the other usage kinds the grammar reaches through `OccurrenceUsagePrefix` (SysML.xtext `RefPrefix`) were read as parameters named by the kind keyword and rejected with `"concern" is a reserved keyword` and `expected '{' or ';' after parameter`; they now parse as the directed usage they declare and lower as parameters of the owning action or calculation, as a directed `item` or `part` does. A directed `analysis` or `verification` usage is that parameter only: it is no longer also lowered as a case step the action performs.

- **A body reads a name as the feature name resolution binds it to, not as whatever nearest frame binds that spelling.** Lowering now carries the declared feature on each `Attribute`, `Declare` and `Accept` node, every frame indexes its bindings by that feature beside the simple name, and a read resolves the name in its own scope first and reads the frame holding that feature. A short name now reads the value the performance holds (`attribute <sx> x` written through `x` and read through `sx` no longer answers the declaration's default), a body attribute shadows a same-named accept payload instead of being overwritten by it, and a transition declared in a state definition reads the definition's attribute rather than a same-named attribute of its source state or exhibiting part. A nested attribute declared with no value reads as missing (`NoValueError`) rather than as a same-named attribute of the enclosing body.
- **An unset attribute or parameter no longer reads a same-named feature of an enclosing scope.** When the frame holding the feature a name resolves to has no value for it, that frame decides the read: the feature is unset — the empty sequence for a parameter nothing binds, `no value` for an attribute — and the name walk never answers with another feature of the same name. Models that relied on the fall-through (a performed action's unbound `in` parameter reading the performer's same-named attribute, a typed usage's body reading the enclosing action's output through the callee's unset one) now report the unbound value.

- **A keyword only the other language reserves is a name wherever a name stands.** `step`, `expr`, `inv`, `class`, `feature`, `chains`, `type` and the other literals of `KerML.xtext` that `SysML.xtext` does not spell are ordinary names in a `.sysml` file, and SysML's own literals (`action`, `part`, `attribute`, `snapshot`, …) are names in a `.kerml` file; before this, a `.sysml` file reading `attribute step : Real;` could declare the attribute but not use it — `assert constraint { step <= upperBound }` was parsed as a KerML step declaration and reported `expected an expression`, and `step < upperBound` as a short name missing its `>`. The parser now reads such a word as a name before an operator, a `:`, a `;`, a `=`, a `[`, a `)`, a `}`, a `,`, a `.`, a `::` or a `(`, in both notations, and keeps the keyword only where a named declaration of the other notation follows it (`step s;`, `feature redefines x;` in a `.sysml` file; `action a;` in a `.kerml` one), which the `kerml-notation` warning and the `SysML notation` error continue to report. Before a body or a multiplicity the word is the name as well: `step { }` and `step [1];` declare keywordless usages, and an anonymous KerML declaration such as `inv { x > 0 }` or `connector [1] a to b;` is now a parse error in a `.sysml` file, as in the pilot, rather than a `kerml-notation` warning. The LSP semantic tokens follow the same reading, so `step` in `step <= upperBound` is no longer highlighted as a keyword.

- **The migrator no longer writes a `DiagramLayout::Layout` for an association or connector end's symbol.** On a block definition diagram Cameo keeps a member end's role symbol — a 10×10 box at the end of the line — inside the association's symbol; the migration wrote it as a `Layout` of the end property, so a tree rendering drew every part property whose association the diagram shows as a tiny nested node under its definition, as well as on the composition edge. A shape nested in a path symbol is now counted as lying on the connection on every diagram kind, as it already was on use case diagrams: no `Layout` is written for it, and the report's placement line and each diagram's note say how many symbols lay on connections. The association's line carries the position instead: its `Route` is pinned to the connection def the association is written as, with the points run from the def's first end's type to its second's — the direction a tree rendering reads a connection def's route in — whichever way the diagram drew the line, so the composition edge follows the line from the owning block's box. An anonymous association with a classifier-owned end is written as its end properties, not as a connection def, so its line is left to the renderer. A property drawn as its own box in a block's compartment keeps its `Layout`.

- **A migrated view qualifies the references its own `expose`s would capture.** Each `expose` imports the exposed element's name into the view, where every reference in the view's body resolves before the enclosing scopes do (KerML 8.2.3.5.3, local resolution includes imported memberships). A diagram showing a definition beside a same-named usage, such as a block `GUI` and a part `GUI` typed by it, wrote `expose GUI` and `Layout about GUI` for the definition, which inside the view denoted the usage: the usage's layout was reported as already stated, the definition was left without a position and undrawn, and the associations ending at it fell back to stray boxes. The writer now treats the names the exposes import as the view's own and spells out the full name of what a simple one would no longer denote, for the `expose` targets themselves and for every Layout, Route, Style and Note `about` in the body; a name one expose imports by its simple spelling is written as before, so output changes only where it was ambiguous.

- A string literal or unrestricted name that spans lines now lexes as the one token the
  KerML `STRING_VALUE` and `UNRESTRICTED_NAME` terminals define, instead of being cut at
  the line break and reported as unterminated with a cascade of parse errors after it. A
  literal the file never closes is reported once, at its opening quote, and taken to end
  with its line so the declarations after it are still read; the REPL and Jupyter kernel
  keep reading a line that leaves such a literal open.
- Document planning and query execution read string literals as the KerML `STRING_VALUE`
  terminal spells them rather than as Go string syntax, so a document title, paragraph text,
  column name or query argument may span lines or escape a single quote.

- **A MultiplicityPart may follow the specializations a usage states where its name would go.** `:>> values ordered : Real;`, `:>> values ordered nonunique;` and `:>> values [5] ordered = (…);` now parse as the grammar reads them (`FeatureSpecializationPart: FeatureSpecialization+ MultiplicityPart? FeatureSpecialization*`), in SysML and KerML files alike, with `ordered`/`nonunique` recorded on the feature; `ordered` was read as the usage's name and rejected as a reserved keyword.

- The SysML v1 migrator now reads the symbols Cameo lists under a part shape's `parts` and a
  state shape's `regions` — the nested parts, constraint usages, connectors and comments drawn
  inside a part on an internal block or parametric diagram — as symbols of their own, parented
  to the enclosing shape. They were dropped before, which re-parented the ports and connector
  ends drawn inside them to the outer part and recorded the dropped connectors' ends on it, so
  the outer part counted as a line and lost its placement too. Such diagrams now place the
  nested shapes inside their part, route the connectors between them and keep the outer part's
  `Layout`; the migration report counts them as positioned instead of lying on connections.

- **Instantiating a `ShapeItems::Box` — any `CuboidOrTriangularPrism` — no longer reports its edge and vertex groups as errors.** The library pins `tfe`, `tflv` and their siblings only by `binding [1] bind [0..1] tf.edges = [0..1] tfe` bindings, which link one unspecified value of each end, so the model leaves the groups open. The check `-instantiate` makes over a new object now treats such a feature as it treats an unset attribute — nothing to report — instead of printing `binding end cannot be resolved` for every group, while reading a group (`-e p.b.tfe`, `%eval`, `%features`, the gRPC and LSP evaluation requests) still answers with the typed diagnostic naming the binding and both ends rather than an empty or invented value. Everything the model does determine (`faces`, `edges`, `tf.edges`, dimensions, face-local values) evaluates as before.

- **A redefining feature answers to the redefined feature's short name.** A feature declaring neither a name nor a short name takes both from the feature it redefines (KerML 8.3.3.3 `Feature::effectiveShortName`), and name resolution now binds that short name as it already bound the name: with `part def ADS { part <tms> t : TMS; }` and `part gear : ADS { part :>> t { } }`, `gear.tms`, `gear::tms` and `sua.tms` for any `sua` that specializes, subsets or redefines `gear` bind to `gear`'s redefining feature rather than failing with `unresolved member: tms` or falling back to the hidden `ADS::t`. A redefining feature with a short name of its own (`part <t3> :>> t;`) keeps only that one, so the old short name no longer resolves through it, as in the pilot implementation.

- **An inherited action or state machine is named by the subtype's qualified name.** `%action BigPlant::fill p`, where `BigPlant :> Plant` inherits `fill`, failed with `unresolved reference: BigPlant::fill`; only the declaring type's spelling, `Plant::fill`, was accepted, although the notation resolves the inherited name. The REPL's qualified-name lookup, and with it the gRPC `ExecuteAction`, `ExecuteState` and `ExportGraphs` symbol IDs and the symbols a session's `Perform`, `Instantiate` and `Send` name, now resolve a name the index registers no declaration under through the model's member lookup, so the inherited member — or the redefinition the subtype makes of it — is found, and runs on the object as its own feature. `%state`, `%graphs`, `%sweep` and every other command naming a behavior share the lookup. A name that resolves to nothing fails as before, with its suggestions.

- **A trailing comma in a sequence expression parses.** `(a, b,)`, `(a,)`, `s#(1, 2,)` and `s[1,]` were rejected with `expected an expression` at the closing bracket; the OMG grammar admits the comma (KerMLExpressions.xtext `SequenceExpression`) and it adds no element, so `(a, b,)` is the pair and `(a,)` is `a`, as `(a)` is. An argument list still admits none (`f(a,)` remains an error), and the formatter keeps the comma as written.

- **SysON plugin: "Run with OpenSysML…" opens its dialog from the explorer palette.** SysON 2026.9 shows a tree item's tools in a palette rather than a context menu, and the frontend registered its contribution only at `treeItemContextMenuEntryOverrideExtensionPoint`, so the entry appeared in the palette but did nothing when chosen. `addOpenSysMLContributions` now also registers `RunWithOpenSysMLPaletteContribution` at `paletteToolOverrideExtensionPoint`, which renders the tool in the explorer palette, hides it from a search it does not match and opens the dialog; `@eclipse-sirius/sirius-components-palette` joins the package's optional peer dependencies.

- **The Rust and Python clients report how a private service that served no address really ended.** A child that closed its stdout without reporting an address was given 500 ms (Rust) or no time at all (Python) to become reapable before its status was read, so on a loaded machine one exiting on its own was reported as `exited with code None`, the same words as one killed for hanging or one ended by a signal. Both clients now wait up to `STOP_TIMEOUT` (5 s) for the child's own exit and name it (`exited with code 3`, `ended with signal 11`), and a child still running at the end of that grace is killed and reported as killed.

- Diagram layouts use private aliases in their owners when exposed names hide the private members they position.

- The distinguishability check of a scope's imported names no longer allocates once per member it compares. `memberNames` returned a two-element slice of the member's name and short name; when the short name began to be derived through the resolver, that slice escaped to the heap for every owned and imported member of every scope with imports, so re-analysing a document beside a large one allocated 7% more (`BenchmarkWorkspaceEditSmallDocBesideLarge`: 114.5k → 122.6k allocations). The names are a fixed pair now, and the check allocates as it did before.

- Name resolution takes the specialization chain of a scope's owner from the semantic model's memoized closure (`AllSupertypes`) instead of walking the generalization graph afresh, allocating a visited set and a chain, on every lookup that consults inherited imports. A body that reads a name through that path on each step — a calc's assignment loop reading its own attributes — allocates 23% less per invocation (`BenchmarkAssignmentLoopStep`: 9.5k → 7.5k allocations); a model without a memoized closure is still walked.

- A name read in a body no longer copies every frame it probes. A read resolves its name to a feature first and then asks each frame, innermost out, whether it holds that feature or binds the name without it; those probes (`heldName`, `bindsUnheld`, `has`, `lookup`) ran on a copy of the frame, a dozen words per frame per read. They now take the frame by pointer: the Integer loop rows (`BenchmarkIntegerArithmeticLoop`, `BenchmarkIntegerArithmeticNestedLoop`) run 4–5% faster, with the frame probes' share of a read's profile halved.

- The execution runtime resolves an unqualified name read from a scope once per model instead of asking the resolver on every read. A read resolves its name to the feature it denotes before it is answered from a frame, so a loop body paid a full scope walk per iteration for each name it read (`BenchmarkIntegerArithmeticLoop` +29% against 0.10.0); the memo takes that walk off every step after the first.

- An action execution's root frame sizes the index of the features it holds for all of them up front instead of growing it entry by entry. The index takes every feature of the action, inherited ones included, so each fresh execution paid a dozen allocations and a few kilobytes of discarded map storage for the regrowth (`BenchmarkNewRootFrame`: 43 → 36 allocations, 16.2 → 12.8 KiB; `BenchmarkExecuteActionFreshContext`: 345 → 338 allocations, 42.6 → 40.2 KiB per execution). The index itself, the record of which feature each binding is a value of, stays.
- Migrated document queries now spell names containing quotes, backslashes, or control characters as the document engine resolves them, so tables over elements such as a package named `Frank's Braindump` no longer render as error pages.
- Migrated document queries now name an action of a deferring state's do or exit behavior through the `buffer` or `flush` action the deferral encoding writes that behavior in, so a table or DocGen document listing such an action no longer renders as an error page.

## 0.10.0 — 2026-10-08
### Added

- **The action view draws the action's own parameters as pins on its frame, and its parameter bindings as edges.** An `action def` or action usage with `in`, `inout`, `out` or `return` parameters — declared, or a usage's inherited from its type — has them as pins on the frame boundary, inputs on the input side and outputs on the output side, as the SysML v2 action-flow notation shows them; a nested parameter's value naming the action's parameter (`in b = bread;`, `in bread = ToastBread::bread;`, `out x :>> x = y;`), an explicit `bind pack.boxed = toast;` and a `bind heat.t = pack.t;` between two nodes' pins are binding edges between the pins, in `dot`, `text`, `mermaid` and `plantuml` and through the service's render surfaces. The lowered `ActionGraph` carries the action's `Parameters` and its pins' `ValueBindings` for every consumer. The text form now lists each node's pins under it and names the pins an edge joins; Mermaid and PlantUML say which pins they cannot draw in a `not represented` notice.

- **Agent skills for every OpenSysML client.** `packaging/agent-skills/` adds `opensysml-go`,
  `opensysml-node`, `opensysml-java`, `opensysml-rust`, `opensysml-julia` and `opensysml-matlab`
  beside `opensysml-python`, so an LLM coding agent can parse, evaluate, instantiate, run and verify
  a model from any supported language: installing the client and resolving `sysml-grpc`, requiring
  a clean model, the language's value types and integer pitfalls, and how its errors separate a
  refused call from a reported model failure.

- **Agent skills for modeling with OpenSysML.** `packaging/agent-skills/` holds three skills an LLM
  coding agent can load to write SysML v2 that OpenSysML accepts, check and run it with `sysml`, and
  drive it from Python through `opensysml`, each pointing at the handbook pages it summarizes.

- **Load API element-form JSON files directly.** Explicitly named `.json` model files are converted to SysML notation before parsing; diagnostics refer to that notation.

- **The CLI page runs the real `sysml` REPL in the browser, with guided walkthroughs.** The
  page's toy shell over `sysml-engine` is replaced by `cmd/sysml` itself, built for js/wasm
  with `-tags sysml_prod` (about 12 MB gzipped, downloaded when started), on an in-memory
  filesystem holding the runtime-showcase models. Seven walkthroughs type their commands into
  the prompt: units, the Saturn V mass rollup, the delta-v analysis and sweep, failing
  requirements, stepping an action, a state machine on a clock and a user-written one. Up and
  Down recall earlier entries, kept across visits, and Tab completes through the REPL's own
  completer. `TestBrowserREPLWalkthroughs` runs every walkthrough through the page's host.
- **The landing-page diagram can be edited.** "Edit the model" opens the diagram's SysML
  source; the engine re-parses and re-instantiates it as you type and redraws the parts and
  interfaces, a parse error is shown with its line and column while the last good diagram
  stays up, and "Run the model" executes the edited model.

- **Add case and mixed diagrams.** Case views show use, analysis and verification cases with their actors, subjects, objectives and inclusion relationships; mixed views combine structure, state, action and case content on one canvas.

- **`sysml -compile` compiles Strings, enumerations, mixed numbers, run-time function values and closures, and spends the step budget.** A compiled C or Go calc now takes, computes and prints Strings with the `StringFunctions` the interpreter implements, enumeration literals by identity, Real-typed values that hold Integers and mixed Integer/Real sequences as the interpreter promotes them, and function values that are chosen, stored, returned, compared, held in sequences or close over a body's bindings. A compiled program counts evaluation steps as the interpreter does and fails with its `evaluation step limit exceeded` error at the same count (`OPENSYSML_MAX_STEPS`). Records and calcs read off an object are still refused, as is, for C alone, a closure capturing a String, sequence or function.

- **`sysml -compile` compiles records.** A compiled C or Go calc now constructs `attribute def` values (positionally, by label, with defaults), reads their scalar, sequence and nested features, compares them by identity as the interpreter does, and invokes, passes, stores and compares calcs read off a record (`s.scale`). A record as the program's result or command-line parameter is refused with a typed error naming it, since the interpreter prints a record by a session instance number; a feature read off one (`p.x`) is returned normally. A required feature a construction leaves unbound holds the interpreter's `<unset>` value: it prints `<unset>`, is not null and not empty, equals only itself, and fails with the interpreter's error where a value is required. The C target refuses a record holding a String or a collection.

- Statements in a constraint or requirement body (`assign`, `if`, `while`/`loop`/`for`, and body-local declarations interleaved with them) now run as steps of the check's own performance before its conditions are evaluated, in declaration order: locals live in a fresh frame per check, the constraint's own parameters are copied into it, and each condition of the body is judged in the state the steps left. Assignments reaching outside the performance — a name it holds no feature for, a chained or qualified target — and effects it cannot perform (`send`, `perform`, `terminate`) are refused with the typed `ErrConstraintExternalAssignment`/`ErrConstraintEffect`, as are stated successions (`ErrStatementNotExecutable`), and a body stating steps but no result expression reports `ErrNoConditions`. The solver refuses to translate the steps of such a body rather than solving its conditions as if they ran.

- **Convert to a compact form of API JSON over the service.** `ConvertRequest.compact` writes `api-json` from notation as one `api-json-compact/1` object in place of the element array: a table of the element ids, each written once, and the elements with every reference spelled as an index into it, unindented. `omit_derived` also leaves out the metamodel's derived properties, except the ones `keep_derived` names. Expanded it is the element array, and it composes with `documents`. Ids and references are most of the bytes of a large model, so a client that reads one receives a fraction of them. Reported as the `convert_compact` capability.

- **Convert only some documents of a model over the service.** `ConvertRequest.documents` names documents of a `model_hash` parsed from several, and only the elements they declare are written; a reference into another document of the model links the `@id` that document's own conversion writes, and an element both declare is still refused. A client that edits one document of a large model, such as a project over the libraries it imports, converts that document again rather than the whole model, and replaces its elements by `@id`. Reported as the `convert_documents` capability.

- **D2 is a rendering form.** `-render-form d2`, `%render <view> d2 [palette]`, `"form": "d2"` on
  `opensysml/render` and VS Code's *Export Diagram* write a tree, interconnection, state, action or
  sequence rendering as a [D2](https://d2lang.com) diagram in the Pilot visualizer's B&W look —
  nested containers for a part's parts and its drawn ports, pseudostate glyphs for control nodes,
  D2's `sequence_diagram` for a sequence, the named palettes as fills, `-render-link` templates as
  `link:` attributes — and `-render-all` writes `.d2` files. Documents take `-diagram-form d2` (`%render-document <name> d2`, `diagramForm:
  "d2"`): a ` ```d2 ` fence in Markdown, `<pre class="d2">` in HTML, and in a PDF the figure drawn
  by the `d2` executable that `OPENSYSML_D2` names (or `d2` on `PATH`), kept as source under a
  notice without one. `scripts/download-doc-pdf-toolchain.sh` provisions a pinned D2.

- **A data-import demo.** `examples/data-import-demo` imports a Python script's CSV, JSON, JSON Lines and TSV output into a rover model with `-import … -convert sysml -o`, a mapping file for the JSON report, `-import-dry-run` and `%import`.

- **`-import` and `%import` set feature values from CSV, TSV, JSON and JSON Lines files.** Each row names an element and the values its features take, with units as `mass [kg]`; a declared value is replaced and an inherited one redefined, including a feature reached through an inherited part (`Fleet::car1::engine`) or an alias. An optional JSON mapping file maps differently named columns and nested fields, each `-import-map` and `-import-format` qualifying the `-import` before it, and `-import-dry-run`/`dry-run` previews the edit. JSON numbers and Booleans may be written as strings. The file is checked whole and applied as one edit, so a row, value or unit the model refuses leaves it unchanged; `-convert sysml -o` writes the imported model. See `docs/manual/importing-data.md`.

- **`MigrationMetadata::DeferredKeeper` marks the keeping accept of a deferred signal.** The SysML v1 migrator and the PSSM referee write the accept of each keeping loop of the standard deferred-signal encoding as `#MigrationMetadata::DeferredKeeper action receive accept kept : Sig;`, so the accept that keeps the signal for the state is declared rather than recognized by its shape. An accept node takes prefix metadata like any other usage (`#M action a accept e : E;`), as the grammar's `PrefixMetadataMember` allows.
