- **The `deferred-keeper-unmarked` lint reports a keeping loop written without the marker.** An accept of a deferred signal at the root of the do action of a state annotated `MigrationMetadata::DeferredEvent` where no accept of that signal carries `MigrationMetadata::DeferredKeeper` — the shape the migrator wrote before the marker existed, not an ordinary consumer written beside a marked keeper — is reported as a warning in every mode, since the runtime now runs it as an ordinary accept that consumes the occurrence rather than keeping it; the model still analyses and runs, and re-migrating it or writing the marker on the accept clears the warning. The annotations are known by their resolved type, however the model spells them. The lint is switched off like the others, by its code.

- The SysML v1 migrator writes a DocGen `TableExpressionColumn` written in an OCL subset — navigation chains, `->size()`, `->select`, `->exists`, `->collect`, `oclAsType`, `=`/`<>`, `and`/`or`/`not`, literals — as a `Column` computed over the row when the document's queries run: `_typedElementOfType->size()` counts the features typed by the row, a connector's `end…propertyPath` walks the ends' feature chains to the parts they pass through, their types and flow direction. An expression outside the subset is still refused with the expression quoted.
- A DocGen column whose own chain collects before it reads (`CollectOwnedElements`, `CollectTypes`, a metaclass, stereotype or name filter, then `Name`/`Documentation`) is written as a `Column` applying the chain's query operations to the row and reading the attribute of every element collected; a step with no query spelling, or whose result only the elements known at migration spell (`CollectByAssociation`), keeps refusing the column with the step quoted.
- A DocGen `StructuredQuery` or `Dynamic View` with `loop = true` is written once per element the chain collected, each pass over that element alone — one table (list, figure) per element behind its bullet, or one section per element when `createSections` is set — instead of one table over all of them.
- `DocumentQueries::Distinct` keeps each element of a collection once, in first-seen order, and the query evaluator runs the standard library's `SequenceFunctions` and `ControlFunctions` (`size`, `last`, `excluding`, `select`, `collect`, `exists`, …) over row-relative collections in a `Column` cell — a connection's `connectorEnd`, an end's `chainingFeature`, `RelatedElements(source = row, …)`.

- **The documentation site gains a Downloads page** listing release artifacts and
  package-manager routes.

- **The in-browser engine can render declared views and targeted pseudo-views, with ports and edge endpoint pins in both engine and language-server render data.** Diagram clients can draw a visitor's edited model from the same semantic rendering used by the workspace.

- **A design note on exact Rational values beside a binary64 Real** (`docs/internals/design/exact-rational-literals.md`). It maps where a number is represented today — a `LiteralReal` rounds to a `float64` in `ParseReal`, `RealArith` and `IntQuotient` compute in binary64, `FormatReal` prints the shortest round-tripping decimal — against the two references, the pinned pilot (binary64, so `0.1 + 0.2` is `0.30000000000000004`) and sysml-toolkit (exact, so `0.3`), proposes a `big.Rat` value kind for literals and `Rational`-typed features with the `Real` type as the one place rounding enters, costs it against the tree, and recommends leaving the arithmetic as it is unless the project adopts toolkit parity as a conformance goal. No evaluator behavior changes.

- **Return state-machine execution traces from the service, clients, and browser engine.** Opt-in traces expose typed event, state, transition, and choice records for a single run; a failed run keeps the records made before the failure on the client's execution error.

- **A `GeneralView` draws the requirement, definition and usage, and package graphs its filters select.** A GeneralView whose element filters name the metaclasses the OMG library lists for a specialization — `filter @SysML::RequirementUsage;`, `expose P::**[@SysML::Definition or @SysML::Usage];`, `filter @SysML::Package;`, or an `or` of them — is drawn as a graph of nodes and edges in `text`, `mermaid`, `dot`, `plantuml` and `d2`, under the Pilot and Cameo styles and the palettes: requirements with their id and text and the satisfy, verify, derive, refine, allocate, specialization and typing edges between them; definitions and usages with specialization, typing, composition and reference edges; packages with containment and imports. An unfiltered GeneralView, and one whose filters are of any other shape, stays the containment tree it was. An opt-in `verdicts` overlay — `-render-overlay verdicts`, `%render <view> <form> verdicts`, `"overlay": "verdicts"` on `opensysml/render`, a document `Diagram`'s `overlay` — runs the verification cases verifying each drawn requirement and labels and colours it by their verdicts. A source link template (`-render-link`, `link=<template>`, `linkTemplate`) links the graphs' nodes and edges to their declarations as it does every other kind's. A GeneralView filtered on a case metaclass — `filter @SysML::UseCaseUsage;`, `@SysML::CaseDefinition`, or any metaclass specializing `CaseDefinition` or `CaseUsage` — is drawn as the case diagram, by the case writer, needing only the standard library: its every form, source links included, is what a `CaseView` exposing the same elements writes, but for the provenance line. `CaseView` is the shorter spelling, with the OpenSysML library; a mixed diagram still needs `MixedView`. When filters select more than one graph, requirement wins over package, package over case and case over definition and usage. See `examples/general-views-demo`.

- **Add an advisory grammar-to-metaclass map.** The generated reports relate grammar productions to recognized metaclasses, feature assignments and existing corpus evidence without gating CI.

- **The lowered graph of an action or state machine is a public artifact.** `sysml model.sysml -graphs Pipeline::terrain [-o graphs.json]`, `%graphs <name>` in the REPL and the service's `ExportGraphs(model_hash, subject)` RPC (capability `export_graphs`) write the canonical `graphs:1` JSON an external analysis engine is sent — the subject's `ActionGraph`/`StateGraph` IR and that of every behavior it performs, with nodes, edges, flows, parameters, guards, triggers, effects and source spans — so a tool that drives a pipeline from a model, such as a workflow-DAG generator, reads the same lowered form the runtime executes rather than reparsing the notation. Every client exposes it: `Model.export_graphs(subject)` in Python, Julia and Rust, `model.exportGraphs(subject)` in Node, Java and MATLAB, `Client.ExportGraphs` in Go. An unknown subject is `NOT_FOUND`; an element that is no behavior, or a name the model declares more than once, is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`.

- **Render filtered `GridView`s as relationship matrices.** Matrix views show satisfy, verify, allocation, connection, derivation, refinement and dependency relationships in text, Markdown, CSV and TSV, including unnamed exposed members; Mermaid, DOT, PlantUML and D2 are refused as graph-only forms. Ordinary tables and other renderings keep their existing output.

- **Expose machine-readable view rendering through gRPC and every client.** `RenderView` returns ordered nodes, edges, ports, source spans, optional layout data, table rows and notes without requiring diagram pictures.

- Indexed connector ends, an OpenSysML extension: `connect s.y#(1) to k.u` attaches one element of the collection-valued feature `s.y` (1-based, as `#(` indexes everywhere) at an end of multiplicity one typed by the element's type, on the last segment of the end's feature chain, in `connect`, `connection … connect`, `interface`, `allocate`/`allocation`, `bind`/`binding`, structural `flow` and the KerML `connector`/`binding` forms. The index must be an Integer and a constant index must lie within the multiplicity governing the feature, declared or inherited by redefinition; at run time a connector or binding over the end addresses only the selected element — a binding between two selected elements checks that they agree and assigns through neither — and an out-of-range or non-Integer index is a typed connector-end error. The end is reported as `nonstandard-notation` (an error under `-strict`), is written back by the formatter and the notation converters, and the RDF and API JSON mappings carry the index as `sysx:endElement` beside the standard reference subsetting of the whole feature.

- **An install script for every supported platform.** `curl -fsSL https://opensysml.org/install.sh | sh`
  on Linux and macOS, `irm https://opensysml.org/install.ps1 | iex` on Windows: each works out
  the operating system and architecture, downloads the matching release bundle together with
  `SHA256SUMS.txt`, installs nothing whose digest does not match, and runs the installed
  `sysml` and `sysml-lsp` before reporting where they went. Options pick a release tag or the
  nightly snapshot, the tools (`sysml`, `sysml-lsp`, `sysml-grpc` or `all`), the destination
  and a release mirror; `--dry-run` shows the choice, and `--verify-signature` additionally
  checks the manifest's cosign signature. The Windows script installs the portable ZIP for the
  current user, preferring the signed build when the release carries one and its Authenticode
  signatures verify on this machine, and puts it on the user `PATH`. The scripts live at the repository root, are published from the documentation
  site, and are tested in CI against a release served from localhost.

- **The Java client gains the Python client's newer surface.** `Model.edit()` returns a fluent `Editor` (with `Editor.Body` for action-body statements) over the existing `Edit` records; `Model.renderDocument` renders HTML as well as Markdown (`DocumentForm`, gated on `render_document_html`); `ConversionOptions.withIdForm` forwards `id_form`; `Conversion.write`/`EditResult.save` write files; `Model.find`/`get`/`lookup`/`contains`, `documents`, `ok`, `errors` and `requireNoErrors` match the Python model; `ConnectionOptions.requireCapabilities` and a release check at open throw `CapabilityException` (now carrying a `remedy()`) or `StaleServiceException`; and `NOT_FOUND` refusals for a model or file arrive as `ModelNotFoundException`/`ModelFileNotFoundException`, a missing symbol as `SymbolNotFoundException` with near-name suggestions.

- **Expanded the Julia client** with capability-aware authoring, pinned SHA-256 service downloads, typed model generation, and wrappers for the service API.
- **Changed Julia parsing** so `strict=true` raises `ModelError` for error diagnostics; use `strict_conformance=true` to request strict conformance checks from the service.

- **A Jupyter kernel for SysML v2, `sysml-jupyter-kernel`, installable with `pip install
  jupyter-opensysml-kernel`.** The kernel runs notebook cells through the same REPL session
  `sysml` drives, so declarations accumulate across cells, bare expressions evaluate, and
  every `%` command — instantiating, running, debugging, sweeping, rendering — works as at
  the prompt, with the model's state kept between cells. Views render as rich output
  (`text/vnd.mermaid`, Graphviz SVG, Markdown, CSV), `%render-document` as Markdown or, with
  a trailing `html`, as HTML, and `%features … json` as JSON. Tab completion, `Shift-Tab`
  inspection and the console's completeness detection use the REPL's own completer and
  continuation rules; interrupting a cell stops the run at its next step, and later cells
  run on. The package is a wheel per platform (Linux x64 and arm64, macOS Intel and Apple
  Silicon, Windows x64) that bundles the release's kernel binary and registers the `sysml`
  kernelspec, carrying the OpenSysML mark as its icon, under the environment it is installed
  into, so `pip install` alone adds the kernel to Jupyter; from the sdist,
  `python -m jupyter_opensysml_kernel install` downloads the binary against the digests the
  package ships and registers it. The binary is also a release asset
  (`sysml-jupyter-kernel-<os>-<arch>`), installable with `install.sh --tools
  sysml-jupyter-kernel`, and `sysml-jupyter-kernel -install` registers it by hand. A
  conda-forge recipe is kept under `packaging/conda`.

- **A declaration keyword only KerML spells — `connector`, `class`, `struct`, `datatype`, `classifier`, `subclassifier`, `feature`, `step`, `expr`, `bool`, `behavior`, `function`, `predicate`, `metaclass`, `assoc`, `assoc struct`, `interaction`, `inv`, `multiplicity` — is now reported as `kerml-notation` in a `.sysml` file**, the way `namespace` and `featured by` already were: a warning by default naming the SysML spelling where one exists (`connector c from a to b;` → `connection`/`connect`, `class` → `occurrence def`, `inv` → `constraint`), an error under `-strict`, pointed at the keyword, with no `-fix` since a keyword swap changes the declared kind. The members of a reported declaration are not reported again. The declaration stays parsed, and a `.kerml` file, or a snippet the REPL loaded from one, stays silent; the pinned pilot rejects each of these in a `.sysml` file with `no viable alternative`.

- **The landing page's state machine can be debugged in the browser.** A Debug panel under the
  diagram sends `Commit`, `Pull`, `Check` and `Push` to `ModelJourney`, steps through the
  `ExecuteState` trace forwards and backwards or plays it at a chosen speed, and lights the
  current state and transition on the diagram. It lists every trace record, marks events no
  transition accepted, and shows the events still waiting. `ModelJourney` is now a hub around
  Flexo with two free choices, the start and each `Pull` out of Flexo, and a seed field
  picks those branches reproducibly.

- The landing page's stack diagram zooms (the +/− buttons, <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+scroll, or a pinch) and pans by dragging its background, and a "Download PNG" button saves the diagram as drawn at twice its size.

- The language server answers `textDocument/signatureHelp`, `textDocument/codeLens` and `textDocument/inlayHint`. Signature help lists the parameters a calculation, function or action call binds, with their types, the ones that have a default bracketed, and the parameter the cursor's argument binds active, whether positional or named. Code lenses count the references to each definition and offer a *Run* or *Evaluate* command, naming the element, on each executable action, state, calc, constraint and requirement declared outside another behavior. Inlay hints show the inferred type of a feature declared without one, when it is more specific than `DataValue`, and the constant a feature's value evaluates to from the model alone. All three are advertised in `initialize`.
- The VS Code extension runs a *Run* or *Evaluate* lens as a task: `sysml -action`, `-state`, `-calc`, `-constraint` or `-requirement` over the workspace folders after saving every unsaved model file, with `sysml` found beside the `sysml-lsp` in use, in the workspace's `bin/`, or on `PATH`.

- **Expanded the MATLAB/Octave client with model inspection, analysis, document queries, and chainable source edits.** The client now exposes these service operations and typed edit results without generated protobuf code.

- The Mermaid form fills nodes from `-render-palette` (and a document's `Diagram::palette`, `%render … mermaid <palette>`, the `palette` of `opensysml/render`) as the DOT and PlantUML forms do, by keyword family with the same hex per node: a flowchart `style` statement per node, a state diagram `classDef`/`class` pair. Previously Mermaid noted the palette as not represented. A Mermaid sequence diagram, which fills no participant, still notes it.

- **The SysML v1 migrator performs interaction uses and checks state invariants.** An interaction use is a `perform` of the referenced interaction's scenario at its place among the messages, its arguments bound to the scenario's parameters and a message through an actual gate carried by the performed scenario; an interaction holding one migrates and executes instead of being refused. A state invariant is an `assert constraint` the scenario orders among its steps, so the run checks it where the diagram places it.
- **An `assert constraint` a succession orders among an action's steps is checked when the flow reaches it.** A false condition ends the run with the assertion's violation; one no succession names is still not a step.

- **The SysML v1 migrator writes object flows as succession flows.** An object flow between two actions is `succession flow of T from a.out to b.in`, which orders the actions and carries the value when the source completes; a guarded one is OMG's guarded succession to the flow itself, `first a if g then f; succession flow f of T from a.out to b.in;`, so a false guard moves no value, a guard of literal `true` is no guard, and a flow into a «stream» parameter stays a `flow`. A flow into or out of a fork, join or merge names the node's `inputObjectN`/`outputObjectN` features instead of being rerouted between pins, and an action several object flows reach waits for them without a synthesized join. Edge weights, all UML's default in the measured models, are not written.
- **Values pass through forks, joins and merges, and several succession flows may leave one action.** A control node holds what a flow delivers to its input features and passes it on when a token reaches it: a fork copies it to every branch, a join gathers one value of each input, a merge passes on the one that arrived. Each succession flow leaving an action starts its target beside the action's other successions, as after a fork.
- **A succession may lead to a succession flow.** `first a if g then f; succession flow f from a.y to b.v;` gates the flow on the guard: the runtime and the model checker move the value and order `b` only when `g` holds, so a false guard leaves `b` waiting for that pin (or running on another path's value) rather than on the rejected one. The model form records the guarded succession as the `gate` of the edge and the flow, and action views draw it at its own declaration.

- **The v1 migration report says where a run will stop on a construct the tool simulated and v2 cannot run as written.** A state's entry or exit behavior, or a transition effect, whose activity waits for the clock (a duration constraint on itself or on one of its nodes, an accept of a time event, or a call of an activity, or of an operation whose method is one, that has one) is reported as an approximation naming the wait, since a v2 entry, exit or effect is performed whole at the instant it is triggered. A trigger or accept of a signal that no send action of the model sends — neither it nor a signal specializing it — and only a button of the tool's UI prototype posts is reported as waiting for a message nothing in the model posts, and a «SimulationConfig» whose `UI` names a prototype frame is reported as taking no input from it. A script body that reads a property declaring no default before it assigns it is reported as an approximation naming the property: in v2 the property holds no value until assigned, so a run reaching the read first stops, where the tool's script engine reads null and its arithmetic takes 0; a read after assignments each made only when a name admitting no value holds one is reported the same way, for the run in which none of those names holds one.

- **A SysML v1 model is migrated with `-migrate`, its own verb.** `sysml Model.mdzip -migrate sysml -o Model.sysml -migration-report Model.report.txt` reads a Cameo/MagicDraw `.mdzip`, a UML XMI `.xmi` or an Eclipse UML2 `.uml` export — `-from xmi|uml|mdzip` names one whose extension does not — and writes SysML v2 notation or RDF Turtle through the same mapping as before, byte for byte. The verb says what happens: a migration is ledgered, not lossless, and every v1 element lands in the report as mapped, approximated, unmapped or skipped. The companion flags `-migration-report`, `-migration-results`, `-layout`, `-image-base-url` and `-strict` accompany `-migrate`; a v2 input is refused with a pointer at `-convert`. The help has a Migration topic of its own, and the man page follows it.
- **The service migrates with a `Migrate` RPC.** It takes a `file_path` or inline `content` bytes (so a `.mdzip` archive travels), `from_format`, `to_format` and the command's options — `report`, `results`, `layout_path`/`layout_content`, `image_base_url`, `strict` — and answers the migrated content, the canonical formats, the experimental notice and a `MigrationReport` whose summary and verdict counts always come back, with every element's `MigrationEntry` and the report text when `report` is set, the `-migration-results` sidecar when `results` is, and the image files the migration extracts. The `migrate` capability advertises it; the conformance suite covers the migration and both refusals.
- **Every client migrates.** Go `MigrateFile`/`MigrateSource` with `WithV1Format`, `WithMigrationReport`, `WithMigrationResults`, `WithLayoutFile`/`WithLayout`, `WithImageBaseURL` and `WithStrict`; Python `Connection.migrate()` and `opensysml.migrate()` answering a `Migration` with its `MigrationReport`; Java `Connection.migrate`/`migrateFile` with `MigrationOptions` answering a `Migration`, a failed migration a `MigrationException`; Node `connection.migrate()` answering a `Migration` whose image files `save()` writes beside the notation, with `convert()` refusing a v1 model by format or extension; Rust `Connection::migrate(to_format, &MigrateSource, &MigrateOptions)` answering a `Migration` whose `write()` puts the image files beside the notation; Julia `migrate_file`/`migrate_source` answering a `Migration` that `save` writes the same way; MATLAB `opensysml.migrate(conn, toFormat, 'filePath', …)` answering a `Migration` with `write(path)` and `byVerdict`. Every typed `convert` refuses a v1 model by format or extension, and the conformance runners of every client read binary fixtures.

- **A succession outside a behavior body now orders the performances it relates.** Examples
  are `first b.g then b.m;` in a package, `first grip then move;` in a part def, and
  `first r::move then r::grip;`, which orders every `Robot`.
  - The succession is featured as KerML §8.3.4.5.3 derives: by its owning type, or by the
    innermost common featuring type of its ends.
  - A behavior the runtime starts when an object is materialized is held until its predecessor
    has ended. Unrelated performances keep every interleaving, and snapshots, held images and
    checked state keys carry what a held behavior waits for.
  - An explicit `perform x.beh.start` or `-action` start that would break the order fails with
    `succession-order-violated`, and a cycle fails with `succession-order-cycle`; the start is
    never reordered.
  - A succession with a behavior end that orders nothing in a run is reported with
    `succession-orders-nothing` and the reason. Examples are a requirement end, or a missing or
    repeated performance, which is open under KERML-29.
  - Ends with no common featuring type are rejected with `connector-type-featuring`.

- `make build-engine`, `make build-core` and `make build-syntax` build `sysml-engine`, `sysml-core` and `sysml-syntax` as native executables that serve their JSON-RPC over standard input and output. They are opt-in: `make build`, `make install` and the release artifacts do not include them. See `docs/reference/wasm.md`.

- **Nightly snapshots of the Python and Node clients.** The nightly workflow now builds
  `opensysml` and `@openmbee/opensysml` (with its five platform packages and the WASM
  package) from the same commit as the binaries and publishes them to PyPI and npm through
  trusted publishing, as development versions a default install never picks up:
  `<next release>.dev<yyyymmdd>` on PyPI, `<next release>-nightly.<yyyymmdd>.g<commit>`
  under the `nightly` dist-tag on npm, both derived from the version the tree declares by
  `client/python/scripts/snapshot_version.py`. Each night is kept for 14 days as the
  prerelease `nightly-<yyyymmdd>-<commit>`, whose `sysml-grpc` the wheel pins by digest and
  installs on its own; `nightly` remains the moving alias of the newest night, and nothing is
  marked latest. See [Nightly snapshots](https://runtime.opensysml.org/project/nightly/).

- **The Node/TypeScript client covers the whole service surface.** `@openmbee/opensysml` grew from the five v1 RPCs to everything the Python client does: `parseSources` for a model of several documents, `convert`/`save`, `query` and the document calls, `executeAction`/`executeState` and the `explore*` forms, `verifyConstraint`/`verifyRequirement`/`verifySatisfaction`/`validateInstance`/`calc`/`runAnalysis`/`runSweep` with `listEngines`, `model.edit()`/`connection.applyEdits` for source-preserving edits with the typed `EditError` family, `version`/`requireCapabilities` on `connect()` raising `StaleServiceError`, and the `opensysml-generate` CLI for typed view modules. `connection.rpc` remains the escape hatch for anything not wrapped.

- **The Node and browser clients can connect to the combined `sysml-wasm` module without a service.** Node defaults to a worker thread, with inline execution available, and browsers can run inline or in a supplied worker.

- **The site and the README carry the OpenSysML logo.** The circle badge appears in the
  landing-page hero and at the top of the README, which shows a white version in GitHub's dark
  mode; the header logo and the favicon are its centre mark.

- **OpenSysML MDK runs inside MDK's DocGen documents.** The distribution now drops a bridge jar into MDK's `plugins/org.openmbee.mdk/extensions/` directory with one «JavaExtension» query per operation (Instantiate, Execute action, Execute state, Verify, Evaluate calc, Run analysis); a viewpoint method that applies the matching stereotype gets, per target element, a summary line, an outcome table, a diagnostics table and the state schedule rendered as DocBook. *OpenSysML ▸ Add MDK DocGen extension stereotypes* on the model root creates and applies the profile holding the stereotypes a project needs. Targets may be UML or SysML v2 elements. The bridge finds the plugin through Cameo's plugin registry and exchanges only JDK types with it, so the plugin keeps its own classloader and nothing loads when MDK is absent.

- **`ParseSources` can name the documents an edit reached.** Under the new `parse_sources_affected` capability, a request carrying the hash of the model it was answered last (`base_model_hash`) is answered with `affected`: the documents whose results may differ from that model's. Every other document converts exactly as it did, so a client that keeps each document's `Convert` output (with `documents`) re-converts only the affected ones after an edit.

- **The spec-vs-pilot gap register records where the pinned pilot's own source admits a departure from the specification.** New items cover a KerML metaclass typing a SysML metadata usage (KERML-90), the implied subsetting of decision-node and merge-node successions (SYSML21-306), the default `[1..1]` multiplicity (SYSML21-185), the case-usage declaration production (SYSML2-783), five validation constraints the pilot marks `TODO` but implements, variation parameters and stakeholders, and the multiplicity-bound non-negativity OCL (KERML-199). `omg-issues.md` adds the pilot's name-only membership distinguishability and a drafted KERML-90 follow-up, and the census marks the two variation-membership constraints ⚠️ approximate, since OpenSysML exempts an objective in a variation that their OCL rejects.

- Derive additional reflective KerML and SysML metaclass features and state their validation constraints in the bundled `SysMLValidation` library.

- **Relationships written as notation (`:`, `:>`, `:>>`, `::>`, `=>`, `~`, `conjugates`, `subsets`, `redefines`, `references`, `crosses`) now reflect as metaobjects.** Each written relationship on an element is reachable from `x meta` and the reflective feature layer as a `Subclassification`, `FeatureTyping`, `Subsetting`, `Redefinition`, `ReferenceSubsetting`, `CrossSubsetting`, `Conjugation`, `Specialization` or `ConjugatedPortTyping` object with its `source`/`target` ends and owner-side features (`ownedSpecialization`, `ownedSubsetting`, `ownedRedefinition`, `ownedConjugator`, …) in textual order, so the `SysMLValidation` library now states nine relationship-valued constraints that `-self-check` evaluates (50 constraints in total). Membership relationships (`ownedRelationship`, `membership`, `ownedImport`) are not yet derived, an extended definition (`#service def X`) classifies as Definition, and the `includes` notation of `include use case uc : UC` reflects no relationship.

- **Link rendered diagram elements to their source.** PlantUML and DOT link nodes and edges, Mermaid links supported nodes and participants, and CLI, REPL, LSP and document renderings accept source-link templates.

- **An opt-in `rounded-real-literal` lint.** A decimal literal written to a feature typed by `Real` that no binary64 value equals can now be reported where it is written, naming the double the feature holds: `attribute x : Real = 0.1;` warns that `0.1` is rounded to `0.10000000000000001`, while `0.25` and `1.5e3`, which a double holds exactly, are silent. It covers a feature's value, a parameter's default, a redefinition of a `Real` feature, an `assign` and each element of a sequence written out, but not a computed value. Since most decimals written to a `Real` round, it is off by default: switch it on with `-enable-lint rounded-real-literal`, `%lint rounded-real-literal on` or the new `enabledLints` editor setting (`model.WithEnabledLints` in Go). Like every lint it is a warning in every mode, and `-disable-lint` wins over `-enable-lint`.

- **Render recorded runs as timelines and message sequences.** The CLI and REPL can write a behavior's state occupancy and ordered messages as text, Mermaid or PlantUML without adding model-view vocabulary. Source links connect sequence participants to their type declarations and PlantUML timeline lanes and single-state spans to their declarations.
- A terminated machine's `terminate` event also appears in state-run traces.

- **The Rust client reaches every service RPC through a typed API.** `opensysml` adds `parse_sources` with `SourceDocument`, `convert` with `IdForm`, structured and OSLC `query`, `run_document_query` and `render_document`, action and state execution and exploration, verification with bounds, standings and witnesses, `validate_instance`, `calc`, `run_analysis`, `run_sweep`, `list_engines`, and an `Editor`/`Body` builder over `ApplyEdits` that edits a model of several documents. Every call checks the capabilities it and its argument values need before it is sent, naming the remedy; failures are typed (`Error::Execution` with its `FailureReason`, `Error::WrongKind`, `Error::Edit`, `Error::AnalysisRun` keeping the partial result); `Model` gains `find`, `get`, `lookup` and `contains`, and symbols their type facts. The Rust conformance runner drives every RPC through these calls.

- **`-self-check-package <QualifiedName>` applies the constraint defs of a package the loaded files declare in the same element walk as the bundled `SysMLValidation` rules.** Repeatable and implying `-self-check`: a rule is a `constraint def` whose first `in` parameter is typed by a `SysML::…` or `KerML::…` metaclass, applied to every element the metaclass conforms to and named by its qualified name when it fails; rules that cannot apply warn, unknown packages refuse the run, and a rule package's own elements are never checked. See `examples/self-check-rules/`.

- **A model can read whether a state usage is active: `fill.isActive`.** The bundled non-normative `StateActivity` library (`OpenSysML Libraries/StateActivity.kerml`) declares `isActive : Boolean[1]` as a derived feature `featured by States::StateAction`, so after `private import StateActivity::*;` any state usage reached by name or feature chain reads `x.isActive` from a guard, a constraint, an `assert constraint`, a calc, an assignment or a parent or sibling state's behavior. It is `true` exactly while the state is in the active configuration of the machine running it — a composite while any substate is, a parallel region's state together with its siblings, the state itself during its own entry and exit actions — and `false` while a transition's effect runs (its source has exited, its target not yet entered, so a self-transition's effect reads `false`), before the machine starts and after it ends. The value is read from the executor's active configuration; a write to it is refused as a write to any derived feature is, and the code generator reports such a read as not compilable. Neither `States.sysml` nor `StatePerformances.kerml` declares the feature, so without the import `x.isActive` stays `unresolved member` as before, the import is reported as `nonstandard-notation` (an error under `-strict`), and completion and hover on `x.` offer and type it only under the import.

- **The `OpenSysML Libraries/StateMachines.sysml` library re-expresses the state-machine notation extensions through standard metadata.** A model importing `StateMachines::*` spells a choice, junction, shallow-history or deep-history pseudostate as an annotated state usage (`#choice state pick;`, `#junction state j;`, `#shallowHistory state h;`, `#deepHistory state h;`), so the file is grammar-conforming SysML v2. Both forms lower to the same graph as the `choice`/`junction`/`history` keywords, which remain accepted for now.

- **A production `sysml` build, without the optional features.** `-tags sysml_prod` (`make build-prod`, `make build-wasm-prod`) builds `sysml` without SysML v1 migration, repository sync, native compilation, the HTML and PDF document forms with the Graphviz drawer, FMU import, profiling, and the REPL's `%features … json`; one `sysml_no<group>` tag leaves out one group. Such a build refuses a left-out flag with status 2, as `sysml: -compile is not available in this build (built without codegen)`, and leaves it out of `-help` and `-man`. The default build is unchanged.

- `sysml-core`: a validation-focused WebAssembly command serving `ParseSources`, `ParseFile`, `GetDiagnostics` and `GetSymbol` without protobuf, Connect or the execution runtime. It embeds the standard library, runs validation passes for clean parses, and exposes a synchronous `globalThis.sysmlCore.call(method, paramsJSON)` surface in JavaScript; native and WASI builds use JSON-RPC over stdio. Execution remains on `sysml-engine`, and other service methods remain on `sysml-grpc`. See `docs/reference/wasm.md`.

- `sysml-engine`: a new command that serves the execution RPCs — `ParseSources`, `Evaluate`, `Instantiate`, `ExecuteAction`, `ExecuteState` — with the same JSON the `sysml-grpc` service emits, but without the protobuf and analysis machinery, so its WebAssembly builds stay small enough to embed. On `GOOS=js` it installs `globalThis.sysmlEngine.call(method, paramsJSON)` for a page to call synchronously; natively and under WASI it speaks the service's JSON-RPC over standard input and output. Exploration, verification, document queries and tools stay on `sysml-grpc`. See `docs/reference/wasm.md`.

- **Add `-self-check` over the `SysMLValidation` library.** It applies the library's 41 reflective constraints to workspace elements and reports reflective features it cannot evaluate as unevaluated rather than as violations.

- `sysml-syntax`: a new command that serves the purely syntactic RPCs — `Parse` for a document's syntax diagnostics, `Format` for a `sysml`→`sysml` re-indent of inline content, and `Tokens` for LSP-encoded lexical semantic tokens — with no name resolution and no standard library, so its WebAssembly builds stay small enough to embed. On `GOOS=js` it installs `globalThis.sysmlSyntax.call(method, paramsJSON)` for a page to call synchronously; natively and under WASI it speaks the same `Content-Length`-framed JSON-RPC over standard input and output as `sysml-engine`. See `docs/reference/wasm.md`.

- **Added a SysML v1 to v2 transformation census.** `docs/project/sysml-v1-transformation-census.md`
  enumerates every mapping class of OMG's pinned SysML v1 to v2 transformation model and ties each
  to the migrator code and test that carries it out, with a per-verdict status; `go run -C tools
  ./cmd/transformation-census -check` gates the document, its citations and the scope measurements
  against the committed baseline, and `./scripts/download-sysml-v1tov2.sh` provisions the pinned
  model the extraction is compared against.

- **Publish the combined WebAssembly module.** Stable and nightly releases ship the module and matching Go
  runtime, npm publishes them as `@openmbee/opensysml-wasm`, and Node `connectWasm()` resolves the
  installed package automatically.

- **`sysml-wasm` combines the execution and validation JSON-RPC surfaces in one WebAssembly module.** It routes parsing and symbol facts to the core and evaluation and execution to the engine, shares a single standard-library snapshot, and reports supported capabilities through `GetServerInfo`. JavaScript hosts call `globalThis.sysmlWasm.call(method, paramsJSON)` synchronously; native and WASI builds serve JSON-RPC over stdio. See `docs/reference/wasm.md`.

- **Table views export as CSV and TSV.** `-render-form csv` or `tsv`, `%render <view> csv|tsv`, and
  `"form": "csv"` or `"tsv"` on `opensysml/render` write a table as a header record of its columns
  and one record per row, quoted as RFC 4180 quotes a field, so a spreadsheet or a CSV reader opens it
  directly; `-render-all` writes `.csv` or `.tsv` files and VS Code's *Export Diagram* saves them.

- **A tree rendering draws the relationships between the elements it shows.** `asTreeDiagram` views — the default rendering, and what every migrated SysML v1 block definition, class and package diagram becomes — now carry the edges a general view draws between their nodes: a specialization, subsetting or redefinition to each drawn general (hollow triangle at the general end), a composition (filled diamond at the owner) or reference (hollow diamond) from an element to the definition typing each part, item, port, attribute or `ref` usage it owns, labelled with the usage's name and multiplicity, and the typing of a usage whose owner is not drawn. An edge is drawn only between two drawn nodes, never into a nested one, and a usage's `Route` steers the composition standing for it; a specialization or typing has no member of its own and is routed by the client. The `text`, `mermaid`, `dot`, `plantuml` and `d2` forms, the `opensysml/render` response and the VS Code diagram panel, with new hollow-triangle and diamond markers, all show them, so a migrated BDD's generalization and association lines are drawn between the blocks it exposes.

- **Interconnection diagrams show ports on their nodes and connect edges to them.** Port sides are chosen automatically, and unconnected ports are spaced along the bottom of their node.

- **Imports that make a namespace's memberships indistinguishable are reported.** Two imports bringing members of one name or short name whose metaclasses conform — `import A::*; import B::*;` where each package owns a `part def Engine`, or one recursive `import V::**` reaching two nested `vb` — now warn once per name on the import bringing the later membership, naming every colliding member and the import it came through: `Duplicate of imported member name 'Engine': A::Engine (import A::*), B::Engine (import B::*)` (code `name-conflict`, beside the owned and inherited duplicate-name warnings). An imported member repeating one the type inherits warns the same way (`Base::x (inherited)`). Resolution is unchanged and still takes the first membership. One membership two imports both reach, an alias beside the element it names, an imported name an owned member hides, a pair whose metaclasses are unrelated and the standard library's own members are not reported.

- **SysML syntax highlighting in JupyterLab 4 and Notebook 7, shipped with the kernel's pip package.** `pip install jupyter-opensysml-kernel` now installs `jupyterlab-opensysml`, a prebuilt JupyterLab extension (`editors/jupyterlab`), as shared data under `share/jupyter/labextensions`, in every platform wheel and the sdist — no Node.js, no `jupyter labextension install`. It registers the `sysml` (`text/x-sysml`, `.sysml`) and `kerml` (`text/x-kerml`, `.kerml`) CodeMirror languages, so the cells of a `sysml` notebook, `.sysml`/`.kerml` files in the editor and Markdown fences tagged `sysml` are highlighted: keywords, contextual keywords, comments and `doc` bodies, strings, numbers, unrestricted and qualified names, operators, and a cell's leading `%command`. The keyword tables are generated from the lexer by the VS Code grammar generator (`make vscode-grammar`) and gated by a test, and the kernel now reports `language_info.codemirror_mode = "sysml"`.

- **`sysml model.sysml -list documents|views|diagrams|pseudo-views|all` names what a model declares by the names `-render-document` and `-render` read.** Each document or view is listed by its qualified name as the notation writes it — quoted segments, `::` inside a segment and a line break escaped as `\n` included — so it can be passed back unchanged. `views` marks a view whose kind is not produced as unsupported with the reason; `diagrams` keeps the graph-shaped views (`tree`, `interconnection`, `state`, `action`, `case`, `mixed`, `sequence`, `requirement`, `definition` and `package`); `-list-kind state,action` filters by kind; `-list-form text|tsv|json` writes aligned text, tab-separated values with a header, or a JSON array carrying each item's kind, support, reason, file and 1-based line. The REPL's `%documents` and `%views [diagrams] [<kind>...]` print the same text form, and the workspace's `AllViews` lists the views of all its own documents, each naming its declaring document.

- **`%load` reads Jupyter notebooks.** A path ending in `.ipynb` — named at the prompt, on the `sysml` command line, or found under a directory or pattern beside `.sysml` and `.kerml` files — loads the notebook's code cells in order, each a source of its own, so a diagnostic names `<notebook>.ipynb cell 3` and its line in the cell, and re-loading the notebook redeclares it. A cell is loaded for what it declares: its `%` command lines and its expressions are skipped and the report says how many, so a loaded notebook runs no one's `%sweep` or `%save`. `--cells 1,3-5` or `--cells tag:<tag>` picks cells; a cell tagged `skip-load` is never loaded; a notebook written for another kernel language, or in a format other than nbformat 4, is refused with the reason.

- **The REPL and the Jupyter kernel read and write a SysML v2 API repository with the OMG pilot kernel's four commands.** `%repo [<base path>]` shows or sets the server (seeded from `FLEXO_SYSMLV2_URL`, the token from `FLEXO_INTEROP_TOKEN`, never printed); `%projects` lists every project, paged to the end; `%load [--id=<project id>] [--name=<name>] [--branch=<branch name or id>] [<name>]` reads a project's model at a branch head into the session as notation and remembers where it came from; `%publish [-d] [--project=<project name>] [--branch=<branch name>] <name>` writes the elements rooted in a session element as a new project or as a commit of what changed on the branch, reporting the commit id and the counts created, updated and deleted; `-d` includes derived properties. Any server speaking the standard API serves — the pilot's `SysML-v2-API-Services` without a token, Flexo MMS with one — and the kernel answers argument problems as `UsageError`.
- **The standard SysML v2 API client is its own package.** `internal/translate/interop/sysmlapi` drives projects, branches, commits with `change` payloads, elements and roots with optional bearer authentication and follows a server's `Link: rel="next"` paging to the end; `internal/translate/interop/flexo` keeps Flexo's extras — Layer 1 Turtle, organizations, ETag pushes — over it, and `internal/translate/interop/modelsync` holds what `sysml -sync` and the REPL share.

- `%viz [--view=<VIEW>] [--style=<STYLE>...] [<form>] <name> [<name>...]` draws named elements on demand in the `sysml` REPL and the Jupyter kernel with the OMG pilot kernel's grammar: `VIEW` is `DEFAULT`, `TREE`, `INTERCONNECTION`, `STATE`, `ACTION`, `SEQUENCE`, `MIXED` or `CASE` in any letter case, `DEFAULT` choosing the rendering kind from what the names resolve to; several names draw in one diagram; `--style` maps the pilot's directions to the rendering direction and takes the `pilot`/`cameo` drawing styles, palettes and port displays, while a pilot style no form here draws (`ORTHOLINE`, `POLYLINE`, `COMPTREE`, …) is accepted and noted as not drawn. In the kernel a `%viz` cell with no form shows the diagram as Mermaid and, where Graphviz is installed, as SVG drawn from DOT.
- **Transitions out of a named entry action run effects and routes.** `entry action boot; transition boot do { … } then s;` runs its effect after the entry action and before `s` is entered, and may target a junction or choice of the same body, whose route is resolved after the entry action; availability checks only the incoming transition's own route, and a dead nested default-entry junction fails with a typed run error when its separate entry transition is taken. In a region of a parallel state the effect, like a compound transition's segment effect inside the region it enters, interleaves with the sibling regions' entries, and `-schedule explore` reaches every order. A trigger on such a transition, or an effect on the shorthand `entry; then s;`, is still rejected.
- **Entry routes may terminate the machine.** When a machine's entry route or a composite's default-entry route ends at a terminate action, the effects run and the machine terminates without entering or recording later states.

- **`-portable` makes a SysML v1 migration one self-contained file.** With `-migrate sysml -portable`, the OpenSysML library packages the output refers to — `DiagramLayout`, `MigrationMetadata`, `StateMachines`, `Stochastic`, `DocumentQueries`, and whatever those refer to in turn — are appended after the model, a KerML library in its SysML spelling, so the file loads in a tool that ships only the standard library without changing what the migration writes; the report names the packages inlined. `-strict` remains the way to an output that refers to no OpenSysML library at all.

- Repeated action steps (`a[n]`) now execute in `while`/`for`/`if` bodies, as `perform action run[n]` on parts (each performance its own occurrence), next to control nodes where the SysML-mandated succession ends settle the crossing (per performance into a join or the lone incoming succession of a merge, a written `[*]` barrier into a fork or decision, a written `[*]` fan-out out of a join or merge), and after a guarded succession with a written target end (`first p if g then [*] a;`). `sysml -check` encodes them too, except a step a token may reach again while its performances are live and a repeated step with features or flows of its own. They also support external reads of their features (a sequence over all performances, in repetition-index order), per-performance pin values, and enclosing `bind` assignments with a single-valued end. Flows at a repeated step's pins, multi-valued binding ends, guards out of a repeated step, and a false guard into one that performs more than once remain refused, since the specification leaves those open.

- **A constraint or requirement with `in` parameters is verified with arguments for them.** `VerifyConstraint` and `VerifyRequirement` take `arguments`, bound positionally in declaration order, and `named_arguments`, bound by name, as `RunAnalysis` binds a case's inputs; a name no parameter has, more positional values than parameters, a parameter left without a value, default or same-named value on the checked object, or a value not of the parameter's type is an undecided verdict naming it, and the symbolic `holds` and `satisfiable` questions refuse them. The service advertises the `verification_arguments` capability; every client binds them (`VerifyArguments`/`VerifyArgument` in Go, `arguments=`/`named_arguments=` in Python, Julia and MATLAB, `VerifyOptions` in Node, Rust and Java). `%constraint`, `%requirement`, `-constraint` and `-requirement` take them in parentheses after the name, with an object as the subject after them, as `%analysis` does: `%requirement Pkg::Under(limit = 5) Pkg::part`.

### Changed

- **A migrated operation call runs on the object its target pin holds, and send object actions are migrated.** A `CallOperationAction` whose target pin is fed by an object not read from `this` — an activity parameter, a fork, another action's output — was written as an action typed by the operation's action def running in the caller's context; it is now written with the target pin as a parameter typed by the class owning the operation, to which the operation's `context` is bound (`action n : Owner::Op { in ref :>> context = target; in target : Owner[1]; }`), and the flow into the pin is written, so the call runs on the object the pin holds. A call whose target is an object a `CreateObjectAction` creates acts on an empty pin, since `CreateObjectAction` is not migrated; the migration report says so. A `SendObjectAction` is now written `send request to target;` (or `via` its port), sending the object its request pin holds. A `StartObjectBehaviorAction` or `StartClassifierBehaviorAction` stays a placeholder keeping its pins, its note saying that a v2 object's behaviors start when the object does. The transformation census records the call operation and send object mappings as faithful and the two start mappings as a deliberate divergence, with candidate errata for the output pin's feature chain and for the send object mapping typing the sent item by its request pin.

- SysML v1 migration writes cross-body «Allocate» relations as allocation defs with redefined `source` and `target` ends, writes element imports and aliases, maps InformationItems to item defs and item flows to `flow of Item`, and preserves «DirectedFeature» operation direction on action usages.

- The SysML v1 migrator writes a plain UML class (one with no SysML stereotype) as an `occurrence def` rather than a `part def`, a property typed by one as an `occurrence` (`ref occurrence` unless composite), and a plain class realizing an interface as a specialization of the interface's port def. Link actions are written as actions with their pins, an association end's qualifiers as features of the end, and a link between instances as an individual connection def.

- **A migrated value specification action keeps its result pin's multiplicity, and a node no edge leads to counts as mapped.** The result pin is now written with the multiplicity and ordering v1 declares (`out result : Integer[0..1] = 2;`) instead of a fixed `[1]`; a pin that cannot hold the action's one value (lower bound above one, or upper bound zero) leaves the value unbound and reports it, since binding it would fail validation. The `first start then n;` the migrator writes for an action or fork that no edge leads to is v1's own rule for such a node, so the migration report now counts it as mapped rather than approximated; the notation written is unchanged. The transformation census records the three value specification action mappings as faithful, with a value that would not conform to the pin's type deliberately left unbound and a typed-in string written as the scalar the pin holds, recorded as a candidate erratum.

- **Migration writes UML parameter sets.** A behavior's or operation's `ownedParameterSet` is written `ref set { ref [m] = p; … }` inside its definition, one unnamed reference usage per bound parameter carrying the parameter's own multiplicity; a set's condition has no target and is reported unmapped, and a set binding no written parameter is unmapped.
- **Migrated owned rules are asserted.** A classifier, activity or operation rule is now written `assert constraint N { expr }` rather than `constraint N { expr }`, so the runtime requires it, as a UML invariant means.
- **Migration reads `uml:Expression` else guards.** A guard specification that is an `Expression` with symbol `else` and no operands is written as the else succession of a decision or the unguarded branch of a choice, like the opaque and literal spellings.
- **Expression trees read `xor` and `implies`.** A UML Expression tree symbol `xor` or `implies` over Boolean operands is written as the KerML connective of the same name; over other operands it is refused as a type error, where the operator used to be refused as unknown.
- **`ref` declarations may omit their name.** `ref [1] = x;`, `ref = x;`, `ref [1];` and `ref;` now parse as anonymous reference usages, matching the pilot grammar's optional identification.

- Migration writes a SysML v1 «Viewpoint» as OMG's `view def` with a nested `viewpoint`, its purpose as a `require constraint` and its methods as `rendering` actions; a conforming view is typed by that view def, and concern comments become named `concern` usages framed by reference.

- **Add task-oriented guides for each client and split the Python guide by task.** The shared client overview now compares all seven surfaces and gives each language the same working Demo model.

- **Rerouted wires no longer take bends shorter than one grid square** where a straighter route is free, in the VS Code diagram panel and on the landing page.

- The SysML v1 migrator writes a DocGen `TableStructure` with `loop = true` as one table over the elements its chain holds and ledgers it mapped: DocGen keeps a table's loop and runs none, so one table listing them together is what it prints, not an approximation of one table each.

- **The solver demos state one objective per analysis case.** `MassThenScience` (`examples/solver-demo.sysml`) and `FarthestThenTool` (`examples/disposal-robot-demo/robot.sysml`) each declared a second objective for a lexicographic `%optimize`, which SysML v2 forbids (`Only one objective is allowed.`); both cases and the walkthrough passages running them are dropped. The lexicographic optimization itself is unchanged. The pilot differential records the round: 352 of 384 files fully agree (was 351), only-pilot 93 → 91.

- **Rational values are exact.** A KerML `Rational` is a rational number (KerML 1.0 §9.3.2.2.8), so a decimal or exponent literal is the exact Rational it spells and Integer `/`, the `RationalFunctions` operators, `rat`, `numer`, `denom`, `floor`, `round`, `abs`, `max`, `min`, `gcd`, `sum`, `product`, `ToString` and `ToRational` compute without rounding: `0.1 + 0.2 == 0.3` is `true`, `1 / 3` is `1/3`, and `numer(rat(1, 3))` is `1` where it was `6004799503160661`. A whole result of rational arithmetic stays a Rational unless the declaration returns `Integer`. A terminating Rational prints as a decimal (`0.1`), any other as `numer/denom` (`1/3`). A Rational whose numerator and denominator outgrow the evaluation's size budget is the typed `ErrRationalSizeLimit`, never a rounded double. `Real` stays IEEE 754 binary64: a value declared `Real`, an operation with no rational result (`sqrt`, trigonometry, `exp`, `ln`, a non-Integer exponent) and mixed Rational/Real arithmetic answer a binary64 Real. `RealFunctions::sum` and `RealFunctions::product` over quantities with Integer magnitudes now give Real magnitudes, as they already did over plain Integers (`RealFunctions::sum((1 [m], 2 [m]))` is `3.0 [m]`). A Rational compared with a binary64 Real is rounded once first, as in mixed arithmetic, so `x : Real = 0.1; x == 0.1` and `rat(1, 3) == 1.0 / 3.0` are `true`; Rationals compare with each other, and Integers with anything, exactly.
- **Exact Rationals cross every boundary.** gRPC adds `Value.rational_value`, `Quantity.rational_magnitude` and `DocumentValue.rational_value` under the new `rational_values` capability, carrying a Rational no double holds in lowest terms (a service without the capability reports it unsupported and refuses one sent, as `big_int_values` does); the JSON engine adds `rationalValue`; the Go client decodes `opensysml.Rational`, the Python client `fractions.Fraction`, the Node client `{ kind: "rational", numerator, denominator }`, the Java client `Value.RationalValue` over `org.openmbee.opensysml.Rational`, the Rust client `Value::Rational` over `opensysml::Rational`, the Julia client `Rational{BigInt}` and the MATLAB client `struct('numerator', …, 'denominator', …)`. Each sends every exact Rational, one a double holds included, as `rational_value` to a service with `rational_values`, which reads it as that Rational while an inbound `real_value` stays a Real; to a service without the capability a client sends a Rational a double holds as that `real_value` and refuses any other. RDF export keeps an exponent literal no double holds as its exact `xsd:decimal`, unit-scale composition stays exact, the SMT witness replay is exact over Integers and Rationals (only queries a binary64 Real takes part in are still marked rounded), and `sysml -compile` refuses, by construct, exact Rational arithmetic binary64 cannot reproduce.

- **Exploration probabilities now describe model weights, not schedules.** Outcomes report an exact value or minimum-to-maximum range over schedulers only when weighted model choices occur; unweighted outcomes have no probability, and the wire API reports probability ranges and failed linearization counts.

- **The homepage diagram spans the hero.** The stack diagram under the hero copy is drawn
  across the width of the blue hero instead of a 46rem column; the caption, the run
  controls and the debugger and editor panes below it keep a readable width.

- **The homepage hero draws the stack diagram again.** The interactive diagram of the
  OpenSysML REDK, the OMG pilot implementation and Flexo MMS — drawn in the browser from
  `docs/assets/opensysml-stack.sysml`, with the run, debugger and editor panes — is back
  under the hero copy, now without sysml-toolkit, which is no longer part of the model or
  its `ModelJourney`.

- **The landing-page diagram's boxes can be dragged anywhere in the hero.** They were confined
  to the figure's own box; they now move over the whole blue band and stay inside it, with any
  open detail card, when the band changes size. They also hold still instead of drifting.

- **The landing-page hero is drawn on graph paper.** A faint grid sits behind the hero's text
  and diagram.

- **Redesigned the documentation site's landing page and information architecture.** The homepage now leads with a project overview and an interactive stack diagram that is a real SysML model: the in-browser engine parses `docs/assets/opensysml-stack.sysml` for the boxes, wires and right-click attributes, and runs its state machine to animate a model change travelling across the stack; new top-level pages cover the `sysml` command line (with an in-browser WebAssembly REPL), documentation, client libraries, and community links; a sticky tab bar provides the main navigation, the left sidebar always shows the full tree, and the internals/project records are published but out of the navigation.
- **The documentation site now publishes from `develop`.** The Pages workflow builds and deploys on every push to `develop` instead of `main`, so documentation changes go live when they merge rather than at release.

- **Render the landing-page diagram from the engine-backed model.** The site build bundles the shared diagram code and its routing assets, with wires rerouting as boxes move and dropped boxes kept clear of each other. A dropped box whose port lands just out of line with a wired port lines up with it, so the wire runs straight. Wires keep a right-angled route while a box is dragged too close for libavoid to route around.

- **The landing page's stack diagram draws each project's subsystems inside its box, and titles the Go project "OpenSysML Runtime Environment and Development Kit".** `docs/assets/opensysml-stack.sysml` models the subsystems of all four projects as nested parts, each with a label and a role shown on its card: OpenSysML's parser and formatter, validation, standard library, language server, editor integrations, REPL, Jupyter kernel, execution runtime, constraint solver, analysis engines, gRPC service, clients and bindings, document generation, interchange, SysML v1 migration and OSLC query; sysml-toolkit's, the Pilot's and Flexo MMS's likewise. Right-clicking a nested part shows its card, and dragging a project box carries the parts drawn inside it.
- **Diagrams pack a container's unconnected children into rows.** The automatic layout stacked every nested part no edge reaches in one column, however many there were; a container of such parts now lays them out side by side, sized toward a wide aspect ratio, while connected nodes keep their layers.
- **Diagrams keep siblings in a layer in the order the model declares them.** Crossing minimization used to decide which of several boxes in one layer sat on top; the layout now seeds the layered pass with the declaration order and minimizes crossings interactively from it, so a diagram reads as its model is written. The landing stack declares OpenSysML, then the Pilot, then sysml-toolkit, and is drawn in that order.

- **The MATLAB client's `opensysml.symbol(model, id)` is now `opensysml.getSymbol(model, id)`.** Its file `symbol.m` differed only in case from `Symbol.m`, the class file, so a checkout on a case-insensitive file system (the macOS and Windows defaults) kept one of the two, broke `Model`, and left `git status` permanently dirty. `model.symbol(id)` is unchanged. A hygiene test now fails when any two tracked paths differ only in case.

- **Mermaid diagrams now preserve more rendering detail.** Flowcharts draw definitions and usages, control nodes as symbols, thick connectors, markdown labels, notes, ports, pictures and keyword-family palettes; state diagrams draw fork, join and choice pseudostates, final transitions and notes; sequence diagrams draw notes. The Pilot and Cameo styles set Mermaid's theme and font, document backends inline local images, and anything Mermaid cannot draw keeps a `not represented` notice.

- The SysML v1 migrator writes a `LoopNode` that names a decider as a v2 `loop`, declaring its loop variables — each initialised from its loop-variable input — and the flag ending the loop as private features, writing its setup, test and body parts as action usages that perform in the loop's order, carrying the flows between parts as pin defaults — a loop-level fork may pass a loop variable's value on, while routing through any other control node keeps the body-once form — and reading the loop's results from its variables at the end. A loop that names no decider has no test result to write and keeps the body-once form, reported approximated with why.

- **The SysML v1 migrator writes a ReadSelfAction's result as an `out` feature bound to `this`.** The action was written with no body and its readers named `this` themselves; its result pin is now the `out` feature the mapping writes, bound to `this` (or to the context parameter in a definition's body).
- **A structural feature read or write inside a structured node acts on the object it is given.** `this` inside a structured node is the behavior's context rather than the node, and an object a flow brings into the node from outside is read through the object pin instead of being taken for `this`.
- **A ReadSelfAction's result flows to the pins it feeds.** An object flow from the result into an action input or call argument was dropped, leaving the input without a value; it is now written as a flow from the result. A guarded flow stays unwritten, since a flow from the result would deliver the value whatever the guard says. A node named `this` is renamed, since every action inherits `this`.

- **The SysML v1 migrator writes object-feature actions as assignments and library calls.** Add, remove, optional clear and destroy actions preserve result values, collection operations respect ordering and uniqueness, and required-feature or UML-only differences are reported instead of emitted as invalid v2 notation.

- **The SysML v1 migrator now writes variables, variable actions, buffer nodes, ReduceAction and RaiseExceptionAction.** An activity's variables are declared as private features of its body (`private attribute` for data types, `private ref item` for other classifiers, `private ref` untyped), and the variable actions become assignments to them — a read binds its result pin, a clear assigns `null`, adds and removes use `SequenceFunctions::including`/`includingAt`/`excluding`/`excludingAt`. A CentralBufferNode leading to one target is mapped by the flows through it; a DataStoreNode stays approximated. A ReduceAction folds its collection by the reducer's v2 function (`collection->ControlFunctions::reduce {in x; in y; F(x, y)}`), and a RaiseExceptionAction is followed by a terminate action, since v2 has no exceptions.

- **`-convert` refuses a SysML v1 model; it is migrated, not converted.** `sysml Model.mdzip -convert sysml`, whether `-from xmi|uml|mdzip` or the `.xmi`, `.uml` or `.mdzip` extension names the input, now exits non-zero with ``sysml: Model.mdzip is a SysML v1 model, which is migrated, not converted: every element is mapped, approximated or left unmapped and reported element by element; write `sysml Model.mdzip -migrate sysml -o Model.sysml -migration-report Model.report.txt` ``, since readers took `convert` to mean lossless and the migration accounts for every element instead. The service's `Convert` RPC refuses the same input with the same message (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`), and so do the Go, Python and Java clients' `convert` methods, each naming their `migrate`. **Breaking** for a script that ran `-convert` on v1 input: replace `-convert` with `-migrate` on that command line — the output is unchanged — or `convert(...)` with `migrate(...)` in a client. There is no alias and no deprecation period.

- **The sponsor links open OpenMBEE's NumFOCUS collective on Open Collective.** The landing page's
  Sponsor button and link, the Community page's Sponsor card and the repository's Sponsor button
  now open https://opencollective.com/openmbee/contribute instead of the NumFOCUS donation page.

- **The Cameo plugin is now OpenSysML MDK.** It is positioned as the successor to OpenMBEE's Model Development Kit for SysML v2 work: the source moved from `editors/cameo/` to `editors/mdk/`, the plugin id is `org.openmbee.mdk.magic`, the Java packages are `org.openmbee.mdk.magic.*`, and the Resource Manager archive is `Magic_MDK_<version>.zip`. The context-menu operations, results window and annotations are unchanged. The capability-by-capability comparison with MDK, and the order the gaps are being closed in, is `docs/project/mdk-parity.md`.

- **The documentation site moved to https://runtime.opensysml.org/.** The opensysml.org
  apex domain now serves the OpenSysML REDK (Runtime Environment and Development Kit) landing page published from
  Open-MBEE/opensysml.github.io, which forwards the old documentation URLs and the
  `install.sh` / `install.ps1` one-liners here; `site_url`, the Pages CNAME and every link to
  a published page live at the new subdomain. The homepage leads with OpenSysML itself again —
  what running a model finds, the measurements against the OMG pilot implementation, what it
  does and how to install it — above the documentation map; the former `/ecosystem/` page
  redirects to the landing page at opensysml.org.

- **The pilot differential hands the reference the OpenSysML libraries.** Both reference bridges take `--extension-library DIR`, loading a directory the way the standard library is loaded — resolved against, never validated — and `pilot-diff` passes `internal/workspace/libs/stdlib/OpenSysML Libraries` through it on every batch — the `OpenSysML Libraries` directory of the standard-library root OpenSysML itself loads, so an `OPENSYSML_LIBRARY_PATH` override moves both sides of the comparison together, and a `-repo` naming a checkout whose libraries differ from the ones the build embeds is refused; the baseline's provenance records the one used. A demo that imports `DocumentQueries`, `OOSEM`, `MOSA`, `AnalysisRecords`, `StateMachines` or `OpenSysMLMathFunctions` is now compared on its own diagnostics rather than on the reference's unresolved-reference cascade: only-pilot rows fall 1634 → 93, 351 of 384 files fully agree, and what remains on the demos is enumerated row by row in `docs/project/pilot-differential.md`. Nothing we report moved.

- **The `opensysml` PyPI page reads as a short introduction.** The Python package's README, which PyPI renders as the project description, is the install command, a quickstart, a short account of how the service is found, and links to the guides; the walls of implementation detail that 0.9.1 published move to the [service guide](https://opensysml.org/clients/python/service/) and the API reference.

- **The race-suite shards allow 55 minutes per package instead of 45.** The runtime package takes 31–47 minutes under `-race` on the CI runners, so the old limit killed roughly every other run at `panic: test timed out after 45m0s` with no test having failed. The shards' job keeps its 60-minute ceiling, so a genuine hang still ends the job.

- **The Go project is named the OpenSysML Runtime Environment and Development Kit (OpenSysML REDK).**
  "OpenSysML" alone names OpenMBEE's open source SysML v2 suite and its community; the README,
  the documentation site's title and landing page, the community page and CONTRIBUTING now call
  this repository the REDK, so the runtime and the suite it belongs to no longer share one name.
  Package, module, binary and environment-variable names are unchanged.

- **The PSSM referee writes a deferred signal in the standard buffer / accept-loop / exit-flush encoding, through the lowering it now shares with the SysML v1 migrator (`internal/translate/deferred`).** Nine *Deferred* tests moved with the extension's removal: *Deferred 003*, *004 A* and *004 B* to `differs-by-design` (a transition elsewhere in the configuration fires where PSSM lets the deferring state hold the occurrence), *Deferred 001* and *005* to `differs-by-design` (a replay re-enters the pool behind what is already there), *Deferred 007* to `not-expressible` (a deferred operation call) and *Deferred 006 A/B/C* to `not-expressible` (a deferral in a state an unguarded completion transition leaves); `pass` 60 → 52, `not-expressible` 30 → 34, `differs-by-design` 1 → 5, `fail` unchanged. The rejection oracle's `defer` member case is now rejected in the default mode as well.

- **The custom header menu was removed in favour of the navbar.** The Legend of the Red Dragon link is now a footer icon.

- **The REPL core no longer links the translators, protobuf or the network stack.** `%print`, `%save`, `%query`, `%features … json` and `%render-document`'s Graphviz drawing register themselves from `internal/frontend/repl/replext/*`, which `sysml` links through `replext/all`, so the CLI behaves as before while a program that only creates a REPL session drops from 393 to 190 packages (12.6 MB to 8.6 MB gzipped as `GOOS=js` WebAssembly). `simresults` moves from `internal/translate` to `internal/exec`, and `repl.CompileCalc` takes the compiler as an argument.

- **A signal nothing of its object takes is discarded, not held for a later state.** A message a model sent to an object whose state machines were settled in a configuration with no transition enabled for it stayed on the bus until a state entered later accepted it, so a stale `Abort` sent during one alignment aborted the next one as soon as it began. Once the object's machines are settled and none of its do behaviors or performed actions is parked at an accept for it, the first of its machines still running takes the message off the bus and drops it, reported as a dropped dispatch, as UML discards an occurrence that triggers no transition and is not deferred. A message routed to a port, or to a named receiver other than the object, stays in flight as before.
- **A deferring state's own accept of the deferred signal takes it before the keeping loop does.** The standard deferred-signal encoding kept every occurrence of the deferred signal while the state was active, even one the state's own do behavior was parked at an accept for, so the behavior never went on and the occurrence was replayed on exit. The keeping accept now yields to a branch of the state's do behavior parked for the same occurrence and able to take it, which consumes it; it does not yield to an accept held for an arrival it still awaits, which could not; an occurrence nothing of the state consumes is kept, as before.

- **Refresh the documentation downloads and package links.** Link release assets directly and identify the clients currently published on PyPI, npm and crates.io.
- **Remove the standalone Ecosystem page.** Its stack and ecosystem information is now on the landing page, and the former page URL redirects there.

- **The code quality findings SonarCloud reported against the tree are resolved.** Long Go functions in the Mermaid and state-chart renderers, the command-line front end, the parser and the v1 migrator are split into named helpers over shared constants; the Node client's conformance runner dispatches RPCs through a table, a symbol's children and a migration's images are fetched and written concurrently, and the generator's entry point awaits at top level; the Java client reads with streams where it looped; the Rust client imports each capability by name; and the Python tests assert one fact per line. No behaviour changes.

- `-engine check` on a `-state` without `-advance` now observes the machine after its initial transition only, the point the default run and `-schedule explore` observe, instead of running its do behaviors and advancing its clock to every wait; `-advance <time>` advances all three to the same horizon. When the machine leaves a do behavior, an event, a timer or a change condition pending there, the standing line of `explore` and `check` says the result is `after the initial transition only` and that `-advance <time>` runs them (`scope` in `-json`).

- **Two positional allowances are parse errors rather than `nonstandard-notation` warnings.** An `assume`/`require` member outside a requirement or case body, and a one-ended `first <node>;` outside an action body, now produce a syntax diagnostic with an `ErrorNode` in the tree, matching how the other removed extension spellings report.
- **The `nonstandard-notation` warning for the state-machine keywords names the standard replacement.** `choice pick;`, `junction j;` and `history`/`shallow history`/`deep history h;` each report the `StateMachines` metadata spelling to write instead — `#choice state pick;`, `#junction state j;`, `#shallowHistory state h;`, `#deepHistory state h;` — and carry a quick-fix that rewrites the member and adds `private import StateMachines::*;` where it is missing.
- **The SysML v1 migrator writes the `StateMachines` pseudostate spellings.** A migration emits `#StateMachines::choice state`, `#StateMachines::junction state`, `#StateMachines::shallowHistory state`, `#StateMachines::deepHistory state` — qualified so a member named like the metadata cannot shadow it — and adds `private import StateMachines::*;` to each package holding one, under `-strict` the same as by default.

- SysON plugin: builds against SysON and Sirius Web 2026.9.2.

- **Integers are unbounded, as KerML's `ScalarValues::Integer` is.** An Integer result that leaves `int64` is promoted to an arbitrary-precision integer and demoted again when it fits, so `9223372036854775807 + 1` is `9223372036854775808`, `2**70` is `1180591620717411303424` and `(2**70) / (2**69) == 2` instead of an `arithmetic overflow`; values within `int64` keep the existing fast path. Equality, hashing, ordering, printing, quantities, ranges, collections, library functions, Monte Carlo, sweeps, the SMT/BMC engines and their replay, JSON, RDF, documents, serialized notation and the `sysml-engine` WebAssembly engine all carry the exact value. A result larger than the new Integer size budget (`OPENSYSML_MAX_INTEGER_BITS`, default 1048576 bits, at least 64 so every `int64` fits) is refused with `integer size limit exceeded` rather than hanging, and an Integer beyond `int64` used as an index, multiplicity bound, dimension or size is a typed error, since it addresses nothing. The wire adds `Value.big_int_value`, `Quantity.big_int_magnitude` and `DocumentValue.big_int_value` (decimal strings) for values beyond `int64` only, so existing clients see no change; they are negotiated by the new `big_int_values` capability, so a service without it reports a wide Integer, or a vector holding one, as an unsupported null, refuses a document query bound to or answering one, and every bundled client refuses to send one, document-query bindings included, to such a service rather than have it read as null; the Go, Python, TypeScript, Rust, Java, Julia and MATLAB clients read and write them. `sysml -compile -target go` computes Integers exactly with `math/big`; `-target c` refuses a calc whose Integer arithmetic is not provably within `int64`, naming the construct.

- **Subactions no succession orders are performed instead of refused.** An action's composite
  action usages that no succession reaches — action nodes and `send`, `accept`, `assign`, `if`,
  `while` and `for` written among its members — start with the action's performance, unordered
  against each other and beside any `first`-rooted flow, and the action ends only once all have
  (`Actions::subactions :> subperformances`, `Performances::enclosedPerformances`). An action with
  two such nodes, or a bare `send`, no longer fails with "no initial node found" or "has no position
  in the token flow"; `-schedule explore` enumerates their interleavings. `ref` and abstract usages
  are not performed, and the bounded model checker refuses such a flow as not encoded.

- **`%view` checks every concern a viewpoint frames against what the view exposes, without the view
  restating the framing.** A view no longer has to write `frame concern …;` for each concern of the
  viewpoints it `satisfy`s — SysML v2 admits `frame` in a requirement, concern or viewpoint body, never
  in a view body — so the `violated (framed by the viewpoint but not by the view)` verdict is gone and
  each framed concern is evaluated, in the viewpoint's order, against the exposed elements its subject
  admits; the other verdicts (`conforms`, `violated` naming the element and condition, `unevaluable`
  for no condition or an unresolved reference, `(from <view>)` on an inherited `satisfy`) are as before.
  A `frame` written in a view definition or usage body is still parsed but draws the
  `nonstandard-notation` warning, which `-strict` escalates to an error as the pinned OMG pilot
  rejects it. The views, disposal-robot and self-model demos and the REPL handbook transcript drop
  their view-body framings; their `%view` output is unchanged.
