- **Route a moved node's edges at right angles around the other boxes in the diagram panel.** An edge at a node the model places was drawn as a straight line; the panel now routes it orthogonally around the other boxes — out of every container except the ones its ends lie inside — with libavoid whenever it lays the canvas out, separating edges that share a box and keeping it straight only until the router has loaded, during a drag, or when no route fits. The route is not written to the model unless one of its waypoints is edited.
- **Bundle libavoid in the diagram panel.** The panel ships libavoid 0.4.5 (LGPL-2.1-or-later) as WebAssembly and loads it at startup; until it answers, edges it would route stay straight.

- **The WebAssembly gate checks `sysml-engine`'s dependencies directly.** Both the `js` and `wasip1` builds fail on protobuf, Connect, the gRPC and stdio transports, the analysis framework and its engines, the document backends, the workspace pipeline and the converters. The gzipped size budget rises to 7,700,000 bytes and now bounds growth only, so view-rendering growth no longer reads as a forbidden dependency.

- **The WebAssembly gzip size budgets rise by 50 KB.** The gzipped js build of `sysml-wasm` is now bounded at 8,350,000 bytes and that of `sysml-engine` at 7,700,000 bytes. Exact Rational number support grew both binaries past the previous bounds of 8,300,000 and 7,650,000 bytes (the combined build measured 8,309,493 bytes gzipped, the engine 7,647,486), so `TestWasmRuns` failed on the integration branch itself; the budgets bound growth only, and `TestEngineDependencies` still catches a forbidden dependency.
- **The WebAssembly gzip size budgets now bound gross growth only.** The gzipped js build of `sysml-wasm` is bounded at 10,000,000 bytes and that of `sysml-engine` at 9,000,000 bytes. The size as it stands is accepted: the grid view and relationship matrix grew the combined build to 8,355,865 bytes gzipped and the engine to 7,686,608 on the integration pipeline, over the 8,350,000-byte budget set a step earlier (a GitHub runner builds the same tree about 11 KB smaller, 8,344,612 and 7,686,149, so the pull-request gate passed while every integration pipeline failed `TestWasmRuns`). The budgets no longer move in 50 KB steps with each feature; `TestEngineDependencies` is the gate that keeps a forbidden dependency out of the engine.

- **The workspace model no longer links the execution runtime or the document evaluator.** Building a runtime over a workspace's documents moved from `internal/workspace/model` to the new `internal/workspace/modelrt`, and rendering a workspace's document definitions (`RenderDocumentMarkdown`, `DocumentFiles`) to the new `internal/workspace/modeldoc`; the workspace model keeps the runtime-free half as `Workspace.Detach`. A product that only parses, resolves and validates no longer carries `internal/exec/runtime`, `internal/doc/*` or `internal/translate/filename`. No user-visible behaviour changes.

- **The findings SonarCloud reported against the tree since 0.9.2 are resolved.** The documentation REPL's file listings and the editor landing page's attribute rows sort with an explicit comparator, the diagram layout's nearest-side search starts from a known side, the landing page's last debugger step is an acknowledged promise, the install test's conditionals are bash's, and the nightly workflow pins the Python build tooling and npm it installs. No behaviour changes.
- **The npm publish job installs Z3 before it runs the Node client's suite.** `publish-npm` runs the client's full test suite against the release binary before packing it, and three of those tests ask the service solver-backed verification questions; with no solver on PATH they were undecided and the job failed, as the release rehearsal showed. The job now installs Z3 ahead of the tests, as `node-test` does.

- **The race suite's runtime shard is two shards.** `internal/exec/runtime` under `-race` took 31–57 minutes on the CI runners, straddling its 55-minute limit, so `make test-shard` now runs it as `runtime-corpus` (the suites that run every model of the conformance corpus: exploration, check/explore agreement, witness replay, execution conformance and its policies) and `runtime` (everything else), each about half the time. The merged coverage profile collapses the blocks the two share.
- **The README names the REDK as MDK2.** Its title reads "OpenSysML REDK — Runtime Environment and Development Kit (MDK2)", and the introduction says the REDK is the second generation of OpenMBEE's Model Development Kit, expanded and updated for SysML v2, with the Cameo plugin carrying the OpenSysML MDK name.

- **A `-strict` SysML v1 migration refers to no OpenSysML library.** The output loads in a tool that ships only the standard library — the pilot implementation, or a modeling tool built on it — where it used to fail on `MigrationMetadata`, `StateMachines`, `Stochastic`, `DocumentQueries` and `DiagramLayout` references. Made-up name markers become comments, a choice or junction a plain state whose else branch is guarded by the negation of the other guards, a branch probability a comment, an interval wait its midpoint, a view carries no layout, a Monte Carlo analysis loses its `Simulation::MonteCarlo` generalization, and a history pseudostate, a document (with every view and paragraph in it) or a table is refused with a report line; a layout export is still joined to the views, the report counting the geometry it omits; the deferral encoding keeps its `MigrationMetadata::Deferred*` annotations. The default migration is unchanged.

- The SysML v1 migration writes actors and use cases as the usages a v2 use case model is made of — a UML Actor is a `part` usage, a UseCase a `use case` usage (its subject, includes and behavior inside it) — so each is one element a tool draws once, and writes the lines a use case diagram draws as connections between them: each actor association, «Include», Extend and «Refine» becomes a `connection` named by its kind (`'Operator to Control Instrument'`, `'Control Instrument includes Manipulate Stage'`, `'A extends B'`, `'A refines B'`), hosted by the nearest package holding both ends, with no trailing comment a tool would show as a note; a relationship its author named keeps that name and carries its kind as the connection's `doc` instead, as an extend's extension points and condition are. A refined requirement or block gets one usage in its package only when a connection joins it; a named association's one usage is typed by its `connection def`. Use case diagrams are rendered `asInterconnectionDiagram` over those usages and connections instead of `asTextualNotation`, with their Cameo geometry applied; the interconnection renderer draws use case and requirement usages as nodes and an `include` as the connection's line rather than a nested node. A «Requirement» is a `requirement` usage too, as a tool's users draw one, rather than a `requirement def` with a usage written beside it when a line needs one: a nested requirement is its subrequirement, a «Satisfy» or «Verify» subsets it (`satisfy requirement 'satisfy R' :> R;`), a «DeriveReqt»'s `connection def` ends and a property it types subset it, a «Refine» connection joins it directly, and a requirement table, matrix or «Requirement»-stereotype filter lists the usages by name; an instance specification of a requirement is reported rather than written as an individual. A document query reads a derivation `connection def` whose ends subset requirement usages as relating those usages, and a named satisfy or verify that subsets a requirement usage as satisfying or verifying it too. An «Allocate» whose end is a requirement, actor or use case stays a dependency, as no allocation end can be typed by a usage.

- The non-normative `OpenSysML Libraries/` extension libraries are now maintained upstream at [Open-MBEE/OpenSysML-Extensions-Library](https://github.com/Open-MBEE/OpenSysML-Extensions-Library); the bundled copy is pinned by `scripts/extension-libraries-pin.sh`, refreshed by `scripts/sync-extension-libraries.sh` (`make extension-libraries-sync`), checked for drift on every pull request (`make extension-libraries-check`), and carries `engine-contract.json` recording the qualified names the engine binds.

- **Declare the bundled OpenSysML extension libraries as non-standard.** `DiagramLayout`, `IdentityMetadata`, `MigrationMetadata` and `SysMLValidation` were written `standard library package`, which both asserted membership of the normative Kernel/SysML libraries and drew a pilot warning (`User library packages should not be marked as standard`); they are project extensions and now read `library package`. RDF export no longer marks them `sysml:isStandard true`.
- **`tests/migrate` is a race shard of its own.** The SysML v1 migrator's suite ran as part of the `rest` shard, where under `-race` it grew past 24 minutes on the pull-request runners and past the 30-minute package timeout on CircleCI's `develop` builds, failing every merge. `scripts/race-shard.sh migrate` names it, `make test-shard SHARD=migrate` and `make coverage-shard SHARD=migrate` run it, and both CI configurations run six race shards and five coverage shards.

### Deprecated

- **The `choice`, `junction`, `history`, `shallow history`, `deep history` and `defer` state-machine keywords are deprecated.** They still parse and lower as before — nothing is removed — but each is a `nonstandard-notation` warning (an error under `-strict`) naming the `StateMachines` metadata spelling that replaces it, with a quick-fix that performs the rewrite and adds the import.

### Removed

- **The `defer <event> [, <event>]*;` state member, an OpenSysML extension SysML v2 has no counterpart for, is removed from the language, the runtime and every surface.** `defer` is an ordinary name (`state defer;`, `attribute defer : Real;`, `defer.x`), and a legacy `defer` member in a state body is one `defer-notation-removed` parse error in either conformance mode, pointing at the standard encoding of a deferred signal: an ordered buffer (`item deferred : Sig[*] ordered;`), a do action whose accept loop keeps each occurrence while the state is active, and an exit action that sends each kept occurrence to `self` — what the SysML v1 migrator writes (`docs/reference/sysml-v1-migration.md`, *Deferred signals*), which executes unchanged. Gone with the notation: the runtime's deferral queue and its priority rule (a deferring substate no longer outranks a transition of an enclosing state or a sibling region — a transition that is enabled fires), the `Sig / defer` view labels, the `sysx:DeferMember`/`sysx:deferredEvent` RDF terms (a Turtle file carrying them is refused with a diagnostic rather than read with the member dropped), the REPL's "deferred by the active state" report, and the `defer` grammar and completion entries. Three things the extension expressed have no standard spelling and are documented as losses in `docs/project/spec-compliance.md`: the deferring state's priority, one pooled replay order across different signals, and a deferred operation call.

### Fixed

- **A changelog fragment that outlived its release is not folded a second time.** `scripts/changelog.py` now skips a fragment whose entry an earlier version's section already carries, under the same heading, instead of repeating it in the version being prepared; it still deletes the fragment.

- **An accept node is written as an `AcceptActionUsage`.** `action a accept p : T;` and `accept T;` in an action body were written as an `ActionUsage` owning an `isAccept` reference; they are now the `AcceptActionUsage` the grammar builds (SysML.xtext `AcceptNode`), owning the payload as its `payloadParameter` through a `ParameterMembership`, as a transition's trigger already was. Graphs written with an `ActionUsage` still import.

- **An accept node's `via` is its receiver parameter.** `accept s : Signal via p;` in an action body stated the port as `sysml:via`, a property the metamodel does not have; the `AcceptActionUsage` now owns a second parameter whose value is the reference to `p`, named as its `receiverArgument`, as a transition's trigger already did. Graphs stating `sysml:via` still import.

- **Let an accept suspend the whole call chain and work in loop and branch bodies.** An action performed synchronously that reaches an `accept` now parks its caller and every performance above it until a matching message arrives from a sibling branch, another object, `%send` or the clock, where it used to report `ErrAcceptDeadlock` as soon as the call began; an `accept` in a loop body, an `if`/`else` branch or a nested action declared there is now executed in declaration order instead of being refused (an `accept` written directly among a leaf action's members stays one of its unordered subactions, ordered only by `then`). `ErrAcceptDeadlock` is reported only when nothing in the run can still post the awaited message, and names the innermost accept and the chain holding it.

- **A keyword-less succession in an action body takes end multiplicities.** `first [0..1] a1 then [0..1] a2;` parses as the `SuccessionAsUsage` that `succession first [0..1] a1 then [0..1] a2;` spells with its keyword (SysML v2 §7.13.5: the keyword may be omitted when the declaration part is empty, and a succession's ends take constraining multiplicities), with either end's multiplicity or both. It was read as an initial node, which takes a plain name, and reported as an error.

- **Actions can execute inherited function result parameters as outputs.** Their bodies can write the result and performers can read it; a `return` declared in a non-function owner remains refused.

- **Honor exact multiplicities on supported action-step usages.** Declared finite counts produce the corresponding performances in supported action flows, including unordered subactions that start concurrently; validation reports unsupported or ambiguous execution, including bounds beyond the 64-bit step-count range (for example, 1180591620717411303424) and non-unit counts in loop and conditional block flows, and the SMT engine returns a typed refusal when it cannot encode a non-singleton count.

- **Read a toolkit succession whose source is the nameless `entry;` action.** Reading sysml-toolkit JSON of a state with `entry; then idle;` failed with `cannot convert the reference to …::@0::@0`, since the resolved source end named an anonymous element no `first` clause could spell; a succession from a nameless member written before it now comes back as `then idle;` by position, as the notation declares it.

- **Give each document of a model its own root Namespace in the API element form.** A model of several documents converted to `api-json` wrapped every document's top-level elements in one unnamed Namespace, named after the first document's root, so a package declared in `app.sysml` was owned by the namespace of `lib.sysml`. Each document is a `RootNamespace` of its own in the textual grammar, and the pilot serializes one per document; the element form now writes one per document (`sysx:sourceDocument`), and reading it back drops each.

- **The documentation no longer implies the standard library ships `Matrix`, `Tensor` or `*Array` specializations of `Collections::Array`.** The OMG library declares only `Array`; the handbook now shows how a model declares its own `Matrix :> Array` and shapes a usage of it with `:>> dimensions` and `:>> elements`.

- **Exploration and the model checker reach every order of a calc or constraint body's unordered statements.** A calc body whose `assign y := y * 10;` and `assign y := y + 2;` no `then` relates now explores and checks to both `12` and `30` instead of claiming `12` over every schedule, and a constraint whose verdict depends on the order of its body's statements is both satisfied and violated, and violated under `-engine check`. The body is still performed whole inside the step that evaluates it, so its statements are reordered among themselves and never interleaved with another performance; its result expression or condition is evaluated after them. Each invocation whose result depends on the order is one `statement order` choice point between the distinct results, so a recursive calc adds one choice rather than one per level, and a body whose statements commute adds none. A guard whose verdict depends on the order is a `statement order` choice point between the distinct results the orders produce, an evaluation error among them; inside a preview it is reported as not covered, as is a guard whose constraint body can write outside the constraint. `then` between a constraint body's own statements now orders them rather than being refused. Calcs compiled at run time fall back to the interpreter when their statements can reorder under these schedules. `declared` and the default schedule keep declaration order, so default results do not move. A stated succession a calc body cannot perform is refused naming the construct (`` `first` statement``) rather than an internal type.

- **Report the build's version, commit and time even when the linker stamped none.** `sysml -version`, `sysml-lsp -version`, `sysml-grpc -version` and the other commands used to print `dev` and `unknown` unless built with the Makefile's `-X` flags, so a `go install github.com/Open-MBEE/OpenSysML/cmd/sysml@latest` binary could not say which release it was. Every command now resolves its build through one shared resolver: the linker's stamps when set, otherwise the module version and VCS metadata the Go toolchain embeds — the release tag for a `go install` of a tagged version, the commit and its time (`-dirty` for a modified tree) for a build from a checkout — and `dev`/`unknown` only when neither is recorded. Stamped builds report exactly what they did before.
- **`sysml-lsp` names its real version in the `initialize` response.** `serverInfo.version` was a fixed `0.1.0`; it is now the version the binary prints for `-version`.

- **Keep case-specific rendering behavior isolated.** Case and mixed views use the `OpenSysMLRenderings` library package, read objective documentation without mutating shared semantic models, and limit their specialized shapes and labels to the new kinds.

- The steps of an analysis or verification case that states no succession are now unordered, as an
  action definition's subactions are: `explore`, `-engine check`, replay and seeded runs reach every
  interleaving of them instead of only declaration order, so a case such as
  `action s1 { assign x := x * 10; } action s2 { assign x := x + 2; }` reports both `12` and `30`.
  `declared` and the default `reverse` schedule still perform the steps in declaration order, so
  default results do not change. A case inherited as an action step keeps its case-body lowering and
  declaring scope.

- **A migrated call binds its callee's `context` to the caller's part as a valid feature reference.** When a block's activity calls a behavior that acts on one of the block's parts, the migrated call now writes `action n : B { in ref :>> context = Owner::part; }` instead of the chain `this.part`, which failed validation; the call validates and runs on that part. The transformation census now records call behavior actions as faithful, citing the typed, library, owner-usage and context-bound forms, and the eight common return-parameter mappings as a deliberate divergence: a return parameter stays an `out` parameter because KerML admits a return parameter only in a function or expression, recorded as a candidate erratum.

- **A migrated SysML v1 send signal action addresses the object its target pin holds.** A send whose target pin a read-self action feeds is now written `send new S() to target;` with the pin bound to `this`, and one whose required target pin nothing fills names the pin (`to target`) instead of being written with no receiver; an optional target pin nothing fills keeps the receiver-less send, which reaches the sender. The transformation census records SendSignalAction and its sub-mappings, ReplyAction, UnmarshallAction and BroadcastSignalAction as faithful and AcceptCallAction as deliberate, with candidate errata for the payload's InvocationExpression, the dropped onPort and AcceptCallAction's target.

- **Two CircleCI steps that failed only on `develop` pass again.** The `Go gates and binaries` job's solver-absent gate gets a 30-minute no-output limit: `go test` prints a package's `-v` output only once that package finishes, and `internal/frontend/repl` alone takes eight to ten minutes there, so CircleCI's default ten-minute no-output limit killed the step after every test had passed. `Go static checks` now persists the landing-page bundle it builds (`docs/assets/landing`) to the workspace, so the link check `make scripts-coverage` runs in `Python client tests` finds the libavoid-js licence text that `docs/project/third-party-notices.md` links instead of reporting it missing on an executor without Node.

- **The CircleCI `build-test` workflow on `develop` passes again.** `Go coverage profile` now joins four parallel `Go coverage shards`, one per race-test package shard, because the instrumented suite run serially overran the 60-minute job limit; the solver-absent gate gives `internal/frontend/repl` a 30-minute test timeout rather than go test's default ten minutes; `TestAllConcurrentDeadlineKeepsWhatFinishedAndMarksTheRest` meets its deadline once the finished engine's step is kept, rather than on a 20 ms clock an instrumented run on a loaded node could overrun before that engine answered; and `Python client tests` installs the pinned documentation toolchain that `make scripts-coverage` needs to test the MkDocs hooks. `make coverage-shard SHARD=…` and `make coverage-merge` reproduce the sharded profile locally; `make coverage` is unchanged.

- **Report an expression written as a connector end once.** `connect [1] s.y#(1) to [1] k.u;` used to produce four errors for the one mistake — `expected 'to' between connector ends`, `expected '{' or ';' after declaration`, `expected a metadata feature name after '#'` and `expected a body member`. A connector end is a feature chain (SysML BNF 8.2.2.13.1) and `#(…)` is an expression operator, so the model is still rejected, now with the one diagnostic `a connector end names a feature, not an expression; name the feature itself and put any multiplicity before it` at the end, and the parse resumes at the next end or the end of the declaration. Element selection `#(…)`, indexing `[…]`, `->` invocations, `.?`, arithmetic and calls are all recognised, in `connect`, `connection`, `interface`, `allocate`, `succession`, `bind` and `flow` ends alike.

- Connector-end accessibility (`Must be an accessible feature (use dot notation for nesting)`) now rejects an end spelled as a definition's owned feature by qualified name (`allocate ToastBread::applyHeat to Toaster::heating` at package level, and the `connect`, `bind`, `flow`, `interface` and KerML `connector`/`binding`/`flow` forms of it), as the pilot does. A connector with no featuring type reaches only features that have none, so a feature owned by a definition was wrongly treated as package-level and exempted. Dot-notation chains, ends of the owner's own or inherited features, `interface def`/`connection def` end parameters, package-level connectors between package-level usages, variants and enumeration literals stay accepted.

- **Reflect connector ends and feature-chain targets.** A connection usage's `connect` clause now owns the ends it writes, named or not, so `ownedMember`, `ownedElement`, `ownedFeature`, `ownedEndFeature` and `endFeature` of its metaobject hold the two end `ReferenceUsage`s, and the API JSON export lists them under `ownedMember` as it did under `connectorEnd`. Each end owns the `ReferenceSubsetting` its attachment states, so `ownedReferenceSubsetting` and `ownedSubsetting` of an end derive; they used to fail with `reflective feature is not derived`. The target of an owned specialization, typing, subsetting, redefinition, reference subsetting or cross subsetting written as a feature chain (`connect s.y to k.u`, `attribute :>> sub.w`) is now reflected as the feature the chain denotes, owned by the relationship as `ownedRelatedElement` and answering its `chainingFeature` in order, for a loaded model and a library record alike; its `target`, `subsettedFeature`, `redefinedFeature` and `referencedFeature` used to be underived. A usage of an interface whose definition pairs its ends by a `flow` no longer warns that the ends are not conjugate, and a flow is read by the end its chain starts at, so two ends typed by the same port are paired only by flows between them, not by a flow within one end.

- **Apply the default `[1..1]` multiplicity by usage metaclass, and withhold it on reference subsetting.** Enumeration, view, rendering, actor and stakeholder usages without a declared multiplicity now hold exactly one value, including at runtime; `::>` and `references` to a type-owned feature withhold the default.

- **An accept of a deferred signal written beside the keeping loop takes the occurrence first.** The runtime identified the keeping accept of a deferring state's do action by its position and signal, so an ordinary accept of the same signal declared at the do action's own level counted as a second keeper, and an occurrence arriving while both were parked could be kept instead of taken, depending on the schedule. The keeping accept is now the one the `MigrationMetadata::DeferredKeeper` annotation marks, lowered by resolved type into the action graph; every other accept is an ordinary consumer the keeper yields to whenever it can take the occurrence that step.

- **Three demos are now valid SysML v2 by the reference implementation as well as ours.** `disposal-team-demo/team.sysml` and `runtime-showcase/mass-rollup.sysml` sum quantities with `QuantityCalculations::sum` instead of `RealFunctions::sum`, so the two `Bound features should have conforming types` warnings each drew are gone and their walkthroughs no longer present them as expected output; the team demo's `Unit` counts commands in `commandsAccepted`, a name the `accepted : Transfer` every state inherits from `StatePerformance` no longer shadows in the reference; and `expressions-demo.sysml` passes `calc` usages (`square`, `halve`) where it passed `calc def`s as function values, the form the reference rejects with `Must be a valid feature`. The handbook's calculations-as-values transcript follows the same form. Every answer the walkthroughs record is unchanged.

- **Wires laid out automatically meet their port squares.** They stopped half a port short of the square in the VS Code diagram panel and on the landing page.
- **Landing diagram boxes leave room for every shared wire lane, and rerouted wires stay inside the hero.**

- **Treat same-named members of non-conforming metaclasses as distinguishable.** KerML `Membership::isDistinguishableFrom` also distinguishes two memberships whose member elements' metaclasses conform in neither direction, so `part def A; attribute def A;` or `class A; datatype A;` in one namespace no longer warns `Duplicate of other owned member name`, and an inherited `self` reached from both `Action` (an `ActionUsage`) and `Part` (a `ReferenceUsage`) no longer warns `Duplicate of inherited member name`. Same or specializing metaclasses (`part def A; item def A;`, two `part p;`) still warn, as does any pair whose metaclass is unknown or whose alias target does not resolve; which member a reference resolves to is unchanged.

- **A loop or `if` node of a state's do body stating a flow yields as a statement does.** Adding `then` to a do body made it a stated flow whose `for`, `while` and `if` nodes ran whole in one do round, so two regions' bodies no longer interleaved inside them. Such a node now gives the machine up after each iteration and each branch statement, as the statement-list body does, under every schedule; explore, the model checker, replay and seeds reach those interleavings.

- The SysML v1 migrator writes the `doc` comments a connector owns inside its `connection`, so a document column reading a connection's documentation finds it.

- The downloads page now links the prebuilt `opensysml-sysml.vsix` from the nightly release.
- The install guide now points at the downloads page for the full list of artifacts and package-manager routes.

- **Exploration and the model checker reach interleavings inside concurrent action bodies.** A leaf action body's initial values are read when its performance starts and each `assign` writes when it ends, so another concurrent performance may run between them: two performances of `action a[2] { attribute t : Integer := c; assign c := t + 1; }` now explore to `c = 1` and `c = 2` instead of claiming `c = 2` over every schedule, and two fork branches with that body do the same. Such a body yields after its start and after each statement under `explore`, `-engine check`, replay and seeded schedules wherever another performance's moves can change its result; `reverse` and `declared` keep their results, and the SMT engine reports such a flow as not covered (`body interleaving`) rather than encoding one order. The same holds when each branch performs that body as an action of its own (`action a : Inc;` or `perform action pa : Inc;`), and between an `if`'s guard and its branch.

- **Executors due at one instant interleave move by move under `explore`, `check`, `replay` and seeded schedules.** An executor drawn from the due order used to run all its work at the instant before another could move, so two actions reading a shared feature and then writing it never reached the lost update. The executor drawn now makes one move and keeps its turn only while its next move is independent of everything each other due executor may still do at the instant; otherwise the due order is drawn again. Explore runs and check state counts grow on models whose messages cannot be resolved statically. `declared` and `reverse` keep their order.

- **Exploration distinguishes setup failures from runtime outcomes.** Root executor creation failures and pure structural start-check failures before model behavior runs fail the exploration without an outcome; failures during initial behaviors and later remain visible as error outcomes and make the result fail rather than prove.

- **Exploration and the model checker reach every order of a body's unordered statements.** The direct statements of one action, state behavior or calculation body that no succession orders are subactions the library leaves unordered, so `{ assign x := 1; assign y := x; }` now explores and checks to `y = 0` and `y = 1` instead of claiming `y = 1` over every schedule. `then` between statements, an `if`'s guard before its branch and a loop's iterations stay ordered; two statements that touch nothing the other does are explored once. `explore`, `-engine check`, replay and seeded schedules choose the order, recorded as a `statement order` choice point; `declared` and `reverse` run a nested body's statements first to last as before, and an action definition's own statements in the token order each policy already gave them (`reverse` last to first), so default results do not move. An `explore` run whose body has more orders than its run budget reports the search incomplete, and the SMT engine reports such a body as not covered (`statement order`) rather than encoding one order.

- **`all T` now answers three namespace-level shapes it used to get wrong.** Binding connectors owned by a package (`bind a = b`, `bind c = d.w2`) join their ends into one equivalence class denoting the class's one object — several bindings on one usage and bindings written in another package included — instead of each end naming its own; the objects an object's action, state, connection, interface, allocation and flow usages hold — perform and exhibit included — are reached by an extent whose target they may hold; and a package-level collection usage denotes the objects the usages subsetting it denote first, anonymous members only to make up its lower bound, an under-count or over-count a typed `ErrMultiplicityViolation` naming it. A bound class's shared lower bound and a namespace collection's own fill past its subsetters' values are held lazily past 1000 members, as a collection's own lower bound is, so `bind a = b` over `Car[1000000000]` and a subsetter of one count and index without allocating it.

- **Mark writes through object pins to hidden features as unmapped.** References cannot reach features written as private or protected in v2.
- A write through an object pin no longer makes its activity declare a context parameter.

- **A document regathered for the identity audit names the ids it first did.** An annotation body's owner, which the qualified names of the members it redefines run through, is the annotated element until the document is resolved and the metadata definition after. The identity union gathered a document before it was resolved and again, unchanged, after an edit elsewhere dropped what its analysis read, so `@Tag { :>> label = "…"; }` was filed under `…::part::label` and then under `…::Tag::label`. The id groups looked moved, every document filed in them was judged again, and an incremental analysis could report a diagnostic (`Must redefine an owning-type feature`) a fresh one did not. A gather now links the document's annotation bodies first, as resolving it does.

- **An end feature that may vary in time is now read-only, as the specification's implicit constancy requires (SysML v2 §8.4.2.2, KerML 1.0 §8.3.3.3.4).** Reflection already answered `isConstant` true for such an end, but `FeatureReadOnly` did not, so assignments, flows, accepted payloads and `SetFeatureValue` wrote them; they are now refused with `… is constant as an end feature: its value does not change over the lifetime of its featuring occurrence`, statically and at run time. Interface records persist the derivation as a new `ModMayTimeVary` modifier, so recorded symbols answer the same flags as parsed ones.

- **`include <ref>;` is written with the `ReferenceSubsetting` the metamodel gives it.** The `IncludeUseCaseUsage` of `include pay;` now owns a `ReferenceSubsetting` to `pay` in Turtle and `api-json`, as `IncludeUseCaseUsage` and its derived `useCaseIncluded` require, instead of a `sysml:includes` property the metamodel does not have; `include use case u : T`, which includes itself, no longer states one either. Graphs written with `sysml:includes` still import.

- The interconnection rendering now draws the ports a part has from its definition — `part heating : HeatingSystem` shows the `durationIn : ~DurationPort` its definition declares — on the part's border, named and typed as declared with `~` for a conjugated one, and ends a connector, interface, flow or binding at the port it names rather than at the part: DOT as record cells or pin nodes (`"n2":"n2.0" -> "n1":"n1.0"`), PlantUML as `port` elements of the part's rectangle, text as `port` lines under the part and `part.port` edge ends, Mermaid as a `subgraph` per ported part holding a node per pin (`n1.0["durationIn"]`) with the connector drawn pin to pin. By default a part draws the ports a connector of the view ends at and no other — each a small square on the part's border with its name beside it, so a crowded diagram shows what it connects and nothing more, a part none of whose ports is connected drawing as before — and `-render-ports full` (`%render … full`, `"ports": "full"` on `opensysml/render`, `Diagram::ports` in a document) draws every port a part has, labelled `name : Type`; the new `openSysmlRenderPorts` capability lists the two displays. Previously only a part def's own nested ports were drawn, so `-render '#interconnection:Toaster'` and a declared interconnection view named no port at all. A part whose definition declares no port draws as before. `-render -help` now states the `#<kind>` and `#<kind>:<element>` pseudo-view forms. A Mermaid flowchart's multi-line subgraph titles are centred line under line by a `themeCSS` rule in the frontmatter that reserves their height, instead of being set flush left inside the centred block. Every graphical form now heads a node with its kind, `«part def»`, above the name and centred, as the graphical notation and Cameo stack a header; the kind stood under the name before.

- **A chain call values its feature by the calc's result, and a calc without a result parameter gives none.** `attribute v = holder.make();` now resolves `v.wheels` through the result of `Holder::make`, where the call's chained callee was not followed, and `attribute v = noResult(1);` for `calc def noResult { in a : Integer; }` no longer reads `v.a` from the calc's own parameters: a function's result is its result parameter, or Anything when it declares none, as the pilot has it.

- **An untyped feature valued by an invocation or a constructor is typed by the value's result for member lookup.** `action run = Base(x = 3, z = 7);` now resolves `run.r` to `Base::r`, so the model runs and reads `7`; `attribute c = mk();` reads the members of the calc's result and `part c = new Car();` those of `Car`. The rule is KerML `checkFeatureValuationSpecialization`: a feature with a value, no declared specialization and no direction specializes the value's result, so a `default` value, a directed feature or a `:>` feature is typed by its declaration alone, as the pilot has it.

- **The Java client reads a few answers as the Python client does.** A protobuf `null` carrying an unsupported-value reason stays a `Value.NullValue` whose `unsupported()` names it instead of reading as the model's `null`; a JSON body's `-0` keeps its sign; near-name suggestions rank as `difflib` does; a `Convert`/`ApplyEdits` refusal for a model the service no longer caches is a `ModelNotFoundException`; and a failed conversion is a `ConversionException`, still a `ModelException`.

- Check a join's way out against prior arrivals and the segments chosen in the current signal/change dispatch. Timer expiries are separate occurrences even when due together, so a dead completing segment leaves its source's other timer-group alternatives available.

- The language server's JSON-RPC framing now quotes a malformed header line in its error (`malformed MIME header line: "Content\x01Length: 3"`), as `net/textproto` does from Go 1.25.11, so control bytes from a client never reach the log raw. The module now names `toolchain go1.25.11`.

- **Closed Julia connections now reject requests.** Calls made after closing an external or private connection report a transport error rather than reaching the service.
- **Action and state execution return typed results instead of raw dictionaries.** Action outputs expose decoded values and performer attributes; state runs include visited states, final context, and final time with defaults.
- **Julia transport failures no longer include request bodies or stack traces, and read/connect timeouts are reported as service timeouts.**
- **Strict parse failures include full diagnostic locations and messages.**
- **Connect JSON preserves protobuf non-finite floats and negative zero.** NaN, infinities, and the sign of zero survive value encoding and decoding.

- **Junctions without an enabled route no longer consume events or completion triggers, and join segments fire on their own occurrences while waiting for the remaining segments.** This preserves unmatched and deferred events, lets other enabled transitions proceed, and keeps a join from completing after its owner has been left.

- **The release build cross-compiles `sysml-jupyter-kernel` for Windows again.** `make build-jupyter-kernel` with `GOOS=windows` stamps the kernel's `VERSIONINFO` the way the other three binaries are stamped, from a `packaging/windows/sysml-jupyter-kernel.winres.json` that was missing, so `scripts/build-release-artifacts.sh` — and with it the nightly snapshot — no longer stops at the Windows kernel with `open packaging/windows/sysml-jupyter-kernel.winres.json: no such file or directory`.

- **A `jupyter-opensysml-kernel` platform wheel bundles only its own kernel.** The release built the five wheels from one source tree, and setuptools kept the kernel staged for the previous platform in `build/lib`, so the `win_amd64` wheel carried the macOS arm64 `sysml-jupyter-kernel` beside its `sysml-jupyter-kernel.exe` (the other four wheels were unaffected, each overwriting the same file name). `setup.py` now drops a stale kernel from the build tree before every wheel, and `scripts/build-jupyter-kernel-dist.sh` refuses a wheel whose `jupyter_opensysml_kernel/bin/` holds anything but the one kernel for its platform.

- **`first a if g then b;` in an action body is a `TransitionUsage`.** The keyword-less guarded succession was read as a `SuccessionAsUsage` carrying its guard as text, while `succession first a if g then b;`, which the grammar makes the same `GuardedSuccession`, was a `TransitionUsage`; both are now the transition, with its guard owned through a `TransitionFeatureMembership` of kind `guard`. A transition in an action body converts back in the keyword-less spelling unless it was written with `succession` or declares a name, never as `transition`, which an action body does not admit.

- The landing page's Run button follows the debugger's seed, and picks and shows a random seed when none is set, instead of always taking the same path through the model's free choices.

- **Hold a large or expression-valued lower bound without allocating it.** A feature whose lower bound is past 1 000 (`part p : C[5000]`), or given by a model-level evaluable expression (`C[k * 1000]`, `C[n..*]`), now holds exactly that many values instead of reporting a multiplicity violation: the identities are reserved up front and each object is made when it is first read, indexed, iterated, written or bound, so `size(p)`, `p#(i)`, `includes`, iteration, `all T`, snapshots, held images, the explorer's state key and the RDF/JSON/gRPC object listings all see the same values under stable identities. Work that must reach every value is charged to the element budget (`OPENSYSML_MAX_ELEMENTS`) and refused with `ErrElementLimitExceeded`; a lower bound of `*` (`[*..*]`) remains `ErrInfiniteLowerBound`, a multiplicity violation naming KerML 1.0 §7.4.12.

- **`sysml-lsp` answers its first request promptly on a multi-file workspace.** The folder scan indexed the workspace's files one at a time, re-deriving every package's wildcard imports per file, and opening a file the scan had already indexed reparsed it, invalidated the workspace and re-analyzed every open document. The scan is now one batch, and a buffer holding the text already indexed is only marked open. The first hover on the 28-file Apollo 11 model after opening every file went from 13–17 s to under 1 s; later hovers, diagnostics and inlay hints are as they were. A document's interface record now also holds beside a byte-identical version of a library file: the version answers as the library document it stands in for, which the record's key already names, rather than as a workspace document the record never saw.

- **Align MATLAB client decoding and transport behavior with the service and Python client.** JSON map keys, validation results, integer bounds, symbol lookup and HTTP failures now preserve their intended values and errors.

- **Mermaid container titles no longer overlap what they contain.** A flowchart subgraph or a composite state now writes its keyword and name on one line (`«part def» Toolchain`), the one line of title height Mermaid reserves, so the outer title of a nested interconnection is no longer hidden behind its first child cluster and a composite state's name no longer sits under its start dot; leaf node labels keep their lines. The `subGraphTitleMargin` frontmatter is written only for a container title that still spans several lines, since Mermaid grows every cluster by it after layout and pushed stacked sibling clusters into one another.
- **The REPL's `%help` and reference now show that `mermaid` takes a drawing style.** `%render-document` is listed as `<name> [mermaid|dot|plantuml|d2 [pilot|cameo]]` as its usage line says, and the `%render` and `%render-document` rows of the REPL reference name the palette and `pilot`/`cameo` style the Mermaid form accepts.

- **Evaluate excluding type-filter sources once.** Migrated row queries bind the source expression as a defaulted parameter so repeated type checks do not traverse it again.

- **A migrated comment's cross-references are resolved, not printed as markup.** Cameo stores documentation as HTML, and the SysML v1 migration reduced it to text, so a View Editor cross-reference (`<mms-cf mms-cf-type="name" mms-element-id="…">[cf:APS Location.name]</mms-cf>`) printed its `[cf:…]` fallback and an element hyperlink (`<a href="mdel://…">`) kept only its text between doubled spaces. Both are now resolved through the export by id: in a document paragraph each becomes a `Ref` run to the section, figure or table the target became in the same document — rendered `Section N.N - Title`, `Figure N`, `Table N` and linked, as a native reference is — or to the element by name, between `Span` runs of the prose; elsewhere (`doc` comments, requirement text, stereotype tags) the reference reads as the target's current name. A `val` reference reads the slot's value and a `com` one the target's documentation, each noted as approximated. A reference to an element the export does not contain prints nothing (an `<mms-cf>`) or keeps its text (a hyperlink), and the comment's report row says which id is missing; a hyperlink whose text is stale is written with the current name and noted. The spaces dropped tags left are collapsed. A `Ref` may now also target a model element outside every document — a usage by name, any element as `E.metadata` — and renders as its name.

- **A migrated parametric diagram of MagicDraw's Monte Carlo analysis pattern draws the analysis again.** The view `sysml -migrate` writes for the diagram exposes the generated `analysis def '<Block> Monte Carlo'` in place of the pattern's `Mean` symbol and the def's `return`/`out` statistics in place of the other statistic symbols, positions the def where the symbol was, and routes the `Mean` binding along the def's `observed`, whose value binds it to the analysed value; the report counts that binding as routed, and the shown statistics as exposed, where it had counted them as not written. An interconnection rendering now draws an exposed analysis def as a node with its subject, parameters and attributes as pins on its border, and a drawn feature whose value names another drawn feature as a binding edge, so the migrated view shows the analysis box and its line as the v1 diagram did.

- **A JavaScript body's assignment to a name nothing declares creates it.** The v1 migrator refused the whole body when a script assigned a name no pin, parameter, property or local bore (`tdiffOld = tdiff;`), so an effect accumulating a duration ran nothing and the duration stayed 0. A JavaScript body now declares such a name a local `attribute` of the action, typed by the assigned expression, as the tool's script engine creates the variable, and reports the declaration as an approximation. A name some scope declares but the body may not read, a name read before it is assigned, and every such name in a Java body stay refused.
- **A v1 `ReadStructuralFeatureAction`'s result keeps its pin's multiplicity.** The migrator wrote every read as `out result[1]`, so reading a feature declared optional or plural was a multiplicity violation at run time whenever it held none or several; the result is now `out result[0..*]`, `[0..1]`, … as the pin declares.

- **The migrator's ledger counts a send action as a sender only once it is written, and a button of the tool's UI prototype as posting the signals it specializes.** An accept of a signal is noted as waiting for a message only the tool's UI posts when no written send sends it or a signal specializing it — a send left as a placeholder, which performs nothing, no longer hides the note — and a button posting a specialization of the accepted signal now raises it. A send whose port is not the sender's keeps the note on its arguments beside the one on its port, and an entry, exit or transition effect is noted as waiting for the clock only for a wait the writer writes, not for a time event or duration constraint it leaves as a placeholder, nor for one in a behavior named by a call written as a placeholder or never firing, which invokes nothing.

- **A multiplicity bound that names a package-level feature with no value is rejected.** `feature k : Natural; feature d [k];` in a package now reports `Must have a Natural value`, as the reference implementation does: KerML requires a model-level evaluable bound to evaluate to a non-negative value, and a valueless `k` evaluates to itself. The same bound inside a type is still judged by its type.

- **A multiplicity bound is a literal or a feature name, as the grammar has it.** A bound written as an arithmetic or other operator expression (`[n+1]`, `[1..2*3]`), a cast (`[0..(2 as Integer)]`), a parenthesised expression (`[(n)]`), a feature chain (`[a.b]`), an invocation (`[f()]`) or `null` is now a syntax error, `a multiplicity bound must be a literal or a feature name (KerML.xtext MultiplicityExpressionMember)`, as it is for the pinned pilot; a computed bound is written as a feature value and named (`feature n : Natural = 2 + 1; feature d [n];`). The `validateMultiplicityRangeResultTypes` documentation now cites KerML 1.1 8.3.4.11.2 `MultiplicityRange`.

- **Accept feature references to named multiplicities.** KerML defines a multiplicity as a feature, so it can be used where a feature reference is required.

- **A named send or assignment node is one element.** `action s send x to r;` was written as an `ActionUsage` named `s` owning an unnamed `SendActionUsage`, so a succession or reference to `s` reached an element that was not the send; it is now one `SendActionUsage` named `s` (SysML.xtext `SendNode`), as the unnamed `send x to r;` already was. `action a assign x := y;` is likewise one `AssignmentActionUsage` named `a`. Both convert back to the notation they were written in.

- **The nightly snapshot's kernel-wheel check compares like with like.** `jupyter_opensysml_kernel.binary.built_against_releases()` returns a list, but the `nightly.yml` step that verifies the built `jupyter-opensysml-kernel` wheel compared it to a tuple, so every snapshot failed with `the installed wheel is built against ['nightly-…'], not ('nightly-…',)` after building correct artifacts. The step now expects `[tag]`.

- **The Node client answers the degraded handshake on `serverInfo()` too.** A service without `GetServerInfo` — a release older than the handshake — now answers `{ version: "", capabilities: [] }` instead of throwing `UnsupportedOperationError`, as the Python client does.
- **A dead service is a `ServiceUnavailableError`, not a bare `ServiceError`.** A refused connection or a service that died before answering now raises the dedicated subclass, keeping the reported status code; a browser fetch that never answered — a dead address, a CORS refusal, a service gone mid-call — maps the same way through the `TypeError` cause the transport wraps as `UNKNOWN`.
- **`strictConformance` is the documented parse option; `strict` is its deprecated alias.** Passing both with different values is refused on `load`/`loads`/`parseSources`; to raise on parse errors call `model.raiseForErrors()`.
- **`load`/`loads` refuse an unknown `language` before the call**, with the same `RangeError` `SourceDocument` raises.
- **`model.symbol("")` and `find("")` answer the model's root**, matching Python's `model.get("")`.
- **A `query()` that asks for nothing answers every element**, as Python's does; an OSLC-only request still sends just `oslcQuery`.
- **Non-string arguments are refused before any call.** `load`, `loads`, `parseSources` documents, `eval`, `symbol`, `symbolById`, `find` and `instantiate` raise a `TypeError` naming the parameter, and source text holding a lone UTF-16 surrogate raises a `RangeError`, instead of silently coercing or leaking an internal error.
- **Value inputs accept what Python's client accepts and refuse what it refuses.** JavaScript arrays encode as sequences and `Set`s as sets, collection elements accept every input form, a bigint outside int64 is a `RangeError` at any depth, `unset` and foreign objects are refused, and a unit named without its reduction to base units is an `UnsupportedValueError` instead of a crash.
- **A missed qualified lookup still names its near misses.** `symbol()` attaches `suggestions` — scored against short and qualified names alike — so a missed `Demo::Vehicel` reports "did you mean" like a missed short name; `symbolById()` stays the single call, reporting no suggestions and walking nothing.
- **A symbol's `multiplicity` answers `isOptional` and `isCollection`**, computed as Python's `Multiplicity` does.
- **A verdict's `standing` answers `reported` and `reached`**, and `CalcResult`, `AnalysisResult`, `Validation` and `SweepTable` gain `strength` and `bounds` getters beside `engine`.

- **Make case and mixed PlantUML links clickable in SVG.** Mixed control circles and each non-empty objective-note line retain source hyperlinks.

- **A standalone `@M about x;` annotates `x`, not the namespace it is written in.** The `@` spelling of a metadata usage with an `about` clause is now indexed exactly like `metadata M about x;`, so `x.metadata`, `annotatedElements`, and the `[@M]` import and expose filters see it, and the owning namespace no longer does. A bare `@M;` still annotates its owner.

- **Derive reflective feature flags for KerML declarations and wrapper elements.** Classify KerML usages by their metaclass and apply ownership-derived reflective semantics without changing execution compositeness.
- **Derive the type of prefix metadata reflectively.** Preserve its resolved metadata definition in interface records so `MetadataFeature` validation can evaluate the declared type.
- Derive message connector endpoints reflectively and match the pilot's abstractness for message flows with fewer than two related features.
- Place each subject parameter first in reflective input lists while preserving the owned-before-inherited order of other parameters.
- Classify objectives as requirement usages rather than part usages, so `PartUsage` type filters and queries no longer return them.

- **A behavior no longer writes a `constant` or `derived` feature.** An assignment (direct, qualified or through a feature chain), a flow or succession flow into a feature, an accepted payload bound into a feature the behavior declares, an invoked action's output returned into a same-named enclosing feature, an entry/do/exit or transition effect, a performer's write and a session's `SetFeatureValue` now refuse such a target — and one redefining or subsetting a constant feature, or redefining a derived one, implicitly as a parameter or across an imported interface record included — with `runtime.ErrReadOnlyFeature`, leaving the value as it was and naming the feature and whether it is constant or derived. A constant still takes its initial value or default, and a derived feature is still bound and re-evaluated. The constraint tier reports the same assignments as `assignment-referent-read-only` wherever the target, or a chain's final feature, resolves statically.

- **Mint each document's root Namespace apart from every other document's in the API element form.** A model of several documents converted to `api-json` minted each document's root `Namespace` and `OwningMembership` ids knowing only the graph's own subjects, so two documents whose roots were spelled `P` and `P_ns` (or `Q` and `Q_om`) both took the id `P_ns_ns` (or `Q_om_om`) and were written as one namespace. One minter now serves the whole export, so a suffix repeats until the id is free in the graph and among the ids already minted, and reading the form back still drops every wrapper.

- **The Rust client reads what the service could not send, and recovers from a dead private service.** A value the service sends as a null naming a reason is now `Error::UnsupportedValue` rather than `Value::Null`, and an instance feature holding one reports the reason through `FeatureValue::error` while the rest of the instance stays readable. `Connection::private` replaces a private service that has exited instead of handing out its dead address. Inline SysML content parses on a service without `inline_language`; `instantiate` refuses with `MissingCapability` on a service without `feature_values` before sending, as the instances it would return need; an empty vector is sent rather than refused; and `Symbol::children` leaves out a child the service cannot resolve instead of failing.

- **The Rust client replaces a private sysml-grpc child that dies as it is joined.** A killed child closes its sockets a moment before its exit can be observed, so `Connection::private()` could join the dying child and fail its handshake with a connection reset or refusal. A joined child whose handshake fails is now given a moment to report its exit and, once it has, is replaced; one that stays alive still surfaces the handshake error.

- **Decide a satisfaction through the requirements it requires by reference.** `satisfy wagonSpec by wagon1` where `wagonSpec` states `require ladenLimit { in w = unit; }` used to fail with `no value for feature actual` when `ladenLimit` redefines an attribute from its subject (`attribute :>> actual = w.tare + w.cargo;`); the reference's body now binds the referenced requirement's parameters, read in the requiring requirement's environment, so the redefined attribute is computed from the wagon the `satisfy` names and each referenced requirement is decided — a wagon over one cap violates the satisfaction, one within both holds it. `require r` keeps r's assumptions assumptions rather than requiring them, and `assume r` assumes every condition of r.

- **`===` over sequences is element-wise identity.** `(1, 2.5) === (1.0, 2.5)` is now `false`, as `SequenceFunctions::same((1, 2.5), (1.0, 2.5))` already was: each element must be `===` its counterpart, so an Integer element is never the same as an equal Real one, at any position or depth. `(1, 2.5) === (1, 2.5)` stays `true` and `==` still compares elements by equality. A program compiled with `sysml -compile`, C or Go, gives the same answers.

- The npm and Maven release jobs now stamp the release's five `sysml-grpc` digests into the
  `release-digests.json` the `@openmbee/opensysml` tarball and the `org.openmbee:opensysml` jar
  ship, as the Python wheel and Rust crate already did, and fail unless the packed artifact pins
  the tag; a published Node or Java client therefore downloads its own release on a pin without
  the optional sigstore dependencies. The committed shared table is brought up to date with every
  signed release through `v0.9.2`, from the cosign-verified `SHA256SUMS.txt` of each. The tag's pipeline now
  ends by opening a `chore/pin-vX.Y.Z` pull request against `develop` that pins the release
  in the committed table and every client copy, and a hygiene test fails a release branch
  whose predecessor the committed table does not pin.

- **A step budget of the largest 64-bit integer still binds.** With `OPENSYSML_MAX_STEPS=9223372036854775807`, the step counter could overflow once the budget was spent, in the interpreter and in a program built by `sysml -compile`; in C that overflow is undefined behaviour. Each charge is now checked against the steps left before it is spent, so the run fails with `evaluation step limit exceeded` and the counter saturates instead of wrapping.

- **A printed String reads back as the same String.** The interpreter and compiled C and Go programs printed a String with an unprintable character as a Go escape such as `"\u200b"`, which neither a String literal nor a compiled program's argument admits. A String now prints in KerML String notation: only the quote, the backslash and `\b \t \n \f \r` are escaped, and every other character is written as it is, so a result can be given back as input.

- **A "did you mean" suggestion follows the names the model spells, and only them.** The suggestion table was read as "every name" by the analysis that happened to build it, so any edit anywhere re-analyzed that document and everything depending on it, while a document analyzed afterwards recorded no dependency on the table at all and kept a stale suggestion when a name it could mean was added (an incremental parse then disagreed with a fresh one). Every suggestion now records that it read the set of names spelled, which an edit changes only when a name comes, goes or is spelled otherwise.

- SysON plugin: the real-artifact build (`-Psyson-artifacts`) now compiles its tests and frontend typecheck against the published 2026.9.0 SysON and Sirius packages.

- **A `terminate` inside a loop body names its own loop performance's node only.** `terminate slow;` in a loop body reached by several tokens ended the paused `slow` performances of the other loop performances too, and ran on as if it had ended its own. Each step of a statement node now owns what its body performs, so the terminate ends that body's `slow`; one that has already ended is reported as `performance already ended`, since `TerminateAction` ends an occurrence during its own performance.

- **A terminate action usage's body may state a flow.** `action stop terminate { assign code := 42; then terminate; then assign other := 7; }` now runs its `then` chain, where it was refused as stating no flow of its own. The terminate the usage stands for is unordered against its body's statements: `explore` and `-engine check` reach it falling before, between or after them, an output pin left unwritten binding no value, while `reverse` and `declared` still perform it after the body. A `terminate` naming the usage inside its own body ends the usage's performance rather than the body's. A terminate usage inherited into a specialized action retains its stated body flow.

- **Traces and witnesses name loop, `if` and other statement nodes by what they do.** A token resting at an unnamed statement node is now spelled `2@loop until`, `3@for i`, `1@if`, `4@assign x` or `5@terminate slow` rather than by a Go type name such as `2@*ast.WhileLoopActionNode`, in `explore` witnesses, `-trace` output and choice points alike.
- **A `for` loop variable declared with only a short name (`for <s> in steps`) is now declared, bound and traced under that name.** A body reference to it no longer fails name resolution.

- **A transition's guard is owned through a `TransitionFeatureMembership` of kind `guard`.** The `api-json` and Turtle forms wrote it under a plain `OwningMembership`, so a reader of the standard structure found no guard; it now follows SysML-textual-bnf `GuardExpressionMember` (`'if' { kind = 'guard' }`), and the transition states it as `guardExpression`, as its trigger and effect already were.

- **The WebAssembly gate no longer hangs at random in Node's exit under `-race` on CI, and a hang it does hit is explained in the log.** The `js` target runs under `node --single-threaded`: Node exits by joining V8's worker threads, and a worker still compiling (Sparkplug earlier, Maglev now) can wait for a garbage collection the exiting main thread never runs, so `sysml -version` printed its answer and then sat until the two-minute deadline. With every compile and GC task on the main thread there is no worker for the exit to wait on. The deadline itself now reads the process's `/proc` state before killing it and reports the elapsed time, the command, the resident size and every thread's state beside the output so far, and that path has a test of its own against a runtime that never exits.

- The Xpect oracle baseline (`docs/project/pilot-xpect-baseline.json`) is re-recorded after the part-definition rule (`A part must be typed by at least one part definition.`, three pilot `noErrors` fixtures now adjudicated in `docs/project/pilot-xpect.md`) and the unresolved-reference import hint (wording only, on sixteen already wording-only rows) moved its verdicts without the record following, which failed the nightly oracle reproduction. Pull requests now reproduce the baseline too: `pilot-xpect -check` runs as its own job in the pull-request workflow, so a rule or baseline change that moves a verdict fails before it merges rather than the next night.

- **Track `=` values in action, calculation, statement-block, and state-machine bodies.** Their dependencies are re-evaluated when read after a source changes, while `:=`, `default`, and values assigned to a binding retain their existing write behavior. An unsupplied inherited action parameter such as `z = x * 2` now follows a later write to `x` (`action_inherited_default_root`: `z` and `r` change from 6 to 20); the supplied argument in `action_inherited_supplied_parameter` remains held. Body-local calc usages remain evaluated once per declaration. Explore/check now agrees on the shared-writers `=` case with `{3}` rather than `{1,2,3}`.

- **A case local that a run left declared without a value stays unvalued in the frame its conditions
  read.** `frame.markUnvalued` allocated its map on a copy of the frame, so a mark on a frame that
  held no unvalued name yet was lost, and an objective or assertion naming such a local could read
  through to a binding of the same name instead of being undecided for the missing value.

- **A decision's branches are `TransitionUsage`s.** `if g then b;` and `else b;` after a `decide` in an action body were written as `SuccessionAsUsage`s carrying their guard only as `sysx:guard` text; they are now the `TransitionUsage`s the grammar builds (SysML.xtext `GuardedTargetSuccession`, `DefaultTargetSuccession`), the guard owned through a `TransitionFeatureMembership` of kind `guard`, the `then` succession owned as a transition's is, and the decision as `source`. Both convert back to the notation they were written in.

- Enumerating the members an import surfaces — completion through a wildcard import, an `expose`, the imported-name check — visited every simple path through a cycle of packages publicly re-importing one another, so six mutually importing packages did not finish; each import edge is now enumerated once per enumeration, as a lookup already searched it once.

- **A decision's branch stating a trigger, or another source through `sysml:sourceFeature` alone, is refused.** `if g then b;` and `else b;` have no notation for a trigger and take their source from the member before them, yet a graph stating `sysx:trigger` on such a branch was written back without it, and one stating a different source through `sysml:sourceFeature` rather than `sysml:source`, or a second source beside the member before it, passed the source check and was rebound to that member. Each is now refused as unsupported, as a branch stating another `sysml:source`, an effect or a body already is.

- **The Jupyter kernel binds its channels in `Listen` and serves them in `Serve`; `Run` is the two in sequence.** A TCP channel whose port is 0 is bound on a free port and the connection `Listen` returns names the port taken, while a port given is bound as given, so a connection file keeps naming the ports Jupyter chose. The kernel's own tests used to reserve five free ports, release them and have the kernel bind them later; another process on a busy CI runner could take one in between, and the test then waited minutes on a dial the kernel would never answer. They now bind on port 0 and dial the ports `Listen` reports, and a kernel that fails to bind fails the test at once.

- **A view listing of a file read from the analysis cache reports the rendering kind the view states.** A view whose kind came from a `render` member (`render Views::asElementTable;`, `render Views::asTextualNotation;`) was listed as a `tree` view when its file was held as its cached interface record; the editor's view list and the workspace listing now read the file's parsed body first.

- **A migrated item flow finds its ends through the parts and blocks a tool names.** An «ItemFlow» whose `informationSource` and `informationTarget` are the parts on the realizing connector's ends, the blocks typing them, or the connector's owner at its own port — as Cameo writes a flow drawn between parts — is now written as the `flow` between the ports those ends name. It was left as a comment, since only the connector ends' roles themselves were accepted; the report's note for a flow naming neither end now says what was tried.

- **The terminal prompt prints every line of a usage error.** A repository or notebook command given wrong arguments — `%load --id x`, `%load --id=a --id=b`, `%load --tags=a a.ipynb` — printed only the usage line at the prompt, leaving out the line saying what was wrong (`--id is not an option of %load`), which the Jupyter kernel's `UsageError` traceback showed. The prompt now prints the usage and the problem, as the kernel does.

- **A state usage inherits a definition that declares a view.** A `view`, `viewpoint`, `rendering` or `metadata` annotation in a state def body — what a migrated state machine diagram writes into its state def — made every usage typed by it fail to lower, so a state diagram showing such a usage rendered empty and its layout annotations warned that no node is drawn. Such members are not state content and are now passed over, like a comment or a nested definition.

- **A SysML v2 API server cannot have the bearer token paged on to another host.** A `Link: rel="next"` header naming a page on another server, on another port, or over plaintext is refused with the error a redirect there gets ("the token is for *host* only"); the page is never requested with the token. Without a token the link is followed as before.

- **The repository URL the environment supplies is held to the plaintext rule.** `FLEXO_SYSMLV2_URL` naming an `http://` server off this machine is refused by `%repo`, `%projects`, `%load` and `%publish` before any request goes out — the same refusal `%repo <url>` gives, lifted the same way by `FLEXO_ALLOW_PLAIN_HTTP=1` — instead of sending `FLEXO_INTEROP_TOKEN` in the clear.

- **A branch read before its first commit is still checked for a moved head.** A change set computed against a branch with no head commit is refused as a stale branch when another writer made the first commit in between, instead of being posted onto the commit it never saw.

- **`%publish` by name reaches the project the session loaded by id.** When the session tracks a project of that name on the server, the publish resolves it by the stored id, so a second project with the same name no longer makes the name ambiguous; `--project` naming another project and the refusal of a shared name no session project matches are unchanged.
- **A root view renders although nested elements share its name.** `-render 'Scanning Electron Microscope'` and `-render-all` refused the root-level view when parts, states or packages nested anywhere were also named `Scanning Electron Microscope`, reporting the name ambiguous although nothing more qualified can be written for a root declaration. A simple name that is a root declaration's qualified name now denotes it; nested namesakes are still reached by their qualified names.
- **An action definition that specializes another now performs the inherited steps, successions, flows and statements.** Previously `action def P :> B` silently ran only its own members, so `action def Plain :> Base;` completed with no results and `action def Extra :> Base { action b { ... } }` ran `b` but not `Base::a`. Inherited content is lowered into the specialization's flow (nearest general first, a diamond's shared general once), a step redefined by name replaces the inherited one and inherited successions retarget to it, and cyclic specialization, a redefinition naming a missing step, an incompatible replacement and an ambiguous retarget are reported as errors, and a specialization's own flows may connect inherited steps.
- **A specialization preserves gates on inherited succession flows.** A guarded succession targeting an inherited succession flow retains its guard and delivery, so the specialized action follows the same guarded path as its general.
- **A redefining action step with no multiplicity of its own now repeats as often as the step it redefines.** `action :>> a { … }` over `action a[3]` performs three times (it performed once), and `run`, `explore`, `check`, `validate` and the SMT refusal all use the same effective count; a redefining step also keeps the redefined step's body alongside its own.
- **An inherited assertion stays unchecked unless a succession or flow connection in its declaring body or elsewhere in the specializing action's inheritance chain names it.** A specialization therefore carries an assertion ordered by an intermediate general while leaving an unsequenced assertion unchecked.
- **A typed action usage, entry/do/exit behavior or performed action that states a body of its own now merges that body with the typed or performed action.** Its own members run alongside the action's inherited content as one flow, unordered unless a succession orders them, and its redefinitions replace inherited steps; an entry/do/exit behavior doing this is no longer reported at execution. A body that only binds pins still performs the action as before. An executable typed body nested in its own type, directly or through inheritance, is refused at execution rather than recursively unfolded; feature-only self-typed bodies remain lazy.
- **A specialization's body binding on an inherited action node now overrides the inherited binding of that pin.**

- **The two `SI` magnetic dipole moment units are typed by the quantity they measure.** `ISQ::*` re-exports a `MagneticDipoleMomentUnit` from both `ISQElectromagnetism` (IEC 80000-6, L^3·M·T^-2·I^-1) and `ISQAtomicNuclear` (ISO 80000-10, L^2·I), and KerML 7.2.5.4 hides a name two imports bring, so the unqualified `MagneticDipoleMomentUnit` in `SI.sysml` names nothing by the letter and the electromagnetic unit by first match. The declared errata overlay now qualifies both lines in the bundled library: `'m²⋅A'` (line 233) by `ISQAtomicNuclear::MagneticDipoleMomentUnit`, so it no longer reports a dimension mismatch, and `'Wb⋅m'` (line 303) by `ISQElectromagnetism::MagneticDipoleMomentUnit`. The published library text is unchanged; the documentation records the eight `ISQ` names the clause hides and that resolution, like the OMG pilot's, binds the first import's member.

- **Change triggers observe every completed write.** A state machine's `accept when <condition>` is re-evaluated after each completed feature write that touches a feature the condition reads, and every false-to-true rise queues one change signal, so a condition that becomes true and false again within one run-to-completion step still fires; a value one assignment writes and replaces before it completes raises no rise. Snapshots, held images, replay and `-schedule explore` carry the observed and pending rises.
- **A store's started behaviors write after the store completes.** Their feature writes are observed individually, so a true-then-false condition change inside a classifier behavior still fires; if a started behavior fails, rolling the store back also restores the change-trigger observations.

- **Every target of several successions out of an ordinary action node is performed.** A
  succession is a `HappensBefore` link that orders its source before its target and excludes no
  other target, so `first a then b; first a then c;` now performs both `b` and `c` after `a`,
  unordered relative to each other, as a fork would; a guarded succession whose guard is false
  leaves out only its own link, and two holding guards out of a non-decision node follow both
  instead of failing with "more than one succession is enabled". The same holds for statement
  nodes (`if`, `while`, `loop`, `for`, `assign`, `send`), the start node and a `[0]` step. Only a
  `decide` node chooses one branch; a `join` or `merge` with several outgoing successions is still
  refused, as their validation constraints require, and a repeated step still needs written end
  multiplicities on each edge out of it. The `smt` engine encodes the fan-out as it encodes a fork.

- **An untyped v1 property is no longer always a `ref`.** The SysML v1 migrator wrote every property without a type as `ref x;`, ignoring MagicDraw's «ValueProperty», «PartProperty», «SharedProperty», «ReferenceProperty» and «ConstraintProperty» markers and then writing the marker as an applied stereotype. An untyped property now takes the usage its marker names (`attribute`, `part`, `ref`, `constraint`), with the marker omitted as it is on typed properties; without a marker it is a `part` when its aggregation is composite and an `attribute` otherwise, reported approximated. A property whose default is an individual stays a `ref` typed by it. A slot of such an untyped `part` holding an individual, which crashed the migration, is written as `individual part :>> x : 'the individual';`, checked against every type the part inherits when it redefines, subsets or shadows typed ones.
- The behavior guide's transcripts now show what the binary prints: the `standing:` line that ends every `-action` and `-schedule explore` run, the `1 choice point` note a fork/join run reports, and the `caught.fault` result the signal-handling example lists; the `Heater` and `Tank` state machines on that page now run as written (`ScalarValues::Boolean` is qualified, and `Step` declares its entry transition).
- The `-engines` listing in the external-engines reference matches this build: the `check` and `solve` rows name every question kind they answer, and the `smt` and `tool:fmi` engines are listed.
- The grammar reference states the current size of the parser's golden AST and negative test suites.
- **The CLI and REPL documentation matches the 0.10.0 binaries.** `-migrate` is documented as writing `api-json` too; the `-engines` listings show the `sensitive` answers of `check` and `smt`, the `holds` answer of `solve` and the `tool:fmi` engine; the SMT input domain of an `Integer` is stated as unbounded, not 64-bit; the `-convert` refusal shown for an unsupported input is the SysML v1 refusal that names `-migrate`; the file-load, materialization and RDF conversion samples show what the binary prints; `environment.md` lists all eight `OPENSYSML_MAX_*` budgets, `OPENSYSML_RECORD_CACHE` and `OPENSYSML_CALC_COMPILE` among the variables that answer to a legacy `SYSML_` name, and names `tests/identity` as the pilot-library XMI gate; `repl-commands.md` shows the relationship lines, Mermaid front matter and composition edges `%render` now writes and the positional arguments `%invoke` takes; the legacy CLI page labels its cards with flags that exist (`-validate`, `-action`).
