- **The language-server lifecycle tests wait for a killed server before reading its stderr.** `TestSilentServerFailsWithinDeadline` read the captured stderr as soon as the server's stdout closed, before `os/exec` had finished copying the goroutine dump the `SIGQUIT` produced, so the dump was sometimes empty and the test failed at random under load.

- **A PDF finds an `Image` block's file beside the document's source.** `DocumentQueries::Image.location` is relative to the document's file, and `-doc-form pdf` now resolves it there as `-doc-form html` does — an output directory other than the model's no longer fails with `missing-image` — falling back to the output's directory only for a document read from standard input. HTML and Markdown write the location relative to the output's directory so the page finds the file too. A location stated in one of several files loaded together resolves against that file, not the joined session buffer, so a `Picture` on a view drawn into a document finds its file.
- **A hidden diagram symbol shows nothing on the migrated view.** A symbol MagicDraw keeps but does not draw (`visible` false), with the symbols nested in it and the parts, regions or compartment rows it lists, no longer exposes its element on the view or lends it a position or style — a hidden compartment row stays off the view though the diagram's element list names it and its owner is drawn; and a symbol's own position and style go to the element it names, not to the last part or region listed under it. Only the image kinds the migration writes (PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP, WebP, SVG) are recognized as pasted pictures; an icon is reported as no image.

- Migrated «InstanceTable»/TableStructure columns and sorts over MonteCarloAnalysis statistics are written as member-path columns and render their stored values instead of being omitted when an instance the table lists records them; otherwise the column is omitted with the note saying so.
- Document query columns and `Project`/`OrderBy` properties read quoted metadata feature names, such as `Meta::'tag with space'` and `Meta::'x::y'`, from the row's annotation.
- Metadata column paths reject malformed or undeclared feature names instead of reading an empty cell.

- **Positioned and Cameo-style DOT renderings are faithful to the drawn diagram again.** A `DiagramLayout::Note` stated in a view's body is now drawn in that view alone instead of leaking into every enclosing rendering — a note a nested view states for itself no longer appears in the outer view's drawing, and a note anchored to a node the drawing omits is dropped with a notice rather than anchored to nothing, so `neato -n` no longer places note boxes below the canvas. A view exposing both an element and one of its members no longer draws the member twice (once as a root, once under its owner). Positioned and Cameo-style drawings head each node by the shortest suffix of its qualified name that still distinguishes it among the nodes the drawing declares, as the source diagrams name their scattered elements, and positioned drawings name `layout=neato` so `dot -n` keeps the pinned positions.

- **The positioned DOT tree no longer draws a containment edge to a child boxed inside its owner's box.** Such a child draws as a compartment row of the owner, so the edge painted a connector across the box on top of the row; children boxed outside the owner's box keep their edges, and an unpositioned tree is unchanged.
- **Quoted-name escapes decode in graphical labels.** A `\n` escape in an element's name printed literally in the DOT, Mermaid and PlantUML forms instead of breaking the label line; `\t`, `\'`, `\"` and `\\` decode the same way (the glyph-less control escapes are dropped), and the Cameo frame header decodes too.
- **A note whose stated box encloses a node now paints behind it.** Graphviz paints in file order, so a large note box — a Cameo text box used as a group frame — covered the nodes it framed; enclosing notes are written before the nodes, nested frames outermost first, and captioned at their top; notes enclosing nothing stay on top.

- **A view with `DiagramLayout::Layout` positions draws the same nodes in every graph form.** Only the DOT writer left the members no position placed undrawn; the Mermaid and PlantUML forms drew the whole exposed tree, so a migrated diagram exposing a package it never drew expanded into thousands of nodes and tripped the Mermaid workload bound. One placement decision now serves `dot`, `mermaid` and `plantuml`, through `-render`, `-render-all`, document figures, the LSP and gRPC alike: the placed members and the edges between them by default, every member under `-render-unplaced strip`, and each form accounts for what it left out in its own comment syntax (`%% not represented: …` in Mermaid, `' not represented: …` in PlantUML). The bound itself is unchanged.

- **Queries identify unnamed declarations by their export-compatible position in the gRPC service, the REPL's `%query` and `sysml -query`.** Their IDs match RDF and API-JSON export and can be used as a query scope; declarations without an unambiguous export identity remain omitted.

- **Recording an analysis run into a model with a transition parameter named after its type no longer fails.** Migrated SysML v1 state machines declare transitions such as `transition first a accept s3 : s3 do action { assign s32 := s3; } then b;`. Re-checking the model after the records joined it — or after any reindex — resolved the parameter's own typing from the transition's scope, found the parameter under its name and reported `cannot bind a value of type s3 to a feature typed by s3`, so `-record-run`/`%record` of a `Simulation::MonteCarlo` sample refused with the model left unchanged. A trigger parameter is no longer a candidate for the names its own header writes: its typing names the enclosing scope's definition on a cold resolver as on the document walk, and the runs are recorded.

- **A sequence mixing a quantity with a plain number records as text.** It has no single unit to record under, so it no longer settles to a bare Real that drops the measured element's unit.
- **A repeated sequence value is refused by an existing unique record member.** Recording into a definition that declares the member `[0..*]` without `nonunique` names the `into` remedy instead of generating a record that fails validation; generated definitions, declared `ordered nonunique`, keep admitting repeats.

- **The release job no longer mistakes the platform archives for the Python sdist.** The check that one sdist is staged beside the binaries matched `opensysml-*.tar.gz`, which the `opensysml-<platform>.tar.gz` archives staged in the same directory also satisfy, so the first release to ship the Python distribution from the core tag failed its own verification with "matches 5 files, not one" and published nothing. The check now matches the sdist by its version-led name (`opensysml-[0-9]*.tar.gz`).

- **`-render-documents` writes documents whose file names differ in letter case alone instead of stopping.** Two documents `Reports::Summary` and `Reports::SUMMARY` stopped the run with "render to file names that differ only by letter case"; each is now written under its name tagged with `~` and a hash, as `-render-all` writes such views, and the same planner escapes a stem Windows reads as a device and cuts a name too long for a path component. Cross-document links point at the tagged name where one was needed, in a set and in a single document rendered on its own — by `-render-document`, `%render-document`, the LSP's `opensysml/renderDocument` or the gRPC `RenderDocument`, which all plan the set's file names the same way. Documents sharing a short name in different packages were always written apart, by qualified name, and naming one to `-render-document` by the short name alone is refused with every candidate's qualified name.

- **`OPENSYSML_TOOL_ENV_PASSTHROUGH` refuses malformed entries.** An entry containing `=` or a NUL byte is not an environment variable name; the tool's invocation now fails with an error naming the variable and the entry instead of forwarding it.

- **A migrated DocGen view's own documentation opens its section.** The comment a document view carries as its documentation was written as the `doc` of the migrated `view` but never reached the `DocumentQueries::Section`, so a rendered document showed a heading with the view's prose missing wherever the text was the view's documentation rather than a collaborator paragraph or a method step. The section now opens with that documentation as a `Paragraph`, before the method's content and at every depth of the view tree, as DocGen prints it; a comment that is also shown by one of the view's collaborator paragraphs is written once, in its place, while a malformed collaborator application over it is refused without hiding the documentation.

- **Tool previews do not print passed-through environment values.** `-tool-dry-run` and `%tool` list variables taken from this process (`PATH`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `LANG` and the `OPENSYSML_TOOL_ENV_PASSTHROUGH` names) as `NAME=<from this process>`; only the manifest's own `env` entries show their rendered values.

- **Parser and structural validation now follow the grammar for enumeration prefixes and result parameters.** Enumeration definitions reject definition prefixes, requirement-like bodies reject return parameters, and duplicate returns are reported across function and expression kinds.

- **An action's pins are drawn on its border, and object flows run pin to pin.** A migrated action's `in`/`out` parameters were each written as a detached `shape=note` node at the pin's bounds, too small for its name, and the flow ended beside the action in mid-air. A directed parameter, a bound result and a pin a flow names are the node's ports now — in the rendering as `Node.Ports` and `Edge.FromPort`/`ToPort`, and in DOT as 12 px squares set on the action's border where the route meets it with the pin's name in small type beside them — and the flow edge is attached to the pins, so its `out to in` text is no longer needed there. An unplaced action draws its pins as cells of its label instead. The `opensysml/render` reply and the text form carry the ports and the flow's ends.
- **An action whose name the model did not give heads with what it does.** A migrated UML value specification action (`action value3 { out result : Boolean = true; }`) read **action** over the detail `own flow`; a call read the same over `own flow`. Such a node is headed now by the literal its result is bound to (`true`, `"SH-0"`), an accept's trigger, a send's message, the one assignment its body makes, or `: Type` for what it calls, and `own flow` is shown only when the nested flow draws nodes of its own.
- **Forks, joins, decisions, merges, choices, junctions and history pseudo-states are drawn as Cameo draws them.** A fork or join was a filled box at a fixed size with its synthesized name inside it, over the neighbouring action; it takes its `DiagramLayout::Layout` bounds now, standing or lying as the box is longer, with no text inside and a name the model gave beside it. Every pseudo-state the lowered state graph knows is drawn as its symbol at its stated bounds — the initial dot and its outgoing transition, a junction's dot, a choice's diamond, the final bull's-eye, a terminate, and a shallow or deep history as a ring lettered `H` or `H*` — where an initial pseudo-state and a junction were dropped from a placed rendering before.
- **A routed edge is drawn to both of its stated ends.** The spline kept its `e,x,y` end alone and its curve ran to the head's tip, so Graphviz clipped the last segment and the head landed short; a tail arrow lost its end altogether. The spline now carries `s,x,y` and `e,x,y` as its arrows call for, its curve stopping an arrow's length short of each, and a node's routed box never grows past its stated one, so the drawn ends are the stated ends (a test runs Graphviz when `OPENSYSML_DOT` names it and asserts every drawn endpoint within 3 px of its route's).
- **A transition is labelled once, in Cameo's form, beside its route.** Every routed transition carried two labels — a fitted `label` at the midpoint and the full text as an `xlabel` beside it — and the Cameo style kept the `accept` keyword. One `label` is written now, `trigger [guard] / effect` with `accept` and quotes off under `-render-style cameo`, and it is placed beside the segment of the route where it covers the fewest state boxes and notes.
- **A state's compartment names its behaviours in every style.** A state's line read `entry`, `do`, `defers` when the behaviour was an anonymous usage typed by an action; it reads `entry / prime`, `do / Initialize`, `exit / Settle` now — the behaviour's name, else its type's, else what its body does — and `«state»` is not written in the Cameo style, which shows a keyword only for a stereotyped state.
- **A migrated diagram's comment notes reach the view, anchored to the states they annotate.** The migration took a `Note` symbol naming an element that is no Comment — Cameo's note showing a state's documentation — for the element's own symbol, so the note was never written and its anchor was dropped, while every name and stereotype label nested in a symbol was counted as a note, so the report's note count was inflated. A `Note` symbol whatever it names, a comment's symbol and a free text box are the notes now, a label nested in an element's symbol is not, a note anchored to several elements is one `Note about` them all — drawn as one box with an anchor to each, while notes stated apart stay apart even when their text and box coincide — and a region's initial transition is written as a named `transition … first start then s;` when a diagram routes it, so the route is drawn from the initial dot.
- **A route of a control flow the migration split at a made-up join is drawn where Cameo drew it.** The route was pinned to every member the flow was written as, including the succession into the join no symbol stands for; it is pinned now to the members whose both ends the diagram draws. The migration marks such a member `MigrationMetadata::StandIn` besides its synthesized name, and in a positioned rendering a fork, join or merge so marked, which no diagram positions, is elided and the edges through it joined pairwise along their routes, the notice counting the nodes elided and the routeless pairs left undrawn; a control node the source had but left unnamed keeps its symbol.
- **A layout stated for a body's `start` or `done` positions that body's alone.** Every action and state body inherits the one library `start`, so `metadata Layout about Acquire::start` positioned the `start` of every body a view drew, the first stated winning. A `LayoutSite` now records the namespace an `about` clause named the member through (`Via`), and a synthetic member's layout is looked up under the body it is drawn in (`MemberLayoutOf`).
- **A stated box's label is fitted at the size the node is drawn in, and within the Cameo compartment table.** A node whose `Style` set a font size was measured and fitted at the skin's size, so a label that fit at the skin's 11 pt was drawn at the style's 14 pt and Graphviz reported the box too small; the fitting starts from the style's size now, a shrunk size is written relative to it, and an unstated box is sized to its label at that size. A Cameo label with detail lines is fitted to the box less the table's cell padding (4 pt of width, 8 pt of height), and when no detail line fits there the table is dropped and the title alone is fitted to the whole box. A line is fitted at 1.25 em, the height the font's own metrics give it under Graphviz, rather than the 1.2 em estimate a box could still come out too small under; and a stated note too narrow for the `«comment»` head leaves it off rather than overflow its box.
- **A migrated Cameo table shows the rows, columns and nesting the tool shows.** The reader now
  keeps an instance or generic table's `excludedElements`, `displayMode` (`List`, `Compact tree`,
  `Complete tree`), `showScopeAsRoot`, `expandedRows`, `columnWidth`, stereotype-tag columns
  (`QPROP:stereotypeTags:<<Profile::Stereotype>>.tag`) and the row filter saved with it, and the
  migration lowers each: the rows of an instance table are the individual definitions its
  instance specifications became, not the slots typed by them, excluded rows are subtracted with
  `Except`, a tree mode nests the rows
  with `Tree` so a nested instance is listed at its depth under the row containing it, its name
  as written, `showScopeAsRoot` adds the scope as the root, `QPROP:Element:Id` and `Text` on
  requirements read the short name and documentation the migration wrote the «Requirement» tags
  to, `QPROP:Element:classifier` reads `general`, which now lists a usage's types as well as a
  definition's, a user stereotype's tag reads
  the metadata def feature it became (every value of a multi-valued tag), widths become
  `Table.columnWidths`, and the saved filter becomes `WhereText` over the projected columns its
  `ChoiceProperty` value selects — every column when the selection is empty, and no filter when the
  query reads none of the selected columns. The rows a table
  lists explicitly lead the scope's other elements in the order the tool listed them, so an
  unsorted table and each level of a tree keep that order. Each column is headed as the tool
  headed it — `Id`, `Text`, `classifier`, a tag's name over both of two columns reading the same
  tag — through the new `Table.columnLabels`, while the column names the query, `groupBy` and
  HTML `data-column` know stay unique. An element-valued
  cell — a `general`, a tag value, a feature whose declared value names an element of the model
  such as an enumeration literal, which was printed as the qualified name it was written with —
  is that element, printed by its effective name, the one the `name` property reads, as Cameo
  prints a classifier or a tag value; HTML carries the qualified name in `data-element`, and
  `WhereFeature` compares such a cell as the name it prints, or as its qualified name against a
  qualified value. A user profile
  marked «auxiliaryResource» is migrated like any other user profile, since its stereotypes'
  applications carry the user's data. The report row states each setting applied and each still
  refused.
- **Wide tables fit their pages in PDF.** A table of many columns collapsed its name column to a
  few characters and spread a handful of rows over dozens of pages. Cells now wrap at word
  boundaries and break a token only when nothing else fits, the header repeats on every page,
  rows do not split, a wide table's first column keeps a readable width, stated `columnWidths`
  size the columns proportionally though never narrower than a heading's longest word, and a
  table whose headings need more than the line its column count has — a portrait page in the
  ordinary type under seven columns, a landscape page in the wide type under eleven — is set on
  a landscape page in the dense type when they fit that line, whatever a theme sets ordinary
  cells at, and a table whose headings overrun even that line — or of more than twelve columns —
  is written on it as continuation tables that repeat the first column, at no less than its
  readable width, ahead of the columns that head unbroken beside it, under the caption marked
  "(continued)", in HTML and in the Markdown the pandoc engine converts alike; an unsized wide
  table's columns share the width evenly, none narrower than its heading, and headings that
  need more than the line between them share it in proportion rather than overrunning the page; a
  table without a caption continues without one. `DocumentQueries` gains `Tree`, `WhereText`, `Table.columnWidths`
  and `Table.columnLabels`; rows carry a nesting depth that Markdown marks with `↳` and HTML with
  `data-depth`.
- **The parser now enforces the declaration shape of relationship clauses on classifiers and features.** A classifier declaration admits a single `:>`/`specializes` list ahead of its `disjoint from`/`unions`/`intersects`/`differences` clauses and none of the feature clauses (`:`, `subsets`, `:>>`, `::>`, `=>`, `chains`, `inverse of`, `featured by`), so forms like `part def X :> A :> B;` are now rejected with a diagnostic instead of being silently accepted (#615). On features, specializations must precede relationship clauses, `ordered`/`nonunique` may each be stated once, and a KerML classifier's multiplicity must precede its specialization list. KerML-only relationship clauses written in a `.sysml` file are now reported as KerML notation, matching the existing `featured by` warning.
- **The OpenSysML libraries now validate clean against the OMG pilot implementation.** The `DocumentQueries` and `MOSA` parameter values are `default` so a named argument may override them; `MOSA::ModularSystemInterface` is abstract and `StandardMetadata`'s `baseType` a default so `ConsensusStandardMetadata` may rebind it; the `MOSA` and `OOSEM` views satisfy their viewpoints as `satisfy requirement` usages; and `Simulation::MonteCarlo` declares its `subject analysed` first.
- **The SysML v1 migration writes references the v2 accessibility rules admit.** A reception's method reads the accepted signal's attributes through the accept that holds the payload (`receive.setLevel.value`, not `setLevel.value`, a sibling action's feature being reachable only by dot notation); an «Allocate» between two definitions, which no `allocate` can end on, is written as an `allocation def` whose two `end` features are typed by them; and a stereotype tag referring to a definition is written as the element cast to its metaclass (`owner = Acme meta SysML::PartDefinition;`), since a type is not a value. The reference implementation accepts each; before, it rejected them.
- **A block's activity that reads the block's features is written as an action usage of the block.** A `Run` whose body assigns an attribute, accepts a signal at a port, calls an operation of a part, or weighs a decision by a «Probability» property, is `action run { … }` on the `part def` (`perform action run { … }` for the classifier behavior), its body running on the object and reaching its features by their bare names — a definition nested in the block reaches none, so the reference implementation rejected `accept sig via rx`, `perform action x ::> motor.spin` and `p = bias` written in one. Calls, receptions, state behaviors, interactions, simulation configurations and Monte Carlo analyses name the usage (`perform action x ::> run;`), and an activity some call cannot reach a usage from — one whose target pin holds a parameter or a created object — stays an `action def`. An «Allocate» ending on such an activity's node is `allocate run.measure to probe;` in the part def when the activity is performed; one ending on a node of an `action def`, or of an `action` usage a call runs (a constant feature, which the part's variable `allocate` cannot relate), is a plain `dependency`, with the reason.
- **The checker reports a connector end and an annotation value that name a definition.** `allocate A to B` or `connect A to a` where `A` is a definition, and `@Org { owner = Acme; }` binding a `part def`, now report `Must be a valid feature`, as the reference implementation does; the checker was silent on both.
- **SysML v1 migration writes a decision's `else` branch right after the `decide` node.** It was written after the decision's named guarded successions, where the SysML v2 grammar admits no `else <target>;` (a target succession sequences from the member written right before it); the pinned pilot implementation rejected it with `no viable alternative at input 'else'` and lost the rest of the file.
- **`then <target>;`, `if <guard> then <target>;` and `else <target>;` after a member that is not an action node are reported as OpenSysML notation** (`nonstandard-notation`, an error under `-strict`): after a `succession`, a comment, a two-ended `first a then b;` or a structural usage the grammar has no production for them and their source is unstated.
- **The SysML v1 migration renames a top-level package named like a standard library root.** A qualified name starting with `Requirements`, `Views` or another library root resolved to the library package rather than the migrated one, so references into it broke under the OMG pilot validator; the migration now writes such a package as `<name>Model`, records the source name in a `MigrationMetadata::LibraryNameAvoided` annotation, and reports the rename as approximated.
- **The checker warns about a root package or root-level alias named like a standard library package.** The new `library-root-name` name-resolution warning marks the portability hazard on any model, not only migrated ones.
- **The migration writes a note about a transition effect it cannot run before the transition, not inside its declaration.** The SysML v2 grammar admits a `/* */` comment only where a member may appear, so the pilot implementation rejected the previous placement; OpenSysML now warns (`nonstandard-notation`, an error under `-strict`) when a regular comment is written where no member may start, while still reading it.
- **CSV `errorColumn` refuses on any data row, not only the last.** A `csv` reply's `errorColumn` now fails the performance when any data record's cell in that column is non-empty, so a refusal on an earlier row is no longer missed when `row:` selects a different record.

- **Misspelled type names after `accept` in transitions now report unresolved references.** Bare, qualified, named-payload, and subsetting type references resolve normally; unqualified misspellings receive did-you-mean suggestions where applicable. The OpenSysML `when <name>` injected-signal spelling is unchanged.

- Route port-qualified accepts in nested definitions through the definition context during migration and validation, while preserving direct port references in behavior usages.
- Declare and bind reception contexts for port routes emitted by non-block owners.
- Resolve send-with-body `via` targets as route members for diagnostics and editor navigation.

- **Explicit succession end multiplicities no longer produce the end-feature warning.** The warning is limited to source-end multiplicities on shorthand action-body successions, matching the ownership distinction in the model.

- A chained write (`assign a.b := v`) whose step holds a collection of exactly one value now reaches that one object, as a chained read already did; a step holding none is reported as an uninitialized feature value and one holding several keeps the "a write reaches one object" refusal.

- **An `ApplyEdits` batch resolves its references against the whole batch.** A succession (`first g;`, `then g;`, a guarded `then`) or a `#Tag` metadata prefix an operation writes may now name a member or metadata definition a later operation of the same batch declares, as typed members already could; the check runs once against the model the batch leaves, so operation order no longer decides it. Each reference is followed through the later operations' rewrites, so a rename of what it names, or of the declaration carrying it, is judged under the new name, and a duplicate prefix is refused whatever its target is renamed to. A reference nothing in the batch declares is refused as before, at the same operation and with the same message; a body that sequences `first g;` no longer keeps a member named `g` from being declared, nor hides a `g` the body inherits from a succession.

- **An action's pins are drawn on its border, and object flows run pin to pin.** A migrated action's `in`/`out` parameters were each written as a detached `shape=note` node at the pin's bounds, too small for its name, and the flow ended beside the action in mid-air. A directed parameter, a bound result and a pin a flow names are the node's ports now — in the rendering as `Node.Ports` and `Edge.FromPort`/`ToPort`, and in DOT as 12 px squares set on the action's border where the route meets it with the pin's name in small type beside them — and the flow edge is attached to the pins, so its `out to in` text is no longer needed there. An unplaced action draws its pins as cells of its label instead. The `opensysml/render` reply and the text form carry the ports and the flow's ends.
- **An action whose name the model did not give heads with what it does.** A migrated UML value specification action (`action value3 { out result : Boolean = true; }`) read **action** over the detail `own flow`; a call read the same over `own flow`. Such a node is headed now by the literal its result is bound to (`true`, `"SH-0"`), an accept's trigger, a send's message, the one assignment its body makes, or `: Type` for what it calls, and `own flow` is shown only when the nested flow draws nodes of its own.
- **Forks, joins, decisions, merges, choices, junctions and history pseudo-states are drawn as Cameo draws them.** A fork or join was a filled box at a fixed size with its synthesized name inside it, over the neighbouring action; it takes its `DiagramLayout::Layout` bounds now, standing or lying as the box is longer, with no text inside and a name the model gave beside it. Every pseudo-state the lowered state graph knows is drawn as its symbol at its stated bounds — the initial dot and its outgoing transition, a junction's dot, a choice's diamond, the final bull's-eye, a terminate, and a shallow or deep history as a ring lettered `H` or `H*` — where an initial pseudo-state and a junction were dropped from a placed rendering before.
- **A routed edge is drawn to both of its stated ends.** The spline kept its `e,x,y` end alone and its curve ran to the head's tip, so Graphviz clipped the last segment and the head landed short; a tail arrow lost its end altogether. The spline now carries `s,x,y` and `e,x,y` as its arrows call for, its curve stopping an arrow's length short of each, and a node's routed box never grows past its stated one, so the drawn ends are the stated ends (a test runs Graphviz when `OPENSYSML_DOT` names it and asserts every drawn endpoint within 3 px of its route's).
- **A transition is labelled once, in Cameo's form, beside its route.** Every routed transition carried two labels — a fitted `label` at the midpoint and the full text as an `xlabel` beside it — and the Cameo style kept the `accept` keyword. One `label` is written now, `trigger [guard] / effect` with `accept` and quotes off under `-render-style cameo`, and it is placed beside the segment of the route where it covers the fewest state boxes and notes.
- **A state's compartment names its behaviours in every style.** A state's line read `entry`, `do`, `defers` when the behaviour was an anonymous usage typed by an action; it reads `entry / prime`, `do / Initialize`, `exit / Settle` now — the behaviour's name, else its type's, else what its body does — the triggers a state defers are named too (`defers Reset, Halt`, each `Reset / defer` line in the Cameo compartment), and `«state»` is not written in the Cameo style, which shows a keyword only for a stereotyped state.
- **A migrated diagram's comment notes reach the view, anchored to the states they annotate.** The migration took a `Note` symbol naming an element that is no Comment — Cameo's note showing a state's documentation — for the element's own symbol, so the note was never written and its anchor was dropped, while every name and stereotype label nested in a symbol was counted as a note, so the report's note count was inflated. A `Note` symbol whatever it names, a comment's symbol and a free text box are the notes now, a label nested in an element's symbol is not, a note anchored to several elements is one `Note about` them all — drawn as one box with an anchor to each, while notes stated apart stay apart even when their text and box coincide — and a region's initial transition is written as a named `transition … first start then s;` when a diagram routes it, so the route is drawn from the initial dot.
- **A route of a control flow the migration split at a made-up join is drawn where Cameo drew it.** The route was pinned to every member the flow was written as, including the succession into the join no symbol stands for; it is pinned now to the members whose both ends the diagram draws. The migration marks such a member `MigrationMetadata::StandIn` besides its synthesized name, and in a positioned rendering a fork, join or merge so marked, which no diagram positions, is elided and the edges through it joined pairwise along their routes, the notice counting the nodes elided and the routeless pairs left undrawn; a control node the source had but left unnamed keeps its symbol.
- **A layout stated for a body's `start` or `done` positions that body's alone.** Every action and state body inherits the one library `start`, so `metadata Layout about Acquire::start` positioned the `start` of every body a view drew, the first stated winning. A `LayoutSite` now records the namespace an `about` clause named the member through (`Via`), and a synthetic member's layout is looked up under the body it is drawn in (`MemberLayoutOf`).
- **A stated box's label is fitted at the size the node is drawn in, and within the Cameo compartment table.** A node whose `Style` set a font size was measured and fitted at the skin's size, so a label that fit at the skin's 11 pt was drawn at the style's 14 pt and Graphviz reported the box too small; the fitting starts from the style's size now, a shrunk size is written relative to it, and an unstated box is sized to its label at that size. A Cameo label with detail lines is fitted to the box less the table's cell padding (4 pt of width, 8 pt of height), and when no detail line fits there the table is dropped and the title alone is fitted to the whole box. A line is fitted at 1.25 em, the height the font's own metrics give it under Graphviz, rather than the 1.2 em estimate a box could still come out too small under; and a stated note too narrow for the `«comment»` head leaves it off rather than overflow its box.

- **A lone carriage return in a comment now reads as a line break.** KerML 1.1 §8.2.2.1 counts `\r`, `\n` and `\r\n` as line terminators, but `Comment::body` processing (§8.2.3.3.2) and the documentation prose treated only `\n` and `\r\n` as line breaks. A comment written with lone `\r` line endings therefore kept each later line's `*` margin in its body, in reflective reads and in the API JSON and RDF exports. It now reads the same as its `\n` spelling.

- **A connection typed by an allocation or interface definition is accepted.** `connection h : H` where `H` is an `allocation def` (or an `interface def`) was reported as "A connection must be typed by connection definitions.", though the metamodel's AllocationDefinition and InterfaceDefinition specialize ConnectionDefinition. A connection is now typed by any connection definition; one typed by a part or action definition is still reported.

- **`-convert` of several files refuses to write over one of them.** `sysml a.sysml b.sysml -convert ttl -o b.sysml` replaced `b.sysml` with the graph; `-o` naming one of the model's files, or a link to one, is now refused as the single-file path already refuses it.

- **A dependency's prefix metadata is an Annotation.** `#Tag dependency a to b;` wrote the metadata usage under an `OwningMembership` of the dependency; the grammar's PrefixMetadataAnnotation makes it the annotating element of an `Annotation` the dependency owns, which the Turtle and API JSON forms now write and read back (#652). A usage's prefix metadata stays an `OwningMembership` (PrefixMetadataMember).

- End every syntax node's span at its last token (or the comment body it owns) instead of at the next token's start, so diagnostic ranges in the CLI, LSP and gRPC service, and LSP reference and declaration locations, no longer cover the whitespace and line comments after the name or declaration they report.

- **Replacing documentation keeps its visibility.** `add_documentation` with `replace` rewrites only the `doc` declaration, so an explicit `public`, `private` or `protected` in front of the replaced `doc` is kept rather than dropped.

- **RDF conversion no longer refuses two members of one namespace sharing a name.** `sysml -convert ttl` and `sysml -convert json` used to fail on `part def A; part def A;` because a qualified name was the element's identity in the graph. The first member keeps the qualified name now and each later one is identified by its position among the owner's members, as an element declared unnamed already is, so both export as separate elements and `model.to_api_json()` returns them with distinct ids. The duplicate is still reported as a warning when the model is analyzed — SysML has no reopening of a declaration within a namespace, so merge the declarations (or rename) rather than rely on the position. Names that spell positions or contain `::` are quoted in the identity so they remain distinct without moving existing names.

- **`ExecuteState` and `ExecuteAction` on a performer report the performer's attributes.** A behavior run with `performer_symbol_id` (`performer=` in the Python client) already took its guards and expressions against the performer's feature values and wrote the performer, but an executed response carried only the behavior's own values, so two performers leaving different values looked alike. `ExecuteStateResponse.final_context` now holds the performer's attributes under `this.` (`this.speed`, `this.seen`), as an explored outcome's `outputs` already did. `ExecuteActionResponse` gains `performer_attributes` (field 7) with the same entries, leaving `outputs` the action's output parameters alone; the clients expose it as `ActionRun.Performer` (Go), `ActionRun.performer()` (Java) and the `performer` attribute of the `ActionOutputs` dict `execute_action` returns (Python). Without a performer neither response changes.

- Converting RDF back to notation no longer shortens a reference to its own enclosing element to an empty name.

- **A view with many exposes no longer stalls analysis.** Each expose's target was resolved afresh every time a name was looked up in the view, and each resolution searched the other exposes' unresolved targets again, in every order: a view with 11 exposes and one metadata usage took 35 s, and one with 12 did not finish (#636). An import's resolved target is now kept, as the OMG pilot keeps a linked cross-reference; a miss is still never kept, since it may only mean that sibling imports were suspended. Twenty exposes analyse in half a second.

- Filter conditions that read through a feature chain rooted in a feature with no featuring type (`import P::*[root.inner.k == 3]`, or a read feature whose value is computed from its siblings) are now evaluated through every hop and through the value expression, as KerML §8.3.4.8.4–8.3.4.8.5 make them model-level evaluable; a chain that passes through a feature with its own value, one whose read feature has no value of its own, or that ends in a value that is not a number or boolean, or that reads a feature of a metaclass, still reports that it is not evaluated.

- **A flow's ends export as `FlowEnd`s.** In the Turtle and API JSON forms, each end of a `flow` or `succession flow` was a `ReferenceUsage` subsetting the whole chain it named. It is now a `FlowEnd`, as the grammar's FlowEndMember says: its `ReferenceSubsetting` names all but the last segment and its owned FlowFeature redefines the last, so `a.p.fuel` subsets `a.p` and redefines `fuel`. The ends read back as written, from the graph alone too (#651).

- **A flow's payload is a `PayloadFeature` under a `FeatureMembership`.** `of T`, `of T[1]` and `of p : T = v` were written as the expression `sysx:payload`, and a declared payload was not written as an element at all, so `m.p` could not reach it and a graph without source text came back as `of p`. The feature now carries the payload's name, typing, multiplicity and value, and the head is rebuilt from it (#652).

- **Packages that publicly import one another in a cycle now analyse in polynomial time.** Looking a name up through imports searched every path through the cycle, so six mutually importing packages never finished validating (#633); each import is now searched once per lookup, as the spec's visible-membership rule intends.

- **A usage declared without a kind keyword is now classified as a referenceUsage.** `x : Integer;`, `ref x : Integer;` and direction-prefixed parameters (`in energy : …`) carry the `referenceUsage` kind and report `@type = ReferenceUsage`, matching what RDF export already wrote (SysML v2 §7.6.3–7.6.4); `attribute`-keyword usages keep `attributeUsage`. Every consumer that treated a kindless usage as an attribute still does — only the metamodel name and the symbol kind differ.

- **A qualified legacy trigger beside the trigger structure reads back.** A graph carrying both a transition's `triggerAction` and an earlier mapping's `sysx:trigger` or `sysml:via` was refused when the text named the payload type or port by a different qualification than the structure writes (`sysx:trigger "T::Sig"` on `accept Sig`). Both spellings are now compared by the element they name from the transition, including quoted names with spaces, global `$::` names and standard library types, so agreeing names read and a name reaching another element is still refused.

- **A root package named like a standard library package is reached from the workspace's other files again.** With `Base` declared in one file and `private import Base::*;` in a package of another, the import resolved to the library's `Base` and its members went unresolved; the workspace's own declaration now shadows the library's from every document, as it already did within one file and as v0.9.0 did with all files in one root.

- **A `message` owns its ends as `EventOccurrenceUsage`s, and a `transition` owns its `then` succession.** The RDF and API-JSON export wrote a `message m from a to b;` end as an unnamed `ReferenceUsage` under an `EndFeatureMembership` — the connector-end shape, so a `message` read back as `flow` — and a `TransitionUsage` carried only `sysml:source`/`sysml:target` with no `SuccessionAsUsage`. A message's ends are now `sysml:EventOccurrenceUsage`s owned through `sysml:ParameterMembership`s (SysML.xtext `MessageEventMember`), and a transition owns its `then` edge as a `sysml:SuccessionAsUsage` through a `sysml:OwningMembership` (SysML.xtext `TransitionSuccessionMember`), stated as `sysml:succession` — the empty source end plus the connector end whose `ReferenceSubsetting` names the target. `flow` ends keep the connector shape, a `message` reads back as `message` from the graph alone — its ends included when the graph states their `ParameterMembership`s only from the membership's side (`sysml:membershipOwningNamespace` or `sysml:owningRelatedElement`), with no `sysml:ownedMembership` on the flow — the owned succession is implied rather than written back as a member, and a succession whose ends disagree with the transition's `sysml:source`/`sysml:target` is refused. When a graph links only some of a message's ends from the flow itself and the rest from the memberships' side, `sysx:memberIndex` or the flow's `sysml:sourceFeature`/`sysml:targetFeature` orders them — a message neither source orders is refused rather than written with its ends reversed.

- **A behavior's time expressions can read its owner's features.** Relative-time accept events, duration-constraint bounds, and change-event conditions are now considered when choosing an action usage or context parameter; pure literal durations and absolute time events remain unchanged.

- **The v1→v2 migration no longer writes `this.<feature>` inside behavior definitions.** In SysML v2 `this` denotes the definition's own occurrence, so the emitted code was unresolvable. A block-owned definition whose body needs its owner now gains an appended `in ref context : <Block>;` parameter and reads `context.<feature>` (`assign context.level := value;`, `via context.tx`, `p = context.bias`, `::> context.motor.spin`); usages owned by the block keep reading features lexically (`assign level := value;`), and every call site binds the parameter inside its own body as `in ref :>> context = <expr>;` instead of a `bind` statement. `this.<feature>` inside a definition now resolves against the definition's own occurrence, matching the standard's `Occurrence::this` semantics.
- **Positional invocation of an action with a trailing `in ref` context parameter now binds the performer.** `InvokeOperationWith` normalizes positional arguments to named before seeding the implicit reference binding, so `obj.nudge(delta)` runs instead of failing `no argument for context`; a redefinition binding a reference parameter to an instance of an unrelated type now fails the declaration check rather than slipping through, and `this` read as a value inside an action- or state-definition body statement denotes the definition's own occurrence rather than erroring.
- **`this` inside a behavior definition run directly denotes the run's own occurrence**, materialized the first time it is denoted and seeded with the performance's own feature values, instead of erroring or denoting the performer.
- **Inside a nested action or state usage body an owner's features now spell bare** (`assign level := value;`, `via tx`, `to drive.motor`, `assign ctrl.got := …`), since `this` there is the usage's own occurrence, not the object; a feature a nearer parameter or local shadows spells through the owning definition (`Bench::level`, `Scope::azimuth`), and assignments, sends, and `inout` write-backs through such qualified targets now reach the performing object's feature.

- **Migrated documents sorted by documentation now list elements in the order their generated `OrderBy` query returns them.** The migration planner keyed each element on the exact comment body, while the query's `documentation` property reads the displayed documentation. A single-line doc (`A `) and a multiline one (`A\nZ\n`) with the same opening text therefore sorted in opposite orders.

- **The SysML v1 migrator routes sends through the ports of a viewpoint or view nested in the object an activity acts on.** A block's own activity sends via `context.Review.tx`, and an activity using sibling viewpoint ports or mixing a definition's own ports with a nested viewpoint's ports takes a context typed by their shared definition instead of losing the routes.

- **The SysML v1 migration counts every read of a block's features its writer spells when deciding how a block's behavior reaches them.** The analysis that gives a nested `action def` its `in ref context : <Block>;` parameter, or writes the behavior as an action usage of the block, now resolves opaque bodies as the writer does — guards on control flows, pin values and other opaque expressions as much as opaque actions, and names a swimlane resolves through one of the block's parts (`'ESW Seq'.i`) as much as the block's own features. Before, a definition whose only owner reads were guards, or whose reads went through a partition's part, read them bare (`if i >= maxSearchCircles`, `assign 'ESW Seq'.i := …`), which the accessibility rules reject; such a behavior is now `context.i` in a def, or bare in a usage whose body runs on the object. A definition nested in a behavior written as a usage takes the block as its context, since it is its own occurrence; a definition whose calls bind through ports keeps the port owner's context. A behavior that reads its owner and is called only from other definitions of the block keeps the parameter when they bind it, without making a caller outside the block name an object of it. A name a body declares as a local (`let count = 1; count += 1;`) is no read of the block's feature so called, and the parameter a definition takes is the same whichever of the blocks calling each other's behaviors the migration reaches first. Definitions of the block calling each other in a cycle all take the block once one of them reads it, binding each other's parameters to their own rather than to the caller's occurrence; a definition no block owns takes the block its callees read as its own parameter; a part holding a collection of a block's objects, or a number of them its bounds do not tell, is no one object for a caller to bind a parameter to or perform a usage on, so the caller leaves it unbound and says so; and a swimlane over one of the block's parts is a read of the block, not a port of it, so a definition reading its owner through one keeps the owner as its context over another block's ports, whose send then loses the port and says so. A body the writer keeps as a comment — a guard or an opaque action it cannot translate — reads nothing of the block, so it neither makes the behavior a usage of the block nor displaces the port owner's context, and a definition declares its context parameter only for names it writes; a behavior whose only such reads are in kept-as-comment bodies is now a plain definition.

- **The SysML v1 migrator keeps a parameter's multiplicity where it redeclares or binds it.** An operation's context body and a reception's parameter bindings wrote every parameter as `[1]`, so an optional (`[0..1]`) or plural (`[0..*]`) v1 parameter became required and single in v2, and a reception binding a plural signal attribute was refused. Both now write the source multiplicity, `ordered`/`nonunique` included. A v1 parameter or property that writes no multiplicity but is `ordered` or `nonunique` now writes `[1] ordered` rather than a bare `ordered`, which v2 reads as `[0..*]`.

- **Make SysML v1 migration output conform for document columns, nested behavior contexts, state transitions, and diagram metadata.** Feature-backed columns use typed cells, Monte Carlo statistics use member paths, nested behaviors retain definition-typed contexts, transition endpoints use dotted state paths, and shadowed diagram metadata fields are explicitly redefined.
- Invocation argument values, chained assignment targets, and state-transition endpoints are now checked for accessibility from the context where they are written.
- A v1 initial transition into an orthogonal region is reported as unmapped because it has no well-formed v2 form.

- **DocGen tables can now display attributes owned by part usages as columns.**

- **The SysML v1 migrator preserves a context parameter specializing a viewpoint when its ports give an activity its context.** Nested calls bind it at their call sites instead of dropping the parameter. A viewpoint or view nested in a definition is reached through a context parameter typed by that definition, as `context.<viewpoint>.<port>`.

- **Warn on non-unit succession source multiplicities inside nested action bodies.** Validation checks if/else, while, loop, and for bodies in their own scopes, matching the pinned validator.

- Resolve sequence references to declarations made earlier in the same action body without leaking names between sibling branches or out of their body.

- Parse source multiplicities before `then` in nested action-body forms and diagnose malformed items without panicking.

- A performance occurrence now holds each of its parameters at the parameter's §7.6.3 effective multiplicity: a bare `in x : T;` materializes and stores its binding as `[0..*]` rather than the assumed `[1..1]`, so an earlier parameter default reading `this` no longer strands a later bare parameter's empty binding into a multiplicity violation.

- A parameter subsetting several features now takes the intersection of their ranges as its effective multiplicity — `[0..1]` and `[1..*]` bound it to `[1..1]` — instead of the first subsetted bound found, so the written order of the subsets no longer changes whether the parameter is required.

- **Use effective parameter multiplicities when checking action-node declarations.** Bare input parameters can hold no values, while explicit bounds remain enforced.

- **Reference-form assertions accept globally qualified references.** `assert` and `assert not` can refer to features rooted at `$::`, including through a dotted feature chain.

- **A part usage typed only by non-part definitions is rejected.** `part bread : Start;` with `Start` an item definition now reports `A part must be typed by at least one part definition.` (SysML v2 validatePartUsagePartDefinition); an untyped part, one inheriting a part definition through subsetting or redefinition, and a bare `variant x;` reference of a variation are still accepted.

- **A declared flow payload takes `ordered` and `nonunique`.** `flow of p : T[*] ordered from a to b;` was a syntax error, though the payload's multiplicity part allows them (SysML-textual-bnf PayloadFeatureSpecializationPart, MultiplicityPart). They are parsed, exported on the `PayloadFeature` and written back.

- **A performed action's unbound input ends that performance, not its performer's creation.** Materializing a part whose performed action leaves an `in` parameter of a nested step bound by nothing where its effective multiplicity requires a value (`[1..]`; a bare `in` is optional under §7.6.3) no longer fails with `unbound parameter`; the failure is recorded on the performance — ended for good, taking no step and receiving no message — so the object's other features still evaluate. Every other failure of a performed action, and any failure of an explicitly started behavior (`perform obj.beh.start`), still fails as before.

- **The pilot batch validator loads `.kerml` files.** `validate-sysml-batch` accepted only `.sysml`, so a model checked together with OpenSysML's bundled KerML libraries (`RandomFunctions`, `OpenSysMLMathFunctions`) reported every reference into them as unresolved; it now reads both languages into one resource set and validates each file with its own language's pilot validator.

- **RDF qualified names quote names that would otherwise read as positions, contain `::`, or begin with a literal apostrophe.** A member named `'@2'` now has identity `P::'@2'`, distinct from the positional identity `P::@2`; names that need no quotes keep their existing IRI spellings, and backslashes and apostrophes are escaped inside quotes.

- **A «Probability» naming a property of an enclosing block now migrates to a valid read.** An action def nested in a behavior of a block nested in the block holding the property takes its owner as its context, like any def acting on its owner, and its `@Stochastic::Probability { p = … }` reads the property through it (`context.pr`, `context.mission.pr`, and the `1.0 - …` complement alike) when the object it acts on holds the property, by inheritance or in its one part that is exactly one object; when no object in reach holds it, or the part that does may be several objects or none, or is a private feature of a general the object does not inherit, the branch is weighted by the property's finite default with an `Approximated` ledger note, as `-strict` already wrote it. Previously the name was written bare and `-validate` refused it as not an accessible feature.
- **`Stochastic::Probability::p` is checked for accessibility where its succession is.** The value is read when the decision is reached, so its feature reads are judged from the succession's action, as its guards are, in both spellings (`@Probability { p = … }` and `metadata Probability { p = … }`) — a named succession's read of the action's own feature no longer errors, and an anonymous succession's read of an unreachable feature is no longer let through.

- **A `@Tag;` metadata usage is a queryable element.** A metadata usage written in the prefix spelling, `@Tag;` or `@Tag { ... }`, was declared as no member of its namespace unless it was named, so `sysml -query` and the gRPC/Python `model.query()` omitted it (`rdf:type="MetadataUsage"` found `metadata Tag;` but not `@Tag;`) while the API JSON export listed it. It is now an anonymous member exactly as the `metadata` spelling is, identified by its position (`Q::S::@0`), and the `owner` a query reports for a member of such a body is the annotation usage (`Q::V::@0`), not the metaclass its body resolves against.

- **A redefinition in a view with exposes is no longer reported unresolved.** Asking what a view inherits, while one expose's target resolved and its siblings were suspended, resolved the view's redefinitions with part of their scope missing, and the miss was reported (#649). That question is now asked quietly; the redefinition itself is still resolved, and reported if it names nothing, by the document walk.

- `sysml -satisfy` without a name finds the satisfaction assertions of a file loaded from the record cache, rather than reporting none on runs after the first.

- Anchor sequence insertion after declared member names rather than references written by initial nodes.

- Refuse anchored sequence edits after members that cannot be succession sources.

- **`holds` and `satisfiable` follow the evaluator's short-circuit order over a declared value that does not evaluate.** A feature whose default fails to evaluate (`attribute bad : Real = 1.0 / 0.0;`) no longer leaves every question naming it undecided: the translation tracks the left-to-right evaluation path of `and`, `&`, `or`, `|`, `implies`, conditionals and ordered conditions, so `true or bad > 0.0` holds and is satisfiable as `evaluate` says, while `bad > 0.0 or true` and `true xor bad > 0.0` stay undecided because the evaluator reads `bad` first. A feature reached only under some assignments (`free > 0.0 or bad > 0.0`) is guarded rather than left free: a witness or counterexample avoids it, and a proof is claimed only when no assignment reaches it — otherwise the verdict is undecided, naming the feature read `under some assignment of its free features`. A feature a chain reaches (`inner.dependent`) whose declared value does not evaluate is guarded or refused the same way, rather than left for the solver to choose a value for.

- **SysML v1 migration preserves context-parameter order, qualifies accept signal types shadowed by payloads, specializes viewpoint context usages, and qualifies DiagramLayout attributes shadowed by view exposures.** In strict mode, it also omits layouts for refused pseudostates and writes probability metadata only from finite literal defaults.

- **`then done;` exports as one `SuccessionAsUsage` targeting the library's `Actions::Action::done`.** The member-attached `then` no longer mints a `done` Membership whose element the succession's target end reference-subsets — a shape `ReferenceSubsetting` cannot target. The target end now reference-subsets the library's done feature directly, the same end `succession first x then done;` states, and `sysml:targetFeature` carries it. A `done;` written on its own still exports as the `done` Membership, and graphs written in the earlier shape still read back as `then done;`.

- **`then fork F;` declares the fork node F in a body that also declares a member named `fork`.** The lookahead read a control-node keyword followed by a name as a two-ended succession to the member sharing the keyword's name and rejected it; the grammar gives the keyword a declaration of its own (SysML.xtext `ForkNode`, `JoinNode`, `MergeNode`, `DecisionNode`), so the named, bodied and `then [m] fork F;` forms now declare the node, as they do in a body with no such member. Only the bare `then fork;` beside such a member still references it.

- **`then [m] x;` in an action body is accepted, with the multiplicity on the target end.** The SysML v2 grammar lets the target a `then` references carry a crossing multiplicity (`then [0..1] b;`, `then [1] b { … }`, `then [m] done;`, and beside a source multiplicity as `[1] then [0..1] b;`), the same end `succession first a then [0..1] b;` states; the pilot implementation accepts these and OpenSysML rejected them with "a multiplicity in an action body must precede `then`". The parser now records the target multiplicity, the semantic model carries it on the target end, RDF export writes it on the target connector end and reads every form back without source text, and the end-feature-multiplicity warning stays limited to source ends. A multiplicity that opens a body member with no succession end to belong to is still rejected, with a diagnostic naming both valid placements.

- **Toolkit JSON roots keep the order and identities of the root namespace.** A later same-named root is identified by its position in that order, and an unowned root relationship occupies a position just like any other root.

- **Transition accept triggers are checked against resolved usages.** A bare name that resolves to a usage, including an inherited `start`, is rejected as the pilot does.

- **An unresolved library name now names the import that makes the bare name visible.** When exactly one importable candidate matches, an unqualified name's message ends `To use the bare name, import its package: private import <pkg>::*;`, and a qualified name with an unresolvable first segment reports `"<first>" is not visible here; did you mean <candidate>?` with the same sentence plus fixes to rewrite the segment or add the import.
- **A metadata body's `kind = …` exports as a MetadataBodyUsage.** The member carries no `sysml:declaredName`, owns a Redefinition of the metadata definition's feature via `sysml:redefines`, and is flagged `sysx:isRedefinitionImplicit` (SysML.xtext MetadataBodyUsage); the graph reads back as the bare form.
- **An annotation body is resolved even when its metadata type is not.** `@VerificationMethod { kind = VerificationMethodKind::test; }` without an import now also reports the unresolved value, as the `metadata` form and the pilot validator already did.

- **`verify` exports as a verification, not a satisfy.** In the Turtle and API JSON forms, an objective's `verify r;` or `verify requirement v : R;` was a `SatisfyRequirementUsage`, so a reader could not tell a verification from a satisfaction, and the declaration form came back from the graph as `satisfy requirement v : R;`. Both are now the `RequirementUsage` a `RequirementVerificationMembership` owns, as the grammar's RequirementVerificationMember says, and read back as `verify` (#651).

- **Holds and satisfiable questions now report undecided when a read feature's declared value cannot be evaluated, including when it depends on a feature with no value.** A feature with no value remains free for the solver.
- **Satisfaction questions now read chained values through the subject supplied by `by`.**
- **Validation summary verdicts now carry the `evaluate` question and the object's status.**

### Security

- **Releases carry signed SLSA provenance.** `build-release` writes an in-toto statement whose subjects are every artifact `SHA256SUMS.txt` lists and whose SLSA Provenance v1 predicate records the repository, tag, commit and CircleCI job that built them (`scripts/release-provenance.py`), attests it with cosign keyless under the same CircleCI identity that signs the manifest, verifies the attestation against the built artifacts before anything is stored, and publishes `provenance.intoto.json` and `provenance.intoto.json.bundle` beside the manifest. A download is checked with `cosign verify-blob-attestation <asset> --bundle provenance.intoto.json.bundle --type slsaprovenance1` and the pipeline's identity; see "The release provenance" in `docs/project/releasing.md` for what the statement does and does not claim. The clients keep verifying the signed manifest and are unchanged.

### Performance

- **Occurrences of one shape share their derived defaults and their verdicts.** A `=` default
  that one pristine occurrence of a type derives from nothing but declared values under itself
  is now recorded against the occurrence's shape — its type, classifiers and holding feature — in
  a side table of the runtime context, and every other pristine occurrence of that shape reads
  the recorded value instead of deriving it again and materializing the component tree the
  derivation walked; an occurrence that states, writes, binds or classifies anything the
  derivation read derives on its own, and a write under an occurrence invalidates what it took.
  Within one `-satisfy` or `-validate=<object>` report, a check over occurrences of one shape is
  evaluated once per distinct set of inputs and its verdict fanned out to each occurrence, which
  still reports its own verdict, message and path in the same order. Values, verdicts and
  diagnostics are unchanged, as `TestSparseValuesDifferential` asserts with sharing on and off
  (`OPENSYSML_SHARED_DEFAULTS=0` turns it off; a context recording a trace shares nothing, so
  the trace lists every evaluation). On the 12 800-satellite fleet constellation,
  checking its 2 412 satisfaction assertions drops from 8.84 s and 23.4 GiB allocated to 4.59 s
  and 7.2 GiB, and reading one summed attribute over every occurrence from 42.7 s and 141.3 GiB
  to 3.85 s and 2.7 GiB.

- **A batch of files is parsed and analysed on a pool of workers.** `sysml -validate`, `-satisfy`, `-check` and `%load` open the files they are given as one batch: the files are parsed on one worker per CPU, indexed once, wildcard imports are expanded once for the batch instead of once per file, and the documents are analysed in parallel, each with a resolver and semantic model of its own over an index nothing writes meanwhile; the facts the workspace-wide audits (OOSEM, MOSA, identity metadata) need of every document are gathered once for the batch and shared by the workers. The diagnostics are the same at any job count, in command-line order; `-jobs N` or `OPENSYSML_JOBS`, the setting that already bounds how many runs of one check go concurrently, sets the pool. On an eight-CPU machine the 1 600-satellite stress constellation split over 34 files validates in 9.2 s against 22.0 s on one job and 20.3 s as a single file, within a tenth of the single file's peak memory; a 200-satellite split in 1.19 s against 2.63 s. `stress-model -split-planes <dir>` writes the constellation one file per orbital plane, and `docs/project/satellite-network-stress-test.md` records where the split model's remaining time goes.

- **A closed document can be held as its interface record.** `Workspace.InterfaceRecord` writes, from a document's completed analysis, what other documents can observe of it through the language — every element its qualified names and imports reach, with kind, name, aliases, supertypes, features with type, direction, multiplicity, redefinitions and subsetting, abstract/variation, unit, dimension and metadata facts, the imports and filters of each namespace, and the relationships the OOSEM and MOSA audits gather — beside its diagnostics, and `Workspace.OpenRecorded` installs it as tree-less scopes and symbols with the facts attached. A recorded document is never re-analysed and reports its stored diagnostics verbatim; a question only its syntax tree answers — editing, renaming, listing the references it writes, printing its notation, reading the value a feature declares, building a runtime — is refused with a typed `symbols.ErrNeedsHydration` rather than answered from the record. The record is the library cache's `IndexRecord` generalised and lives in the same cache (`libs.Cache`), keyed by document name, content hash, library digest, conformance mode, build and format version; it is installed with the content it was written from, which the document keeps so its stored diagnostics locate in it, and it records the conformance mode those diagnostics answer — a workspace holding a record keeps that mode, and `SetConformanceMode` names the document that must be hydrated to change it. `TestInterfaceRecordDifferential` holds every fixture and OMG corpus document recorded in turn and requires every other document's diagnostics and resolutions to be unchanged. No reader is switched to records yet; `docs/internals/interface-records.md` states what a record carries and why, and `docs/internals/performance.md` what it holds against a loaded plane of the satellite stress model.

- **Writing the API's JSON element form takes about half the time.** The writer copied the whole triple graph twice before writing, once to add the root namespace and once to reconcile collections that were already in order, and had the JSON encoder re-parse and re-indent every element. It now reconciles in place when nothing needs reordering, assembles the wrapped graph without rebuilding its duplicate check, and writes compact JSON indented once. The output is byte-identical. On the 32-satellite stress network (`BenchmarkConvertAPIJSON`), a conversion drops from 19.1 s to 9.7 s and allocates 5.6 GB instead of 8.6 GB.

- **`ParseSources` analyzes a model of many documents in linear time.** Each document was analyzed in a context of its own, which gathered the workspace-wide audits over every document again, so a model of 320 documents took 15 s through the service where `sysml -validate` checks it in under a second. The documents are now analyzed as one batch, as a workspace analyzes them: 320 documents take 0.6 s, with the same diagnostics.

- **Satisfying requirements allocates less than 0.9.0 did and is within 11% of its time.** Sharing derived defaults and verdicts across occurrences records what each check reads; the record copied every path of a nested shared value per read, deduplicated reads through a key string built per read, spelled a dotted path at every ancestor to ask whether a binding governs a read, and every transaction cloned the simulation clock's list of waiting executors. It now records a nested value once, compares paths in place, asks about bindings only where a type declares one starting at that feature, and the clock's list is replaced rather than edited so a transaction keeps the one it saw. On the stress constellation (`BenchmarkSatisfy`), 96 assertions run in 4.2 ms and 1.66 MiB instead of 4.8 ms and 3.1 MiB, and 1 536 assertions allocate 27 MiB instead of 125 MiB.
- **The migration writer is twice as fast.** It allocated a fresh buffer for every block and re-spelled the indent for every line; it now reuses the buffers it closes and indents from a table. `BenchmarkWriterSiblingBlocks` runs in 1.8 ms and 20 k allocations instead of 4.2 ms and 80 k, with the same output.
- **Serializing a verified instance graph over gRPC allocates 6% less.** The implicit nested-usage subsetting recomputed each collection's declared subsettings per read and made its deduplication and reachability sets before knowing they were needed; those are now memoized on the runtime model and made on first use.
- **Analyzing a resolved document is 13% faster.** The undeclared-signal lint walked the document by reflection once to gather the signals it sends and again to check its triggers; the walk is now made once per analysis and read by both.

- **Loop steps, interpreted calcs and model loads no longer pay per read for the 0.9.1 rules.** The effective parameter range, a type's nested-chain and chain-host checks, and a port type's feature list are memoized on the semantic and runtime models, a type with no redefinition group skips the alias map on every write, and a calc's `this` hook is bound once per occurrence instead of per statement. An assignment step in an action loop and `Instantiate` are back to parity with 0.9.0 (from +35% and +14–23%), a derived read-write-read is +28% (from +124%), the interpreted `SumTo` allocates what it did (from 2.2× the bytes), and loading a model costs 4% more per element (from 27%). The record is `docs/project/archive/performance-release-0.9.1-vs-0.9.0.md`.

## 0.9.0 — 2026-09-24

### Added

- **The analysis libraries' conformance is measured, per package, by a runtime test.** `TestAnalysisLibraryCensus` enumerates every public callable declaration of `SampledFunctions`, `TradeStudies`, `StateSpaceRepresentation`, `AnalysisTooling`, `VectorFunctions` and `OccurrenceFunctions`, invokes each through the runtime with a representative model, and records whether the value it produced passed its check, which typed error refused it, or what its value got wrong. The verdicts are committed to `docs/project/analysis-library-census.json`, which the test holds current, and `make docs-counts` renders them as the per-library table in `docs/project/spec-compliance.md`; `go run ./cmd/doc-counts -check` fails when the table and the file disagree. `StateSpaceRepresentation` is recorded as refused by name where its abstract dynamics need a state-space runner, not worked around.

- **Added `examples/analysis-results-demo/`, a worked example of saving analysis runs into the model and reporting them in a document.** The records are `part` usages on the bundled `AnalysisRecords` vocabulary — the same shape `-record-run`/`%record` emits, written by hand to keep pre-edit values — and a generated report groups, filters and lists them, flags the record a later model edit made stale through a derived `drift`/`stale` pair, and contrasts them with `Verdicts` recomputed live at render time.

- **`-convert` and `-from` accept `api-json`, the OMG SysML v2 API element form.** A model written as `api-json` is the JSON array of element objects the SysML v2 API serves — `"@type"`, `"@id"` and the metamodel properties as keys — over the same RDF graph the Turtle mapping builds, so `sysml`, `ttl` and `api-json` convert between each other losslessly; `.json` names the format by extension, and reading or writing it reports the RDF mapping's experimental status as Turtle does.

- **Expressions are written in the KerML abstract-syntax shape.** An `InvocationExpression`, `ConstructorExpression`, `OperatorExpression`, `FeatureChainExpression`, `IndexExpression`, `CollectExpression` or `SelectExpression` now owns one `ParameterMembership` → `Feature` → `FeatureValue` per operand or argument, a `ReturnParameterMembership` → `Feature` for its result, and — for an invocation — a `Membership` whose member is the function it calls (the pilot's serialization of `instantiatedType`); a chained callee is the `FeatureChainExpression` it names, and a body argument is the anonymous `Expression` owned through a `FeatureMembership`, as in the pilot XMI. The collapsed `function`, `operator`, `argument` and `sysx:sourceText` properties stay beside them; the reader accepts either alone and refuses a graph where the two disagree. Graphs earlier releases wrote — a `->` receiver named by `operand` alone, `new` as `sysx:isConstructor` on an `InvocationExpression`, a `return` parameter under a plain `FeatureMembership` — still read.
- **`-convert api-json` writes the pilot's root `Namespace`.** The element form opens with an unnamed `Namespace` (`<root>_ns`, a UUIDv5 under `-id uuid`) whose `OwningMembership`s own the document's top-level packages, as every `.kermlx` of the pilot does; the reader treats the wrapper as transparent, so notation → `api-json` → notation is byte-identical, and the Turtle form is unchanged.
- **Views are written with the SysML v2 view metaclasses.** `ViewDefinition`, `ViewUsage`, `ViewpointDefinition`, `ViewpointUsage`, `RenderingDefinition`, `RenderingUsage`, `Expose`/`NamespaceExpose`/`MembershipExpose`, `ViewRenderingMembership`, `ElementFilterMembership` and `FramedConcernMembership` replace the collapsed view properties, and read back to the `view … { expose …; filter …; render …; }` they came from.
- **Behavior nodes drop this project's extension metaclasses where the metamodel has a form.** `first start`/`then done` are memberships and successions to `Actions::Action::start`/`done`, `if` branches are `ParameterMembership`-owned parameters of the `IfActionUsage`, a state's `entry`/`do`/`exit` is a `StateSubactionMembership` with the normative `kind`, a transition's effect a `TransitionFeatureMembership` of kind `effect`, an alias a `Membership` with `memberName`, and a named multiplicity a `MultiplicityRange`. Graphs written with the older `sysx:InitialNode`, `sysx:FinalNode` and `sysx:IfBranch` still read. `sysx:Pseudostate`, `sysx:DeferMember` and `sysx:ActionExecutionNode` remain, since the metamodel has no element for the notation they carry; `docs/reference/rdf-mapping.md` says why.
- **`-convert sysml -from api-json` reads more of sysml-toolkit's interchange.** A `then` succession whose source is the member before it, a metadata usage that references `ModelingMetadata::Refinement`, the unnamed chain `Feature` (`FeatureChaining`s) an expression reaches or invokes, a membership an expression node owns, the toolkit's `StateSubactionMembership`s and `TransitionFeatureMembership`s, and a full-JSON implied relationship whose derived `relatedFeature`/`chainingFeature` list is ordered differently from its owned ends all decode — the owned structure is authoritative and the derived list is read from it, since a derived property cannot contradict what it is derived from.

- **`-convert api-json` and `-convert ttl` materialize the relationship elements the notation implies.** Typings, specializations, subsettings, redefinitions, multiplicity ranges, conjugated-port definitions, subject/constraint/result/filter memberships and referent memberships are now written as the first-class elements the SysML v2 metamodel defines — `FeatureTyping` (`<S>_ft0`), `Subclassification`, `Subsetting`, `ReferenceSubsetting`, `Redefinition`, `MultiplicityRange` (`<S>_mult`), `ConjugatedPortDefinition`/`PortConjugation` (`<S>_conjugated`, `<S>_pc`), `SubjectMembership`, `RequirementConstraintMembership`, `ResultExpressionMembership`, `ElementFilterMembership` and the `Membership` an expression's `referent`/`targetFeature` is carried by — beside the collapsed properties that already stated them, so element-count comparisons against sysml-toolkit's interchange JSON match and the toolkit's lifter reads the output back to notation. A declared relationship member is owned through an `OwningMembership` like any other member.
- **`-convert sysml` reads sysml-toolkit interchange JSON.** Both `convert --to compact-json` and `convert --to full-json` decode through `ReadAPIJSON`, to byte-identical notation: the toolkit's root `Namespace`+`OwningMembership` wrapper is transparent, `{"@ref": <name>}` and `unresolved:`-derived targets read as the name they spell, stated defaults collapse back off `isImpliedIncluded` elements, and ends the notation cannot place are refused rather than guessed.
- **`-convert` takes `-id uuid`.** Passing `-id uuid` to `-convert ttl` or `-convert api-json` mints name-based uuids the way the SysML v2 library convention does — `uuid5(NamespaceURL, elementIRI(root))` for a root package, `uuid5(pkg, <the qualified-form id>)` for every derived subject under it — while declared and normative ids are never re-derived. Decoding a uuid-form document reproduces the same notation with the ids implied, and only a non-matching `elementId` stays a declared `@ElementId` annotation.

- **A design note on surface parity** (`docs/internals/design/api-surface-parity.md`). It
  inventories what the REPL, the CLI, the editor, the public Go package and the wire each expose,
  sorts every difference as shared already, missing and worth adding, interactive, protocol-bound
  or local-only, and stages the work: one assembly per operation that all four surfaces call; the
  stateless operations the wire lacks (satisfiability, model-checker options, inline replay, view
  rendering, library search, codegen and the XMI migration report through `Convert`); a session
